# CAS Competitive Study — what we have vs what we don't

Prepared 2026-08-13, on Niharika's ask from that day's call: *find market products
similar to our use case, what functionalities they have, what clients they serve,
and do a comparison study of what we have and what we don't.*

**Method & honesty note.** 5 parallel researchers (enterprise vendors, AI-native/
mid-market, grocery-specific, fashion-specific, cloud platforms + market context),
each output then independently fact-checked by a second pass that was instructed to
**delete any vendor, client or price it could not source**. Claims are marked where
confidence is lower. Every load-bearing statement carries a URL — per the standing
ask that we never quote a stat without a link.

> **The uncomfortable headline:** our *architecture* is right, but our *headline
> claim* is not differentiated. Every element of the core pipeline — LLM query
> understanding, embedding retrieval, hybrid keyword+vector routing, LLM rerank —
> is already shipped by vendors our P0 targets run today. **Both P0 accounts have
> an incumbent**: Empathy.co names Kroger; Syte names C&A.

**One correction to the research, in our favour.** The gap list below was compiled
against the codebase as of the morning of 2026-08-13 and says CAS is "single-shot
and read-only" (gap #13). The **Shopping Assistant shipped the same day** (`POST
/chat`, `pages/shopping_assistant.py`) closes the *conversational* half of that gap:
multi-turn dialogue with history, verified resolving "cheaper ones, for men" against
the previous turn. The *agentic* half — cart and order actions, which Instacart and
Prefixbox execute — remains genuinely missing. Read gap #13 as **partial**, matching
the feature matrix row, not as fully open.

---

## 1. Executive summary

CAS is architecturally correct and commercially undifferentiated on its headline claim. Every element of the core pipeline — LLM query understanding, embedding retrieval, hybrid keyword+vector routing, LLM reranking — is shipped by vendors our target buyers already run: I verified Algolia's NeuralSearch tiering directly (https://www.algolia.com/pricing), and Empathy.co's AI Mode is a near-exact copy of our keyword-first router, appearing as an optional button on two-or-more-keyword queries and "does not replace the conventional keyword search experience; it extends it," with every conversation resolving back into search queries "fully compatible with ranking rules, business logic, and merchandising strategies" (https://docs.empathy.co/blog/welcome-ai-mode.html). Both P0 accounts already have an incumbent: Empathy.co names Kroger on its own customers page, and Syte names C&A on its fashion page (both fetched and confirmed this session). What CAS genuinely owns is narrower and less glamorous than the current pitch — typed hard-constraint enforcement with a score ceiling, a per-result generated reason string, per-ingredient brand alternatives (Northfork's site describes single-best-item matching and never mentions brand alternatives), day-one cold-start ranking, and a published reproducible eval harness that no vendor in this market hands over. What would embarrass us in a client meeting is the operating layer: no merchandiser console, no A/B testing, no analytics, no store-level inventory, no substitution engine, no visual search, and ~7s warm latency — and Prefixbox includes A/B testing plus offline relevance measurement at every paid tier from €1,250/month (verified on their pricing page). The sharpest defensible position is not "better search" but "provably better search on your catalog, auditable by your merchandisers" — sold as a paid diagnostic modelled on Constructor's free Proof Schedule (two lines of code, 2-4 weeks, delivers a business case), which is the single most copyable go-to-market idea in this landscape. Engineering time should go to substitution, a margin/private-label ranking term, offline query pre-warming and an experimentation hook — not to further retrieval tuning, which is the one axis where we are already at parity and the market is at zero marginal cost.

---

## 2. Recommended USP

"We make the search you already own provably better on your catalog — and auditable by your merchandisers." Three pillars, in this order. (1) PROVABLY: we hand over a reproducible evaluation harness the client owns and can re-run — P@10/MRR/NDCG plus domain evals (recipe-ingredient completeness for grocery, cold-start ranking of newly added SKUs for fashion) plus injection and robustness tests. Across 18 vendor pages fetched, every one publishes outcomes and not one publishes a methodology; a product vendor's incentive is opacity, a consultancy's is auditability, so this cannot be copied. (2) AUDITABLE: typed hard-constraint extraction that caps the score ceiling rather than soft-boosting, plus a generated one-sentence reason on every ranked result. This is what merchandising, compliance and legal actually need — "why did this rank, and can I guarantee a no-nuts query never returns nuts" — and it is the one technical claim no vendor page markets. (3) ALREADY OWN: the keyword-first BM25 router means nothing gets ripped out, no new per-query meter, no shopper data leaving the retailer's infrastructure, and the AI path runs only on queries that currently return nothing — which is also the honest framing for our ~7s latency. CRITICALLY, DO NOT claim the "sits alongside your stack" position itself as differentiated: Empathy.co (self-hosted, EU-governed, already at Kroger), Lily AI ("no replatforming", verified verbatim), Google AI Commerce Search and Elastic all occupy it. Sell the layer on LatentView's integration and measurement competence, not on the false claim that nobody else does it. PACKAGE IT AS A PAID DIAGNOSTIC modelled on Constructor's Proof Schedule, which I verified is free, runs 2-4 weeks, needs two lines of code plus a catalog upload, and delivers a business case with projected lifts (https://constructor.com/our-proof-schedule). Our version: give us your catalog and 30-50 real queries from your logs, and in two to three weeks we show CAS versus your incumbent, on your data, with a methodology you keep — and translate every result into revenue-per-visitor, add-to-cart and search conversion, never NDCG. That converts our strongest engineering asset into a sales asset and is the single most copyable idea in this entire landscape.

## 4. What CAS has that they don't (real differentiators)

**1.** TYPED HARD-CONSTRAINT EXTRACTION WITH SCORE-CEILING CAPPING. Defensible because it is an architectural property, not a module: constraints are extracted into a typed schema, each one caps the achievable rerank score rather than soft-boosting, and each is individually inspectable and overridable. No vendor page I fetched across 18 sites markets constraint enforcement as a guarantee — every competitor's natural-language understanding is an opaque relevance blend. A competitor cannot close this with a release note because it requires changing how their ranker composes signals. Demo it with 'meat dinner but no chicken' and 'leather wallet for women under $20', and pair it with the auditability argument for compliance and merchandising stakeholders.

**2.** A GENERATED, HUMAN-READABLE ONE-SENTENCE REASON ON EVERY RANKED RESULT, SHOPPER-FACING. Defensible for roughly 12-18 months, not permanently. Constructor's Merchant Intelligence Agent (Beta) promises to 'Research, understand and fix what's behind every result and ranking' (verified at https://constructor.com/), but it is MERCHANT-facing. Azure AI Search returns an extractive caption and a 0-4 reranker score, not a generated justification. Note the correction: the widely-circulated claim that Fast Simon ships a 'Ranking Explainer' on every tier from $39.99 does NOT survive verification — I checked both https://apps.shopify.com/instant-search and https://www.fastsimon.com/pricing/ and found no such feature. Frame this as 'true to source' explainability rather than the abstract word 'explainability'.

**3.** RECIPE DECOMPOSED INTO PER-INGREDIENT CARDS WITH UP TO THREE RANKED BRAND ALTERNATIVES IN A CAROUSEL. Defensible on UX and on MONETISATION, not on the recipe capability itself. Verified at https://northfork.ai/: their AI Matching Engine translates recipes into in-stock retailer items across 20k+ retailers and 25+ markets with no mention anywhere of presenting brand alternatives to the shopper. Instacart's Cart Assistant auto-builds the cart; Samsung Food auto-picks one item per ingredient. The commercial argument is the strong one: a brand-alternatives carousel is a merchandisable and ad-monetisable surface, whereas a silently auto-filled cart is not — which matters enormously to a grocer's retail-media P&L owner. WEAKNESS TO STATE OPENLY: this rests on a negative finding (nobody says they do it), the weakest evidence class. Never call it novel.

**4.** A PUBLISHED, REPRODUCIBLE EVALUATION HARNESS THE CLIENT OWNS AND CAN RE-RUN. Defensible because the incentives are structural: a product vendor's interest is opacity, a consultancy's is auditability. Across 18 vendor pages fetched this session, every single one publishes outcomes (Michael Stars +951% RPV; H-E-B México 34% faster search and 270M+ annual queries; Target Australia +A$13M) and not one publishes a methodology, a sample size, or a way to reproduce it. Our harness spans P@10/MRR/NDCG@10 with honest caveats on why R@10 is intentionally tiny, a label-leakage probe, recipe-ingredient completeness, and injection/robustness stress tests. In a market where buyers have been burned by unfalsifiable '7.1X' claims — and where Syte's own pages attribute the same multiples to different clients on different pages — methodological transparency is the differentiator, not a technical footnote.

**5.** DAY-ONE COLD-START RANKING OF NEW SKUs FROM CONTENT ALONE. Defensible because it is architecture, not a feature: content-based embeddings plus LLM rerank need zero interaction history, whereas the behavioural rankers our competitors lead with (Bloomreach's self-driving search, Coveo's Automatic Relevance Tuning, Constructor's clickstream engine, AWS Personalize) structurally cannot rank a SKU added this morning. Fast fashion turns over weekly. Marqo is the only vendor marketing this argument, and they are not in the enterprise fashion race. Cheapest possible proof: hold out newly added SKUs in the existing harness and measure — roughly a day of work to turn an architectural property into a sourced claim.

**6.** AN AUDITABLE, INSPECTABLE SPONSORED-AD GOVERNANCE POLICY — relevance gate, always labelled, bid never reaching the ranking client. Defensible ONLY as governance, never as innovation: Kroger's 84.51° shipped a relevance-and-inventory-aware ad quality score in 2018. What is genuinely uncommon is that the gate is a documented, inspectable threshold rather than an opaque auction blend, at a moment when Albertsons and Criteo are actively injecting sponsored product into AI search carousels. Sell it to legal, compliance and the brand team; expect resistance from the retail-media P&L owner.

**7.** CROSS-VERTICAL COVERAGE — ONE PIPELINE THAT SERVES FASHION AND GROCERY. Defensible as a GO-TO-MARKET asset rather than a technical one, and it is the cleanest structural advantage we have over the sharpest competitors. Daydream is fashion-exclusive and cannot follow us into grocery. Syte's visual lexicon has no dietary dimension. Instacart, Cooklist and Wynshop/Halla are grocery-only. Prefixbox has no named apparel logo; Stylitics has no grocery analogue. A consultancy that can run the same constraint-extraction and eval methodology across both verticals sells one capability twice, which no product vendor in this landscape can do.

## 5. What they have that CAS doesn't (ranked by pitch damage)

**1.** MERCHANDISER CONSOLE (boost / bury / pin / rules / synonyms, no engineering required) — RANKED #1 BECAUSE IT IS A DEAL BLOCKER, NOT A FEATURE GAP. Verified at https://www.coveo.com/en/solutions/ecommerce-search-platform/merchandising: 'Quickly configure boost, bury, and pin rules for any search query with ease', 'No IT support required', plus a Merchandising Copilot that delegates 'tedious analysis and repetitive tasks to AI'. Every serious vendor ships one (Algolia Visual Merchandising Studio, Empathy Playboard, Lucidworks Commerce Studio, Syte Tag Editor + Lexicon Manager, Athos, Adobe, Google). In fashion, merchandisers OWN ranking — seasonal pushes, brand guidelines and markdown priorities are non-negotiable business inputs. A system a merchandiser cannot override does not get bought, regardless of NDCG. This is the first question a retail buyer asks and we have no answer at all.

**2.** ONLINE A/B TESTING AND EXPERIMENTATION — RANKED #2 BECAUSE IT IS HOW EVERY COMPETITOR CONVERTS A DEMO INTO A BUSINESS CASE, AND WE CANNOT. Verified at https://www.prefixbox.com/en-us/site-search-pricing-for-ecommerce that A/B testing AND offline search relevance measurement are included at BOTH paid tiers from €1,250/month — not a premium upsell. Verified at https://www.lily.ai/ that every headline number is A/B attributed: +28.3% onsite search revenue (statistically significant A/B test, contemporary luxury brand), +28% Google Shopping revenue (matched-spend A/B), +21.4% Meta ROAS (matched-spend with holdout). Our offline harness is more transparent than anything they publish but is a categorically different artifact, and presenting it as equivalent would be caught.

**3.** SESSION AND PURCHASE-HISTORY PERSONALIZATION — NEAR-DISQUALIFYING IN GROCERY SPECIFICALLY. Verified at https://company.instacart.com/enterprise-blog/cart-assistant-from-instacart: the assistant 'learns preferences from past orders and conversations — brand affinities, dietary habits, favorite flavors', handles 'add my usual weekly groceries', and integrates live loyalty data. Algolia's grocery materials put 80% of carts at reorders. Every grocery meeting opens with 'does it know what I bought last week?' We have no user model at all.

**4.** STORE-LEVEL AND REAL-TIME INVENTORY IN RANKING — GROCERY TABLE STAKES. Verified verbatim on Constructor's grocery page: 'Show shoppers what's actually available at their local store or fulfillment center, personalize recommendations based on stock.' Verified on Algolia's H-E-B México case that real-time inventory by store is deployed, alongside the customer stating Algolia indexes 'all the promotions, inventory, products, and all the data.' CAS ranks a static CSV. In a vertical where the shopper is collecting from one specific store today, this is not a roadmap item.

**5.** OUT-OF-STOCK SUBSTITUTION ENGINE — THE #1 CREDIBILITY GAP IN A GROCERY ROOM. Verified on Constructor's grocery page: 'suggesting the right substitutes.' Instacart runs a production two-tower substitution model; Wynshop acquired Halla specifically for search + substitute + recommend. We have nothing, and the failure mode competitors market fixing ('bananas' vs 'banana bread') is exactly the error pure cosine similarity makes — and our eval set contains no such case.

**6.** VISUAL / IMAGE SEARCH — THE #1 FASHION GAP, AND IT IS ALREADY INSIDE A NAMED TARGET ACCOUNT. Verified at https://www.syte.ai/ecommerce-fashion/ that C&A is a named customer alongside Prada, PrettyLittleThing, Decathlon, Farfetch and Tally Weijl, with visual search, shop-the-look, multi-object detection and out-of-stock similar-item recovery. Google Lens has trained a billion-plus shoppers to expect photo input; Instacart even ingests handwritten lists. A fashion buyer will treat camera input as baseline, not advanced.

**7.** PERSISTED CATALOG ATTRIBUTE ENRICHMENT — THE GAP WITH THE LARGEST STRATEGIC COST, BECAUSE IT IS LATENTVIEW'S OWN PITCH SHIPPED BY SOMEONE ELSE. Verified verbatim at https://www.lily.ai/: 'Lily Max optimizes the product intelligence your feed manager, catalog system, and commerce stack already distribute, so there is no replatforming' — with Coach, M&S, J.Crew, Kate Spade, UGG, HOKA, Vuori and Fabletics. Enrichment wins on breadth: a ranker improves one surface, persisted attributes improve site search, paid feeds, SEO, merchandising and forecasting. Our constraint extractor already does this work at query time and discards it.

**8.** ANALYTICS AND ZERO-RESULT / HEAD-TAIL QUERY REPORTING — universal across Algolia, Coveo, Constructor, Prefixbox (2-year history), Athos, Bloomreach, Empathy and Adobe. We capture thumbs up/down into a local JSONL and surface nothing. This is the artefact a merchandising director asks to see in the second meeting.

**9.** SUB-SECOND LATENCY — ~7s warm against ~20ms (Algolia/MILKRUN) and a 300ms tail-query target (Instacart, after merging LoRA weights and moving to H100s). Ranked here rather than higher because it is survivable IF framed: as a fallback-only tier behind the BM25 router it applies to queries that today return nothing. But it will surface live in a demo, not on a spec sheet, so pre-empt it.

**10.** GEO / AGENT-CHANNEL VISIBILITY — an entire product category with no CAS equivalent. Verified at https://athoscommerce.com/: the GEO Assistant 'Enriches catalogs and syndicates to Gemini, ChatGPT, and AI platforms' across 1,400+ marketplaces and 2,700+ brands. Lily AI, commercetools AI Hub and Fast Simon all sell into the same motion. 'How do we appear when the shopper asks ChatGPT instead of visiting our site' is now a standard RFP question.

**11.** MULTILINGUAL / MULTI-MARKET — Prefixbox 90+ languages, Coveo 50+ languages and 50+ markets, plus Doofinder, Luigi's Box, Bloomreach, Attraqt and Stylitics. This blocks a European fashion pitch outright, since H&M, C&A and Zara-tier retailers are multi-market by definition.

**12.** AUTOCOMPLETE / TYPEAHEAD — table stakes and highly visible. H-E-B México runs 270+ million search-as-you-type queries annually (verified on Algolia's case page); in grocery, typeahead is the dominant search interaction and we do not have it.

**13.** MULTI-TURN CONVERSATION WITH MEMORY, AND AGENTIC ACTIONS — Empathy AI Mode, Constructor, Google, Instacart, Athos, Prefixbox and Algolia Agent Studio all ship dialogue. Instacart and Prefixbox go further and execute cart and order actions. CAS is single-shot and read-only.

**14.** RETRIEVAL DIVERSITY — Instacart has published a diversity-based reranking step after mapping generated items back to the catalog; Constructor ships automatic quantity/volume normalization. Our '70 paneer sellers' problem is still an open backlog item and it is the most visible defect in a grocery demo grid.

**15.** ENTERPRISE PLUMBING AND DATA RESIDENCY — 99.99% SLAs, SSO, SOC 2 Type II (Lily AI), EU governance (Empathy.co). Ranked last only because it is a procurement gate rather than a pitch moment, but the EU angle is a live risk: Empathy.co built an enterprise position on 'no big tech, EU-governed' and won Kroger and Carrefour with it, while CAS currently runs US-hosted inference.

## 6. Quick wins — closable with the current architecture

**1.** SUBSTITUTION MODE (highest ROI, ~1-2 weeks). An out-of-stock or unavailable-item substitution is an embedding-neighbour search executed under the existing typed hard constraints — we already have every component (embedding neighbours from the upsell path, constraint plumbing, reranker). The differentiator is free: the reason string ('same brand, 2% larger pack, also gluten free'), because no vendor in this landscape explains their swaps. Closes the #1 grocery credibility gap.

**2.** MARGIN / PRIVATE-LABEL RANKING TERM (~days). app/scoring.py already blends a Bayesian rating signal at weight 0.05 — a margin or own-brand term is the same mechanism and the same code path. Constructor sells KPI-optimized ranking against AOV, LTV and margin as its headline; grocery gross margins are thin and private label is the main lever. Keep it configurable and weighted low by default so it does not violate the standing 'rating as minimal as possible' decision.

**3.** OFFLINE PRE-WARM OF HEAD AND TORSO QUERIES INTO page_cache (~1 week, biggest latency win). Instacart published exactly this pattern: offline frontier-model generation cached for head/torso, real-time small model reserved for cold-start and tail, hitting a 300ms target. We already have app/page_cache.py — extend it into a batch pre-warm keyed on the client's query logs. Turns ~7s warm into cache-hit latency for the majority of real traffic.

**4.** PUBLISH RETRIEVAL-ONLY LATENCY SEPARATELY FROM LLM RERANK LATENCY (~1 day). Never let a bare '7s vs 20ms' comparison onto a slide. Report the BM25 router path, the retrieval-only path and the full reranked path as three numbers with three different jobs.

**5.** DIVERSITY RERANK IN THE PIPELINE (~1 week). Instacart has published the fix for our open '70 paneer sellers' P2 — a diversity-based reranking step applied after candidates are mapped back to the catalog. Move it out of the backlog; it is the most visible defect in a grocery demo grid.

**6.** BATCH / OFFLINE ATTRIBUTE WRITEBACK FROM THE CONSTRAINT EXTRACTOR (~2 weeks). Add a batch mode to app/translator.py that runs the typed extractor over the catalog and persists inferred attributes as a durable artifact rather than discarding them at query time. This puts us in Lily AI's slot — the same work then improves paid feeds, SEO and merchandising, not just site search, which is how enrichment vendors justify 8-figure impact off a 3.3% demand lift.

**7.** COLD-START EVAL (~1 day). Hold out newly added SKUs in the existing harness and measure ranking quality with zero interaction history. Converts our best unclaimed fashion advantage from an architectural assertion into a sourced number, and it is the one advantage no competitor can neutralise with a feature release.

**8.** TRANSLATE ALL EVAL OUTPUT INTO BUSINESS VOCABULARY (~2-3 days). Add revenue-per-visitor, add-to-cart rate and search conversion proxies alongside the IR metrics. Zero vendors publish NDCG; all publish RPV, CVR and incremental revenue. Leading with NDCG 0.950 lands as a research artifact.

**9.** ADD THE FOUR GROCERY FAILURE CASES TO data/recipe_eval.json AND data/stress_queries.json (~1 day). Category collisions ('bananas' vs 'banana bread' — the exact error cosine similarity makes and the failure Halla marketed fixing), pack-size/unit normalization, pantry-staple suppression, and near-duplicate seller diversity. These are precisely what a grocery buyer probes for.

**10.** REHEARSE THE DATA-RESIDENCY ANSWER (~1 day, zero code). app/llm_client.py already reads provider config dynamically via the cfg.X pattern, and gte-small at ~33M params runs on CPU on the client's own hardware with no external call. An EU-hosted or self-hosted swap is a config change. This is a strong answer if rehearsed and a weak one if improvised — and Empathy.co has proven the objection is real and contract-relevant.

**11.** IMAGE-SEARCH SPIKE (~2 weeks, do before any fashion demo). Swap gte-small for a CLIP-class multimodal encoder in app/embeddings.py; search.py, the reranker and the constraint layer all operate on vectors regardless of origin, and products.csv already carries img_url. Even a rough spike converts 'we have no visual search' into 'here is the architecture and a working prototype'.

**12.** UNIT AND PACK-SIZE NORMALIZATION FOR RECIPE QUANTITIES (~1 week). Constructor sells this as a named grocery capability (verified: normalize L, ML, OZ and pack size). Without it, 'three tomatoes' can return a 22-count box — immediately visible and embarrassing in a live demo.

## 7. Products to sign up for / benchmark against

**1.** Algolia — genuine free forever tier, 10K search requests/mo, 50K records, 5K Recommend requests, no credit card. The easiest head-to-head bench in the market: build a comparable index over our catalog in a weekend. VERIFIED THIS SESSION: https://www.algolia.com/pricing

**2.** Daydream (consumer product) — the highest-value fashion benchmark, because it is our exact pitch executed by a fashion-native team with $50M and Julie Bornstein as founder. Run our own eval queries against it before any fashion client meeting. Launch of the B2B 'Powered by Daydream' tier verified: https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/daydream-launches-ai-powered-search-and-discovery-solution-for-fashion-brands-and-retailers-302837597.html (live: STAUD, Alice + Olivia, Couper, Cult Mia, Hampden Clothing; 25+ signed incl. Sandro, Maje, ba&sh). Consumer app: https://www.daydream.ing

**3.** Albertsons / Safeway 'Ask AI' — free, live in the public apps and on the banner sites. The single best grocery benchmark available and it costs nothing: this is Google's Conversational Commerce agent in production at a P0-tier account. Also inspect what appears in the sponsored carousels following the Criteo integration, before writing our ads slide.

**4.** Coveo — 14-day free trial, no credit card, most functionality enabled. Worth it specifically to use the Merchandising Hub and see the console gap first-hand: https://www.coveo.com/en/free-trial (merchandising capabilities verified at https://www.coveo.com/en/solutions/ecommerce-search-platform/merchandising)

**5.** Prefixbox — the closest grocery-shaped rival with published enterprise pricing and free Shopify tier. VERIFIED THIS SESSION: AI Search €1,250/mo, full AI Product Discovery Bundle €2,350/mo (€2,115 discounted), with A/B testing and offline search relevance measurement at BOTH tiers. https://www.prefixbox.com/en-us/site-search-pricing-for-ecommerce and free tier at https://apps.shopify.com/prefixbox-search

**6.** Fast Simon — free tier at 100 sessions/mo plus paid trials; the cleanest map of how the market packages an AI shopping agent by tier. VERIFIED THIS SESSION: Free / $39.99 Starter / $99.99 Essential / $299.99 Top Pro (Top Pro adds merchandising rules, A/B testing, visual merchandising). Note: I could NOT find the 'Ranking Explainer' feature on either the Shopify listing or fastsimon.com/pricing. https://apps.shopify.com/instant-search

**7.** Shopify Search & Discovery — free first-party app, installable on any dev store. Essential for the defensive answer to 'Shopify does semantic search for free': confirm the sub-200,000-product ceiling, plan gating and predictive-search exclusion yourself. https://apps.shopify.com/search-and-discovery

**8.** Empathy.co open models on Hugging Face — their Groceries GLiNER NER model and grocery NER dataset are downloadable and inspectable. This is the ONLY true apples-to-apples benchmark available anywhere for our dietary-constraint extraction, and it belongs to the vendor sitting inside Kroger. Cheap, high-value, and should be done before any grocery pitch. Customers page verified: https://empathy.co/customers/

**9.** Constructor Proof Schedule — do not just read it, request it and experience the motion end-to-end. VERIFIED THIS SESSION: free, 2-4 weeks, install the beacon (two lines of code) plus a catalog upload, and they deliver a ranking analysis, a search-insights dashboard and a business case with projected lifts. This is the exact GTM we should clone. https://constructor.com/our-proof-schedule

**10.** Lily AI — free 30-day trial on 500 products. Worth doing because their positioning IS our positioning ('no replatforming', verified verbatim) and their A/B-attributed lift numbers are the format our evals must be translated into. https://www.lily.ai/

**11.** Elastic Cloud — 14-day free trial plus a fully worked e-commerce semantic search tutorial with notebooks. Important because Kroger and H-E-B are named Elastic customers, so this is likely the incumbent stack our router would sit on top of. https://www.elastic.co/search-labs/blog/semantic-search-elasticsearch-ecommerce

**12.** Marqo — open-source core plus published multimodal e-commerce embedding models and benchmarks at https://huggingface.co/Marqo. The most useful free technical artifact for deciding whether to fine-tune (scripts/finetune_embeddings.py is already in our tree) or go multimodal.

**13.** Vespa Cloud — free trial with $300 credits, no credit card, plus an open-source e-commerce sample app on an Amazon product dataset. The scaling answer to 'does this work past 300K SKUs?' https://vespa.ai/free-trial/

**14.** Samsung Food (samsungfood.com, free consumer app) — run the full recipe → matched product → cart flow against a real retailer. It has been live at Kroger since 2020 and is the direct functional comparison to our shopping-list surface, including the quantity reasoning we lack.

**15.** Syte — no free trial, but request a demo: they are named at C&A, one of our P0 fashion targets, and their AI Tag Editor plus Lexicon Manager are the concrete shape of our merchandising-console gap. Customer list verified: https://www.syte.ai/ecommerce-fashion/

**16.** Athos Commerce — demo request only, but worth it for the GEO Assistant specifically, which is the one product category we have no answer for. Verified at https://athoscommerce.com/: syndicates catalogs to Gemini, ChatGPT and AI platforms, 1,400+ channels, 2,700+ brands.

---

## 8. Risks to walk into a client meeting knowing

**1.** BOTH P0 ACCOUNTS ALREADY HAVE AN INCUMBENT, VERIFIED FIRST-HAND. Empathy.co names Kroger on https://empathy.co/customers/ (alongside Carrefour, and with documented migrations off Hybris Search, Google Search Appliance and Doofinder). Syte names C&A on https://www.syte.ai/ecommerce-fashion/. Neither is greenfield: every pitch is coexistence or displacement of an established vendor, and walking in framing it as greenfield will be corrected in the room.

**2.** GOOGLE AND INSTACART SELL OUR EXACT LAYER AS A PRE-INTEGRATED SKU TO OUR EXACT TARGETS. Google's Conversational Commerce agent is live as 'Ask AI' across Albertsons banners; Instacart's whitelabeled Cart Assistant covers dietary filtering, recipe-to-cart, photo/handwritten-list ingestion, live inventory AND loyalty, reorder and three-tier content moderation (verified at https://company.instacart.com/enterprise-blog/cart-assistant-from-instacart). Many targets already have a Google Cloud or Instacart commercial relationship, so these arrive with near-zero procurement friction. 'We built semantic retail search' is a hard sell to a CIO who can enable Google's in a sprint.

**3.** LATENCY WILL SURFACE IN A LIVE DEMO, NOT A SPEC SHEET. ~7s warm against ~20ms (Algolia) and 300ms tail targets (Instacart). No vendor publishes latency, so nobody will ask until they watch a query hang. Mitigate before the meeting: router-first framing, offline pre-warm, and separate published numbers per path.

**4.** THE RECIPE FEATURE WILL BE CHALLENGED AS UNORIGINAL. Recipe-to-cart is live at Kroger (Samsung Food since 2020, Cooklist agentic cart), Albertsons (six named capabilities including Rapid Restock and Fridge Cleaner), Tesco (280,000-colleague beta built with a consultancy), Ahold Delhaize (three separate vendors), Walmart, and Google. Constructor's grocery page sells dietary-needs missions and substitutions directly. If we claim novelty, the first question destroys the slide. Claim quality and monetisability instead.

**5.** ALLERGEN AND DIETARY CLAIMS ARE A LEGAL EXPOSURE, NOT A RELEVANCE PROBLEM. We extract dietary constraints from the QUERY; correctly enforcing 'nut-free' or 'gluten-free' requires trustworthy per-SKU attributes. Instacart solved it with VLM extraction over ~500K items; retailers license NIQ Label Insight or SPINS. Being wrong about an allergen is a safety incident. Our own safety.md points to scoping claims strictly to licensed data — that must be stated in the deck before a compliance team asks.

**6.** EU DATA RESIDENCY COULD DISQUALIFY THE CURRENT DEPLOYMENT AT A EUROPEAN BUYER. CAS runs on Groq-hosted inference in the US. C&A, H&M and Carrefour may have residency requirements that fail outright regardless of relevance quality. Empathy.co has built an entire enterprise position on 'EU governance, free from any third party or big tech provider' and won Kroger and Carrefour with it — direct evidence the objection is real and buyer-driven.

**7.** LEADING WITH IR METRICS WILL LAND AS A RESEARCH ARTIFACT. Every vendor I checked publishes business outcomes (Michael Stars +951% RPV and +15% AOV; H-E-B México 34% search speed and 270M+ queries; Target Australia +A$13M; Lily AI +28.3% A/B-tested search revenue). Zero publish NDCG. NDCG 0.950 is not bankable to a CFO.

**8.** DELIVERY-TIMELINE BENCHMARKS ARE ALREADY SET AND PUBLISHED. Constructor states '8 Weeks or less to set up' and '98.5% avg client retention rate over the last three years' on its homepage (verified); Cooklist integrates in ~6 weeks. Any LatentView scoping estimate gets measured against those, and a 6-month engagement will read as risk rather than rigour.

**9.** PRICING CEILING. Prefixbox's full AI Product Discovery Bundle — WITH A/B testing and offline relevance measurement included — is €2,350/month (verified). Third-party contract data puts Algolia's median around $41,650/yr and Constructor's average materially higher. Above roughly $200K/yr we compete head-on with Constructor on features and lose. Below that, we must justify the price against a €2,350/month product that already has the operating layer we lack.

**10.** THE RETAIL-MEDIA STAKEHOLDER MAY VETO OUR AD POSTURE. Albertsons Media Collective and Criteo are actively injecting sponsored product into AI search carousels; Kroger has run relevance-gated ads since 2018 and Instacart's Carrot Ads carries demand from 9,000+ CPG brands. Our hard 'never blend into organic' rule is more defensible on auditability and weaker commercially. The P&L owner controls budget.

**11.** MARKET CONSOLIDATION COMPRESSES SEARCH-ONLY VALUE PROPOSITIONS. Klevu + Searchspring into Athos, ViSenze into Rezolve, Attraqt into Crownpeak (then Rezolve), GroupBy into Rezolve, Vantage Discovery into Shopify, Findify into Maropost. Standalone search is a shrinking business; value is migrating to the operating layer and agent-channel visibility. This supports 'CAS is a capability, not a product' — but it also means fewer standalone slots to sell into.

**12.** CLAIMS HYGIENE IS ITSELF A RISK. In verifying 18 pages this session I found a widely-circulated claim (Fast Simon shipping a 'Ranking Explainer' on every tier) that does not appear on either the Shopify listing or the vendor pricing page, and a Coveo margin/return-rate capability that is not on the merchandising page. Syte's own site attributes similar multiples to different clients on different pages. Any unverified stat in a client deck is a credibility liability — the client lead has explicitly demanded links, and several numbers circulating internally do not survive a click.

**13.** THE AMAZON REVIEWS 2023 DATASET IS RESEARCH-LICENSED. Fine for a PoC, but it cannot ship in a commercial client deployment and must be flagged before any client demo — not discovered by their legal team afterwards.

---

## 9. Open questions needing a human decision

**1.** What does Empathy.co ACTUALLY power at Kroger? Their own customers page names Kroger (verified) but publishes no scope, no traffic figures and no case study. Whether they own primary site search or a narrower surface determines whether a Kroger conversation is coexistence, augmentation or displacement — and it changes the entire pitch. Someone should establish this before any Kroger approach.

**2.** Is C&A's Syte deployment visual-only, or does it extend into search relevance and merchandising? Syte's fashion page names C&A but does not break out scope. If visual-only, CAS is complementary and the pitch is easy; if it includes relevance and the Lexicon Manager, we are displacing an incumbent inside our best-known target.

**3.** Does LatentView have an existing analytics or data-engineering relationship at ANY named fashion or grocery target? This is the real moat against Daydream, Constructor and Lily AI — none of whom can walk in on an existing engagement. If the answer is no, the go-to-market needs rethinking before the feature roadmap does.

**4.** THE SINGLE BIGGEST ROADMAP DECISION: do we fund a merchandising console and an experimentation framework, or do we explicitly scope CAS as a component that plugs into the client's EXISTING console and A/B platform? Building both is months of work against vendors who already ship them. Plugging in is faster and fits the 'sell into the existing stack' thesis but requires per-client integration. This needs a decision before any further retrieval tuning.

**5.** GOOGLE AI COMMERCE SEARCH PUBLISHED PRICING — STILL UNRESOLVED. I attempted cloud.google.com/retail/pricing directly this session and the page truncated before the rate table rendered; a prior pass hit the same wall. The circulating figures ($2.50 per 1,000 search/browse requests, $0.27 per 1,000 predictions, $2.50 per node-hour training) are corroborated only by search snippets and a third-party blog. Get a Google rep to confirm in writing before it goes on a build-vs-buy slide.

**6.** ALGOLIA'S AND BLOOMREACH'S 2026 GARTNER MQ STATUS — UNVERIFIED. My WebSearch budget was exhausted this session before I could check, so I am asserting nothing about the 2026 Leaders list. Constructor, Coveo and Netcore Unbxd are corroborated across separate vendor releases in the briefing material; Algolia's and Bloomreach's 2026 placements are not. Pull the actual document before naming anyone.

**7.** WHAT IS THE COMMERCIAL MODEL? Fixed consulting engagement, per-query metering, or GMV-linked? Our per-query LLM cost makes uncapped pricing structurally hard, but the market offers counter-models worth studying: Nosto prices on GMV with no query limits, Miso charges a flat fee with unlimited requests, and Rebuy's Monetize tier makes search a revenue source rather than a cost. Algolia's $0.50 vs $1.75 per 1K request ladder (verified) is the cleanest public evidence of what the market pays for AI relevance over keyword — roughly a 3.5x premium.

**8.** ALLERGEN AND DIETARY ATTRIBUTE SOURCING: do we add a VLM/LLM catalog-extraction stage with measured precision, or scope allergen claims strictly to licensed data (NIQ Label Insight, SPINS)? This is a legal decision, not an engineering one, and it must be settled before a grocery demo touches a dietary query.

**9.** IS THE SPONSORED-AD SEPARATION A HARD ARCHITECTURAL RULE OR A CONFIGURABLE GOVERNANCE POLICY? Needs input from whoever will face the retail-media stakeholder. The market is buying injection into AI answer surfaces; our current posture is the opposite. Configurable-with-audit is likely the sellable middle, but that is a product decision with safety.md implications.

**10.** WHAT ARE THE PER-ACCOUNT DATA-RESIDENCY AND MODEL-HOSTING REQUIREMENTS? Needed before a European fashion pitch. The technical answer exists (dynamic provider config, CPU-runnable embeddings) but the client-specific requirement is unknown.

**11.** IS AGENTIC-CHANNEL / GEO VISIBILITY IN SCOPE FOR THIS ENGAGEMENT? Athos, Lily AI, commercetools and Fast Simon all sell it; we have nothing and it is not on our known-gaps list. It is also the least crowded adjacency in the landscape. Scope expansion or explicit out-of-scope — but it should be a decision, not an omission.

**12.** CAN WE GET A REAL CLIENT CATALOG AND 30-50 REAL QUERY LOGS to run the Proof-Schedule-style diagnostic? The whole recommended USP depends on being able to demonstrate CAS against an incumbent on the client's own data. Without that, we are back to demoing on a research-licensed Amazon dataset, which is both weaker and legally flagged.

**13.** SHOULD THE RECIPE FEATURE BE REPOSITIONED AS THE GROCERY LEAD AT ALL? It is our most demoable asset but the weakest differentiation claim (resting on a negative finding), and it is deployed in some form at every target account. An alternative lead is substitution-with-reasons plus constraint guarantees — less flashy, more defensible, and it fills a gap the buyer actually feels.

---

## 10. Vendor appendix (per segment)

### Enterprise e-commerce search & product-discovery platforms — fact-checked and expanded (Algolia, Constructor, Bloomreach, Coveo, Netcore Unbxd, Lucidworks, Attraqt/Crownpeak-Rezolve, plus three vendors the original report missed: Google AI Commerce Search, Empathy.co, Athos Commerce/Klevu+Searchspring)

#### Algolia (NeuralSearch + Agent Studio) — https://www.algolia.com/

- **What it does:** Hosted search-as-a-service API. Core is a keyword engine; NeuralSearch adds vector/semantic retrieval and merges+ranks both in one index. The differentiator is 'neural hashing' — vectors compressed into hashes claimed to use 1/10th of the RAM, which is how they claim millisecond semantic latency without a separate vector DB. Keyword-vs-semantic balance is tunable via presets or custom weights and can itself be A/B tested. Agent Studio (public beta, announced Jan 2026) is a RAG/agent builder grounded in live Algolia indices with scope guardrails, brand-tone control, business-rule inheritance, token-usage visibility, caching and rate limiting; MCP-aligned with an Algolia MCP Server. NOTE: original report's Agent Studio launch date of 2026-01-30 could not be re-verified this pass (the cited Algolia newsroom URL now 404s) — treat the precise date as unconfirmed, the product itself is real and live on algolia.com/products/ai/agent-studio.
- **Key features:** NeuralSearch: hybrid keyword+vector in one index via neural hashing (1/10th RAM claim); tunable keyword-vs-semantic weighting; presets; Agent Studio (public beta): catalog-grounded RAG agents, guardrails, token/cost controls, MCP-native; agentic search bar, guided selling, fitment/compatibility, product comparison; Real-time personalization (Elevate tier only) + advanced personalization (Grow Plus and up); Visual Merchandising Studio, Rules, Dynamic Re-Ranking, Smart Groups, AI Collections; AI Synonyms (Grow Plus and up), query suggestions / search-as-you-type autocomplete; A/B testing, advanced analytics (CTR, CVR, click position), federated search, 99.99% SLA + SSO on Elevate; Algolia Recommend (separate metered product; 5K Recommend requests/mo included on Free)
- **Named clients:** H-E-B México (grocery, 88+ stores across seven northern Mexican states) — VERIFIED on algolia.com/customers/h-e-b-mexico: '34% increase in search speed', 'more than 270 million search-as-you-type queries annually'; features deployed = Personalization, Dynamic Re-ranking, Semantic Search, Visual Merchandising Studio, Federated Search, AI Synonyms, advanced analytics. Customer states 'the entire ecommerce platform is powered by Algolia'; Co-op (UK grocery) — +39% CVR, +45% add-to-basket, +6% basket size, +13% CVR from Recommend+Search vs Search alone (from original report; case study page not re-fetched this pass — MEDIUM confidence); Auto Mercado (Costa Rica supermarket, 44 stores) — 50%+ conversion-rate increase (not re-verified this pass — MEDIUM confidence); Lacoste (fashion) — +150% sales contribution from search, +210% search use, +37% CVR, -88% bounce rate (not re-verified this pass — MEDIUM confidence); Gymshark (fitness fashion) — Algolia since 2018 for search + merchandising; 150% increase in order rate with new users on Black Friday (not re-verified this pass — MEDIUM confidence); Walgreens, Decathlon Singapore — case studies exist; no metrics verified
- **Pricing:** PUBLISHED — RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-13 at algolia.com/pricing, all figures confirmed exactly. Free: $0/mo, 10K search requests/mo, 50K records, 5K Recommend requests/mo, 5K crawls/mo. Grow: $0.50 per additional 1K search requests + $0.40 per additional 1K records; no contract, monthly billing. Grow Plus: $1.75 per additional 1K search requests + $0.40 per 1K records; adds AI Ranking, AI Synonyms, advanced personalization, Collections — but EXCLUDES NeuralSearch and real-time personalization. Elevate: custom, annual contract, volume discounts; ONLY tier with NeuralSearch, AI Collections, Smart Groups, real-time personalization, 99.99% availability, SSO. Third-party contract data RE-VERIFIED at vendr.com/marketplace/algolia: median annual contract $41,650, range $12,413–$260,594, data from 144 purchases, 23.3% average negotiated saving. CAUTION FOR THE DECK: other aggregators disagree sharply — a search snippet attributes to Vendr an 'average enterprise plan ~$350,000' and SpendHound reports ~$11,407 SMB / ~$72,803 enterprise. Third-party contract benchmarks are anonymous and mutually inconsistent; present the $41,650 median as one data point, not as fact. The ~$50K/yr AWS Marketplace Elevate floor claim remains UNVERIFIED — do not use.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Strong on BOTH, and the best-documented GROCERY reference set of the original seven — H-E-B México is verified with exact published numbers, and Co-op UK / Auto Mercado add supermarket coverage. Maps directly onto the Kroger/Albertsons/HEB-tier target. Fashion equally covered (Lacoste, Gymshark, Decathlon SG). Caveat: their grocery wins are framed as speed + merchandising + re-ranking, not meal/recipe intelligence.
- **Demo/trial:** YES, best self-serve access of the set. Free forever tier at https://www.algolia.com/pricing (10K searches/mo, 50K records, no card). Agent Studio offers 'start building for free' via dashboard plus demo request: https://www.algolia.com/products/ai/agent-studio
- **Vs CAS:** CLOSEST ARCHITECTURAL TWIN. NeuralSearch is the same hybrid keyword+vector idea as CAS's BM25 + gte-small router; Agent Studio is the same LLM-grounded-in-catalog idea as CAS's translator+reranker. Practical consequence: 'we do semantic search' is NOT a differentiator against Algolia. WHAT THEY HAVE THAT CAS DOES NOT: Visual Merchandising Studio, A/B testing, analytics dashboards, AI Synonyms + rules editor, autocomplete, real-time session personalization, federated search, multi-language, SSO/SLA, and agent cost governance (token visibility, caching, rate limiting) — CAS has no LLM cost controls at all. WHAT CAS HAS: per-product human-readable reason strings, typed hard-constraint extraction that caps the score ceiling, ingredient-card recipe surface. PRICING LESSON: NeuralSearch is Elevate-only (custom annual). A retailer wanting semantic search from Algolia must buy the top tier — that is CAS's single best commercial wedge against them, and it is stronger than any feature argument.

#### Constructor — https://constructor.com/

- **What it does:** AI-native product discovery built on clickstream from the ground up ('not merely bolted onto a legacy keyword engine'). Covers search, autosuggest, browse, collections, recommendations, AI Shopping Agent, Product Insights Agents, offsite discovery and attribute enrichment. The distinguishing idea is that ranking models are trained to optimize a chosen BUSINESS KPI (revenue, conversion, AOV, LTV, margin) rather than relevance alone — VERIFIED on the grocery solution page, which explicitly names 'critical grocery KPIs like AOV, LTV, and margin'.
- **Key features:** KPI-optimized ranking — models tuned to revenue / CVR / AOV / LTV / margin, not just relevance; AI Shopping Agent — VERIFIED quote from constructor.com/products/ai-shopping-assistant: shoppers can 'build entire carts from natural-language requests (like \'plan a week of vegetarian dinners\')'. Grounded in catalog + clickstream + live inventory; cannot override merchandiser rules; Attribute Enrichment — VERIFIED on grocery page: 'Automatically normalize product quantity and volume data (e.g., L, ML, OZ, pack size) to power smarter filters, better search relevance, and more accurate substitutions'; Store-level inventory — VERIFIED: 'Show shoppers what\'s actually available at their local store or fulfillment center, personalize recommendations based on stock'; Mission/intent understanding — VERIFIED: 'Understand and anticipate shopper intent (i.e. meal prep, pantry restocking, event planning)'; Trend adaptation — VERIFIED: rankings adapt to 'fast-moving food trends like \'Dubai chocolate\' or \'Super Bowl snacks\''; Autosuggest with auto-generated typo/synonym/reformulation lists; merchandising dashboard (override, boost, pin) with zero-result and trending-search reporting; A/B testing at scale — publishes a 'Split/Test Digest' from 1,000+ ecommerce A/B tests
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED as logos/mentions on constructor.com/products/ai-shopping-assistant: Sephora, Under Armour, Gap, REI, Tractor Supply, QVC, Bath & Body Works, Foot Locker, Petco, Fisheries Supply, Home24, Target Australia, Grove, Bonobos, Monica Vinader, Ashley Furniture, BJ's, Bombas, Crate and Barrel, Cromwell, Journeys, Paperless Post, Plow & Hearth, Rothy's, Serena & Lily, West Elm, White Stuff, Williams Sonoma, Belk; Target Australia — VERIFIED on the grocery solution page: '+A$13M in search revenue'. This is the ONLY named customer with metrics on Constructor's grocery page; Belk (department store) — $35M additional revenue; AI-agent users 'converted at more than twice the rate' (from original report, not re-fetched — MEDIUM confidence); Bonobos (menswear) — replaced Elasticsearch; +9% search revenue, +6% browse conversions, +92% recommendations conversions, +22% recommendations AOV. NOTE: not claimed as a controlled A/B test — post-implementation figures; Petco +13% site conversions; Chubbies +10% add-to-cart; AG Jeans +16% conversion; White Stuff +21% search CVR; Monica Vinader +5% conversion (MEDIUM confidence, not re-fetched); flaschenpost (German grocery/beverage delivery) — +4.14% search CVR (MEDIUM confidence; NOT present on the grocery solution page this pass)
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED — quote-only. RE-VERIFIED at vendr.com/marketplace/constructor-io: average contract value ~$150,000, redline threshold ~$200,000, proposed price point ~$300,001. Vendr does NOT disclose the sample size for Constructor (unlike Algolia's 144 purchases) — so this benchmark is weaker evidence than the Algolia one. Directionally, Constructor sits materially above Algolia/Coveo median ACVs (~$42K) and sets the top of the credible price band for a LatentView engagement. Market context VERIFIED from Constructor's own June 2026 release: the search and product discovery market was 'valued at approximately $17.41 billion in 2025'.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** BEST OVERALL FIT TO OUR TWO TARGET SEGMENTS and therefore the most dangerous competitor. Fashion: Bonobos, Chubbies, AG Jeans, White Stuff, Monica Vinader, Belk, Rothy's, Bombas, Foot Locker, plus Sephora/Gap/Under Armour logos. Grocery: a dedicated grocery solution page with store-level availability, pack-size normalization and meal-mission intent — though note the honest weakness that only Target Australia appears there with a metric, and Target Australia is general merchandise, not a supermarket. Their agent page advertises the exact CAS grocery use case verbatim.
- **Demo/trial:** No free tier, but the strongest pre-sales motion in the market: The Proof Schedule® — https://constructor.com/our-proof-schedule — free/complimentary, non-intrusive, install two lines of code (the Constructor beacon) plus a catalog upload, collect anonymous clickstream, then deliver an opportunity analysis, a business case with projected performance lifts, a custom dashboard and a search-insights report. DURATION DISCREPANCY (both verified): the marketing page says 2-4 weeks with 2-3 weeks of data collection; docs.constructor.com says '3 to 4 weeks' with 'fewer than 30 minutes of effort required on your end'. KILLED — 'never lost an A/B test to a competitor': the original report presented this as a Constructor marketing claim. RE-CHECKED 2026-08-13 against BOTH constructor.com/our-proof-schedule and docs.constructor.com/docs/make-us-prove-it-the-proof-schedule — the claim appears on NEITHER. Do not repeat it in a client deck.
- **Vs CAS:** THE HEAD-TO-HEAD THREAT, and stronger than the original report conveyed. Constructor was named a Leader in the 2026 Gartner MQ 'placing furthest on the Completeness of Vision axis and highest on the Ability to Execute axis among all vendors evaluated' (VERIFIED, PRNewswire, 2026-06-25) — i.e. they are the #1-positioned vendor in the category, not merely one of several Leaders. WHAT THEY HAVE THAT CAS DOES NOT: (1) ranking optimized to a business KPI — CAS ranks on relevance with a 0.05 rating tie-breaker and has no margin/AOV/LTV objective; (2) Attribute Enrichment auto-normalizing pack size and volume — a shipped, productized answer to CAS's still-open '70 paneer sellers' diversity gap; (3) merchandising dashboard with boost/pin/bury and zero-result reporting; (4) 1,000+ A/B tests and a public experimentation practice; (5) session personalization from clickstream; (6) autosuggest with auto-generated synonyms/typos; (7) store-level inventory awareness, table stakes in grocery, which CAS does not model at all; (8) natural-language multi-item cart building — the same surface as CAS's recipe feature. WHAT CAS HAS: per-result explanation strings and explicit typed hard-constraint enforcement. COMMERCIAL LESSON (unchanged and still the single most copyable idea in this report): the Proof Schedule converts an engineering asset into a sales asset. CAS already has a P@10/MRR/NDCG harness — repackage it as 'give us your catalog and 30 real queries, we will show you CAS vs your incumbent in two weeks'. Just do not copy the unverified 'never lost an A/B test' line.

#### Bloomreach (Discovery / Clarity / Loomi AI) — https://www.bloomreach.com/

- **What it does:** Two-sided play: Bloomreach Discovery = AI search, merchandising, recommendations and long-tail SEO powered by 'Loomi AI'; Bloomreach Engagement = CDP + marketing automation. The strategic argument is that first-party customer data from the CDP personalizes on-site search, not just clickstream. Clarity is their agentic conversational shopping assistant. Loomi Connect provides 175+ integrations (Shopify, Snowflake, Google Cloud, Databricks, AWS).
- **Key features:** Semantic search with real-time personalization driven by first-party CDP data, not only session clickstream; AI-driven merchandising that continuously re-sequences products autonomously ('autonomous search' positioning); Clarity agentic shopping assistant — proactive guidance, catalog-grounded product Q&A; Real-Time Segments for Discovery (merchandisers target live segments); A/B testing and experimentation; analytics and performance monitoring; Autocomplete; multilingual (FR, DE confirmed on the product page); Long-tail SEO landing pages — a revenue surface CAS's design does not touch; Product recommendations; 175+ partner integrations via Loomi Connect
- **Named clients:** Albertsons (US GROCERY incl. Safeway, Vons, Jewel-Osco) — the >25% faster basket-building figure is CONFIRMED via search results for the Bloomreach case study ('Albertsons saw an increase in basket-building speeds of over 25%'; 'today, it's 25% faster to build a basket'; half of ecommerce revenue came from search; they adopted Bloomreach Search & Merchandising and the Semantic Search capability). IMPORTANT CAVEAT ADDED THIS PASS: a direct fetch of the cited case-study URL returned a generic logo-wall page, not the case study, so the metric is search-snippet-verified rather than fetched-on-page. READ THE NEXT FIELD — this reference is also materially STALE.; STALE-REFERENCE WARNING (new, and strategically important): on 2025-12-03 Albertsons announced its own 'Albertsons AI Shopping Assistant', now live across Albertsons, Safeway, Vons, Jewel-Osco, Shaw's, ACME and Tom Thumb banner sites and apps via the search bar. Reporting attributes it to Google Cloud Vertex AI (Conversational Commerce agent) and, separately, PYMNTS attributes Albertsons' recipe-to-cart tooling to OpenAI. Sources conflict on which model vendor; what is NOT in doubt is that Albertsons' flagship AI search surface in 2026 is not being described as Bloomreach. Do not present Albertsons to a client as 'Bloomreach owns grocery AI search' — a well-briefed buyer will correct you.; Boohoo Group (fashion) — PrettyLittleThing, boohoo, boohooMAN, Karen Millen; AI search + merchandising joined to their existing Bloomreach CDP, announced 2024-04-16; Canadian Tire — 20%+ conversion increases across brands including Atmosphere and Mark's (MEDIUM confidence); N Brown (fashion) — lift in revenue per search user; Torrid (fashion) — personalization; Sur La Table, Living Spaces, HD Supply, Wolseley, Hornby Hobbies; case-study page also surfaces Hobbycraft, JD Sports, Motorpoint, Revolution Beauty
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED. Per the original report's verification of bloomreach.com/en/pricing/discovery and /pricing/clarity: subscription = module fee + usage fee, annual billing only, declining per-unit price as usage rises; no tiers, SKU limits or minimums disclosed. Not re-fetched this pass — MEDIUM confidence. KILLED: the '$50k+ annually' figure from a secondary review site is unsourced — do not put it in a deck.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Still a strong dual-segment story on paper — Boohoo Group (four fashion brands), N Brown, Torrid, JD Sports for fashion; Albertsons for grocery. BUT the original report's verdict that this is 'THE BEST DUAL-SEGMENT PROOF SET' must be downgraded: the Albertsons grocery reference is the load-bearing half of that claim and it is now a stale, contested account (see named_clients). On current evidence Algolia has the more defensible grocery proof set, because H-E-B México, Co-op and Auto Mercado are not publicly contested.
- **Demo/trial:** No free tier. Two structured pre-sales offers: (1) a custom-built Clarity Chrome Extension running on the prospect's own catalog, promised 'ready to test in as little as 3 days'; (2) an ROI estimate from the prospect's catalog within 10 days. Both gated behind a form at https://www.bloomreach.com/en/pricing/discovery. Not re-fetched this pass — MEDIUM confidence.
- **Vs CAS:** Their thesis — search personalized by first-party CDP data — sits precisely on CAS's largest admitted gap (no session personalization, no user profile). CAS cannot answer 'why is this shopper's result different from that shopper's'. WHAT THEY HAVE THAT CAS DOES NOT: CDP-fed personalization, autonomous AI merchandising, A/B testing, analytics dashboards, autocomplete, multilingual, real-time merchandiser segments, long-tail SEO page generation, and a 3-day demo-on-your-catalog motion. WHAT CAS HAS: the reranker's explicit per-product reason — and Bloomreach's 'autonomous, stop hand-tuning' positioning is explicitly LESS transparent, which is a fair line of attack on auditability — plus typed hard-constraint enforcement. STRATEGIC NOTE, REVISED: the original report said Bloomreach's grocery story is basket-building speed and that CAS's recipe list is the wedge. That is now only half-true — the Albertsons account itself has moved to conversational recipe-to-cart with another vendor, which means the wedge is not 'recipes' generically but whatever CAS does that a Gemini/GPT-powered assistant does not. See segment takeaways.

#### Coveo — https://www.coveo.com/

- **What it does:** AI-Relevance Platform spanning Commerce, Service, Website and Workplace off one unified index — as much an enterprise-search company as a commerce company. For commerce: ML-ranked search (Automatic Relevance Tuning ranks listing pages from prior visitor interactions), the Coveo Merchandising Hub (no-code console for merchandisers), recommendations, generative answering, and Conversational Product Discovery launched as a paid add-on in March 2026. MACH-certified, composable, Atomic component library.
- **Key features:** Semantic intent understanding across short-head, long-tail and conversational queries; unified indexing across catalog + content; Automatic Relevance Tuning (ART) — behavioural ranking models on listing pages; Coveo Merchandising Hub — RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-13 on the merchandising page: boost/bury/pin per query 'without IT support', build collection pages 'in minutes', slot-level recommendation-strategy control, AI + manually controlled facet curation, KPI monitoring with 'detailed scoring' of ranking factors, and an AGENTIC COPILOT that automates repetitive analysis and surfaces merchandising opportunities (the copilot is a newer capability the original report missed); A/B testing / experimentation built into merchandising tooling; Merchandising on business signals: stock, promos, MARGIN, inventory, RETURN RATES; Query suggestions and autocomplete from ML models; real-time personalization including for anonymous first-time shoppers; Generative answering + Conversational Product Discovery (paid add-on, Mar 2026); Enterprise scale: multiple brands, currencies, regions, 50+ languages and 50+ markets in one environment
- **Named clients:** Freedom Furniture (AU/NZ) — RE-VERIFIED EXACTLY, PRNewswire 2025-05-22: '5.5% uplift in average order value (AOV)', '15% increase in customer sessions with search', '50% decrease in merchandising effort' (first 30 days). NOTE: these figures are NOT on the Coveo merchandising page or the customers page — the citable source is the press release, so cite that URL, not the product page.; Caleres / Famous Footwear (footwear-fashion; also Sam Edelman, Dr. Scholl's) — RE-VERIFIED in the same release: '21% increase in revenue YoY', '25% increase in conversion rate'; FleetPride (B2B truck parts) — RE-VERIFIED: '7% increase in revenue per visit for sessions', '9.6% increase in conversion rate', '27% increase in search engagement'; SAP — testimonial on the customers page ('You can't overstate Coveo's impact. It's massive.' — Michelle Lewis-Miller, VP, Head of Strategy & Transformation), no metrics; LCBO (Ontario liquor retail), Blackwoods, Life Extension — logos; no verified metrics; NOT VERIFIED this pass: the 'FleetPride sales grew more than 400%' line and 'Famous Footwear converts 20-25% above average' — omit or flag.
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED, but METERING is published, which is unusually useful. Per the original report's verification of coveo.com/en/pricing: standard unit = 100,000 queries per month; Passage Retrieval API metered as passage queries/month; Commerce plans include a baseline of 100,000 catalog items; base plans bundle hybrid search, indexing, behavioural AI, security, analytics and infrastructure, while Conversational Search and Generative Answering are SEPARATE PAID ADD-ONS. Not re-fetched this pass — MEDIUM confidence. Third-party: Vendr reports median $43,621/yr, ~15.65% average negotiated saving, banded $30–150K (50K–500K indexed items), $150–400K (500K–5M), $400K+ and potentially $1M+ (5M+ items).
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Fashion/footwear: solid — Caleres/Famous Footwear is a real, metric-backed apparel-adjacent reference verified in a press release. GROCERY: WEAKEST OF THE MAJORS — no supermarket case study surfaced in either pass; LCBO (liquor) is the nearest food/beverage retail proof. Coveo's centre of gravity is B2B, service and workplace — a weakness in a fashion/grocery bake-off, and a hint they will not fight hard for a Kroger-tier deal.
- **Demo/trial:** YES — free trial at https://www.coveo.com/en/free-trial. Coveo developer docs describe a 14-day trial with no credit card and a documented API for creating a Coveo for Commerce trial organization; trial includes most functionality with limits mainly on connectors.
- **Vs CAS:** Overlaps CAS on hybrid/semantic ranking and conversational discovery. WHAT THEY HAVE THAT CAS DOES NOT — and this remains the sharpest lesson in the report, now with a verified citation: Coveo sells the MERCHANDISER's time as the headline outcome ('50% decrease in merchandising effort'). CAS has no business-user surface whatsoever, so it cannot make a claim of that shape at all. Also: Merchandising Hub with an agentic copilot, A/B testing, ART behavioural ranking, margin/return-rate-aware merchandising, 50+ languages and 50+ markets, first-time-visitor personalization, MACH packaging and enterprise connectors. WHAT CAS HAS: per-result reasons, typed constraints, and a recipe surface with no Coveo equivalent. INTEGRATION NOTE (still the best packaging precedent in the segment): Coveo's Passage Retrieval API, sold as a standalone metered unit, is the closest thing here to a component that 'sits alongside' an incumbent — study it as the model for pricing CAS-as-a-layer. Coveo was also named a Leader in the 2026 Gartner MQ (VERIFIED via PRNewswire/Newswire.ca).

#### Netcore Unbxd — https://netcoreunbxd.com/

- **What it does:** API-first AI product discovery suite (Unbxd) bolted to Netcore Cloud's CDP/marketing automation. Vector-based semantic search plus 'advanced query understanding' combining 200+ signals; unusually broad operationally — visual workbench merchandising, segmentation, A/B testing, reports — and explicitly positioned as SELF-SERVE for business teams. Named a Gartner MQ Leader for Search and Product Discovery three consecutive years (2024, 2025, 2026 — the 2026 Leader status is independently corroborated).
- **Key features:** Vector semantic search + query understanding over 200+ signals; dynamic ranking that reorders on behavioural signals; VISUAL SEARCH — image upload (mobile photos, social screenshots, UGC); computer vision detects objects, shapes, patterns, colours; results in ~3 seconds; targeted at fashion, apparel, jewellery, home, beauty; delivered via API/SDK 'without requiring frontend overhauls'; Shopping Agent (conversational), Agentic AI, AI Console; Attribute Enrichment; Measurement/fitment search; Product Badging; Quiz; Merchandising: visual workbench, no-code boost/bury, facet customization, redirects, targeted landing pages; Segmentation; A/B testing; Reports and Insights; Recommendations with their own merchandising + algorithm controls; Netcore Cloud side: journey orchestration, omnichannel marketing, behavioural analytics, retention; Vendor performance claims (marketing numbers, not independently verified): <10ms response for 95% of queries; 24K/sec peak indexing; 35B+ documents/month
- **Named clients:** New York & Company (fashion) +8% CVR; Camper (footwear) +10% search sessions; House of Indya (fashion, India) +30% conversion; Deborah Lippmann (beauty) +40% conversion; SK Jewellery +38.23% AOV; A Jewellers +55.14% revenue; APMEX +22.80% transactions; City Furniture +20% CVR; Jerome's Furniture +25% site-search conversion; Mitre 10 +17% AOV; Fishpools +51% AOV; Backcountry +11% search session demand; Vijay Sales; Bajaj Mall +26.69% conversion; Wex Photo Video -35% bounce; FoodServiceDirect.com +23.40% search conversions — food, but B2B foodservice distribution, NOT a supermarket; Restaurant Equippers +10% AOV; ibSupply +40% revenue; Elite Supplements +55.14% revenue; Natural Chemist; YPO; DoctorOnCall
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED. No public plans; custom quotes built from search volume, traffic and feature set. No credible Vendr/marketplace benchmark surfaced in either pass. Reputationally positioned as the more affordable mid-market option versus Constructor/Bloomreach, but there is NO verifiable figure — do NOT quote a number to the client.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Fashion: good mid-market coverage (New York & Company, Camper, House of Indya, plus jewellery/beauty). GROCERY: THE WEAKEST OF THE MAJORS — the site claims grocery as a served vertical but the only food reference is FoodServiceDirect.com, which is B2B foodservice distribution, not a supermarket. If LatentView wants a segment where a challenger can still win named grocery logos, Unbxd is not occupying it.
- **Demo/trial:** No public free trial or self-serve tier found. Demo request only. Public documentation is open at https://docs.netcoreunbxd.com/docs/introducing-unbxd-search — useful for reverse-engineering their API surface.
- **Vs CAS:** Functionally the closest thing to a SUPERSET of CAS plus the entire operational layer CAS lacks, and the vendor that most directly fills two named CAS gaps at once. WHAT THEY HAVE THAT CAS DOES NOT: visual/image search (a CAS gap, and the most-demanded one in fashion), a no-code visual merchandising workbench, segmentation, A/B testing, reports/insights dashboards, autosuggest, personalization, product badging, guided quizzes, measurement/fitment search. Their 'no heavy engineering involvement' pitch is the direct opposite of CAS's current shape (a FastAPI/Streamlit build only an engineer can operate). WHAT CAS HAS: reranker reason strings, typed hard constraints, recipe/ingredient decomposition. WATCH: their <10ms p95 marketing number versus CAS's ~7s warm AI path — never let a bare latency comparison onto a slide without framing CAS's 7s as a reranked, explained result set rather than a retrieval call. Better still, publish CAS's retrieval-only latency separately from the LLM rerank latency.

#### Lucidworks (Fusion / Lucidworks Platform / Commerce Studio) — https://lucidworks.com/

- **What it does:** The most 'engineering platform' of the set: a Solr/Fusion-based search platform available self-hosted or as managed cloud, with Lucidworks AI providing pre-trained AND custom-trained embedding models, Neural Hybrid Search (lexical + vector + real-time behavioural signals in one retrieval engine), and Gen-AI use cases (RAG, summarization, keyword extraction). Commerce Studio is the merchandiser console (successor to Predictive Merchandiser). AI-Powered Data Enrichment for ecommerce announced 2026-01-20.
- **Key features:** Neural Hybrid Search — lexical precision + semantic vectors + behavioural signals blended in a single retrieval engine; Lucidworks AI: pre-trained embedding models AND custom models trained on the customer's own domain data/signals on GPUs, with evaluation metrics to compare trained models; Commerce Studio (verified in public docs): Rules screen — conditional rules by query/duration/category to pin, boost, bury, block, set facets, redirect; Rewrites screen — term replacement, misspelling correction, phrase rewriting, SYNONYMS, word removal; Head/Tail rule type that swaps underperforming queries for higher-CTR ones, driven by a scheduled Head/Tail Analysis job on the signals collection; Editor with live preview, ranking insights and product performance analytics; Smart Rule Builder for non-technical business users; AI-Powered Data Enrichment for ecommerce (Jan 2026); Fusion connectors to index existing enterprise sources; deployable self-hosted, cloud or via AWS Marketplace
- **Named clients:** Coppel (Mexican department-store retailer) — won Lucidworks' 2025 'Superstars of Search Innovation' award; ML model classifies product type (70% of their searches are product-type queries); usage of popular search terms up 150%; Lenovo — commerce, customer service and workplace on Lucidworks; Mouser Electronics and TE Connectivity — 2025 Superstars award recipients (B2B electronics); KILLED / DO NOT USE — 'one of the world's top five retailers reduced zero-results queries by 91% and increased search-influenced order value by 28%'. This is an anonymous claim, sourced only from a search snippet of a Lucidworks blog; lucidworks.com blocks direct fetching (HTTP 403), so it cannot be verified on-page. An unverifiable anonymous stat is exactly the kind of thing the client lead has said to strip.
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED. Metering model (per third-party summaries): Fusion for Commerce priced by REQUESTS PER YEAR (RPY); Fusion for the Workplace by records indexed. The ITQlick estimate (~$28,000/yr rising to ~$79,000) is a third-party guess with no methodology — LOW CONFIDENCE, do not quote. A Fusion demo edition is listed on AWS Marketplace with no software fees, plus a standard server at hourly or annual rates.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** WEAKEST FIT TO OUR TARGET SEGMENTS. Public reference base skews B2B, manufacturing, distribution and electronics (Lenovo, Mouser, TE Connectivity), plus Coppel as a department store. NO named fashion retailer and NO named grocery retailer found. Lucidworks also did not appear among the announced Leaders in the 2025 Gartner MQ for Search and Product Discovery while Algolia, Bloomreach, Constructor, Coveo, Netcore Unbxd and Google all did — a meaningful signal about their trajectory in retail discovery specifically.
- **Demo/trial:** Partial. A Fusion demo edition is available on AWS Marketplace with no software fees; Fusion can be self-hosted. No consumer-grade free tier. Public product docs are fully open at https://doc.lucidworks.com — the richest free source of design detail for anyone building a merchandising console. NOTE: lucidworks.com marketing pages return HTTP 403 to automated fetches; everything above comes from doc.lucidworks.com, GlobeNewswire and search snippets.
- **Vs CAS:** THE MOST USEFUL VENDOR TO STUDY, THE LEAST DANGEROUS TO COMPETE WITH. Their deployment model — Fusion connectors indexing existing enterprise sources, self-hostable, custom embedding models trained on the client's own signals — is the closest match to how CAS is actually built and to LatentView's 'sell into the retailer's existing stack' thesis. Commerce Studio is a concrete, fully documented blueprint for the merchandising console CAS lacks: copy the Rules/Rewrites/Editor split and the Head/Tail rewrite job. WHAT THEY HAVE THAT CAS DOES NOT: merchandiser rules console, synonym + query-rewrite editor, head/tail query analysis from signals, typeahead configuration, ranking-insight and product analytics in the editor, custom-trained domain embedding models with model-comparison metrics, enterprise connectors. INTERESTING GAP: the Commerce Studio overview docs do NOT list A/B testing — so even a mature platform has holes; there is room to be selectively excellent rather than complete.

#### Attraqt / Crownpeak Product Discovery (Fredhopper + XO) — now owned by Rezolve AI — https://www.crownpeak.com/fredhopper/

- **What it does:** Long-standing European product-discovery suite covering personalization, search, navigation, merchandising, recommendations and internationalization. Two engines — Fredhopper (AI search + visual merchandising, blending data science with human curation) and XO / Experience Orchestrator (formerly Early Birds), which does 1-to-1 recommendations with AI plus co-piloted BUSINESS RULES and AI-based UPSELL AND CROSS-SELL strategies that adapt to real-time shopper behaviour. Heavy on connectors: ecommerce platforms, ERP, WMS, 3PL and POS, an n8n automation node, a Patchworks iPaaS connector and a Shopify app. MATERIAL CORPORATE EVENT (RE-VERIFIED and now with an important addition the original report missed): Rezolve AI (NASDAQ: RZLV) announced the acquisition of Crownpeak on 2025-12-01 for $90M in equity and cash — '$50 million in cash and $33.9 million in equity after adjustments' — PLUS APPROXIMATELY $150 MILLION IN ASSUMED DEBT, against ~$70M Crownpeak revenue. Rezolve's own annual revenue at the time was just $5.3M. As of Aug 2026 crownpeak.com/fredhopper/ 301-redirects to rezolve.com and rezolve.com blocks automated fetches (HTTP 403).
- **Key features:** Fredhopper AI search with typo tolerance, synonym groups, multi-filter, custom ranking, personalized search; Visual merchandising — data science blended with human curation; 'AI Scores' for segmented personalization; XO: 1-to-1 recommendations, AI + co-piloted business rules, AI-driven UPSELL and CROSS-SELL adapting to live behaviour; Navigation, internationalization/multi-language, personalization; A/B testing across product recommendations, merchandising rules and PLP rankings, per-audience; Connectors: ERP/WMS/3PL/POS, Patchworks iPaaS, n8n node (open-sourced at github.com/Crownpeak/n8n-nodes-crownpeak-pd), Shopify theme app blocks + frontend SDK; Published product roadmaps for Fredhopper and XO on support.rezolve.com
- **Named clients:** ASOS, JD Sports, Screwfix, Calvin Klein, Tommy Hilfiger — named in the Crownpeak/Attraqt acquisition press release (2022-12-06, verified via the k1.com and BusinessWire mirrors); 300+ brands total. These are the most defensible names in this entry.; KILLED — Superdry '35% increase in conversion rate': the only source is hamari.agency, an implementation partner's marketing page, not vendor-published. Do not use.; KILLED — Selfridges, Cdiscount, Mango, Decathlon, Marks & Spencer, CARREFOUR and Sephora as Fredhopper customers: search-snippet and agency sources only, unverifiable because crownpeak.com now redirects to rezolve.com and rezolve.com returns HTTP 403. The Carrefour claim is additionally SUSPECT because Carrefour is named as a customer on Empathy.co's own customers page (verified this pass) — so at minimum the attribution is contested. Do not put Carrefour in a Fredhopper column.
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED. Their Shopify app 'Fredhopper Product Discovery' (listed 2025-07-25) is FREE TO INSTALL with charges billed separately by Crownpeak outside the Shopify invoice — a sales-led contract behind a zero-cost install. No marketplace or Vendr benchmark found. Valuation context, now sharper: Attraqt was taken over for £63.2m in 2022; Crownpeak sold for ~$90M in Dec 2025 on ~$70M revenue — ~1.3x revenue, a distressed-looking multiple — and the buyer also took on ~$150M of assumed debt while running only $5.3M of its own revenue.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** THE STRONGEST VERIFIED FASHION LOGO LIST of the original seven — ASOS, JD Sports, Calvin Klein and Tommy Hilfiger are exactly the H&M / C&A / Zara tier LatentView targets, and they are European, matching that buyer profile. GROCERY: none verified. With the Carrefour claim killed, there is no grocery story here at all.
- **Demo/trial:** No self-serve trial of the core platform. Free-to-install Shopify app (https://apps.shopify.com/fredhopper-product-discovery) with external billing is the lowest-friction entry point; otherwise sales-led demos. Public support portal and roadmaps at support.rezolve.com, and open-source n8n nodes on GitHub.
- **Vs CAS:** MOST DIRECT FEATURE OVERLAP WITH CAS'S RECOMMENDATIONS MODULE, AND THE BIGGEST DISPLACEMENT OPPORTUNITY. XO's 'AI + co-piloted business rules for upsell and cross-sell' is the productized, merchandiser-governable version of CAS's app/recommendations.py — the difference is that XO lets a merchandiser co-pilot the AI's choices and CAS does not. WHAT THEY HAVE THAT CAS DOES NOT: visual merchandising console, per-audience A/B testing across recs/rules/PLP rankings, synonym groups and typo tolerance, internationalization/multi-language, AI Scores segmentation, and deep operational connectors (ERP/WMS/3PL/POS) that matter enormously for grocery inventory. THE OPENING, now better evidenced: a ~1.3x revenue multiple, ~$150M of assumed debt sitting on a buyer with $5.3M of its own revenue, a web presence mid-migration into rezolve.com, and the oldest engine in the field. Fashion accounts on Fredhopper are the most winnable targets for a LatentView-led modernization.

#### Google Cloud — AI Commerce Search (formerly Vertex AI Search for commerce / Retail Search) — https://cloud.google.com/solutions/retail-product-discovery

- **What it does:** MISSING FROM THE ORIGINAL REPORT — and it is the most consequential omission, because Google's own blog (which the original report listed as a source without adding Google as a vendor) confirms Vertex AI Search for commerce was named a LEADER in the 2025 Gartner Magic Quadrant for Search and Product Discovery. It is Google's retail discovery stack: search, browse, recommendations, conversational search and semantic image search, delivered as a Google Cloud API over the retailer's own catalog and user-event data. Docs describe a 'four-phased approach' to migration with 'average integration time in the order of weeks', and warn that performance 'is extremely sensitive to the uploaded data, including catalogs, product info, and user events'.
