Yet it still charges $499-$1,399/mo, which reframes the pricing takeaway: the market pays four figures monthly even for search WITHOUT an LLM layer, so CAS's LLM pipeline is not automatically a premium-price justification. Second point: it sold for ~$4.45M despite 1,800+ brands — a sobering valuation datapoint if anyone imagines CAS as a spin-out. Feature-wise the only thing it has over CAS is the merchandising rules editor and smart category-page ranking.

#### Vantage Discovery (acquired by Shopify) — https://www.crunchbase.com/organization/vantage-discovery

- **What it does:** NO LONGER AN INDEPENDENT VENDOR. Founded early 2023 by former Pinterest engineering leaders Lance Riedel and Nigel Daley to bring 'Pinterest-like search' to ecommerce using LLMs and multi-vector search. Raised $16M in June 2024 (Lobby Capital / SignalFire / Webb Investment Network). Acquired by Shopify for $59M in cash, closing March 2025; the hybrid search architecture and multi-vector search APIs were folded into Shopify's app and Storefront search. CAVEAT: I was unable to re-fetch the acquisition sources this session (search budget exhausted), so the $59M figure and close date carry the original researcher's sourcing (BetaKit and MarketScreener), not mine. The $59M number should be re-confirmed before it appears on a slide.
- **Key features:** Multi-vector semantic search API; Hybrid keyword + vector architecture; LLM-driven personalization and relevance across large heterogeneous catalogues; Reported 10%+ lift in AOV and conversion at early large-retailer adopters
- **Named clients:** Not disclosed by name — press coverage referred only to 'some of the largest global retailers.' Do not attribute specific logos; Effectively now: Shopify, as acquirer
- **Pricing:** N/A — the standalone product is gone; capabilities surface inside Shopify's native search rather than as a purchasable third-party product.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** N/A as a vendor. The relevant consequence is that Shopify's NATIVE storefront search is materially better, which erodes the app-shaped search category beneath it.
- **Demo/trial:** N/A — acquired.
- **Vs CAS:** STRATEGIC, not competitive. Three takeaways: (1) a two-year-old LLM search startup with no disclosed logos was worth $59M to a platform, so the technology alone is buyable and CAS's defensibility must be the retail-specific pipeline and consulting wrapper, not the vector search; (2) platform owners are absorbing this capability into the substrate, so any pitch that a retailer 'needs' a bolt-on semantic layer weakens each year; (3) their hybrid keyword+vector architecture is precisely CAS's BM25-router plus embedding design — and with Algolia's NeuralSearch doing the same, this confirms the architecture is right but definitively not novel.

#### Swap (swap-commerce.com) — https://www.swap-commerce.com/storefront

- **What it does:** CARRIED FORWARD WITH A CONFIDENCE DOWNGRADE. 2025-2026 LLM-era entrant selling an 'Agentic Commerce Platform' — a ChatGPT-style storefront that replaces the conventional PLP/PDP browse experience, taking a shopper from intent to purchase inside an AI-native surface. Modern Retail reported the platform creating AI shopping agents for brands launching September 2025. I could not re-verify the vendor page or the Forbes coverage this session; treat all specifics as single-sourced from the original research.
- **Key features:** Full agentic storefront rather than a search box bolted onto an existing site — the most radical repositioning in this segment; Native memory of individual customer preferences across sessions; Text AND VOICE prompts; Virtual try-on for fashion; AI demand planning on the back end — forecasting, fulfilment and returns agents
- **Named clients:** NONE DISCLOSED in the coverage — press describes 'brands' generically. Do not imply a client base
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION-LEANING (virtual try-on is an apparel feature), aimed at DTC brands rather than multi-thousand-store retailers. No grocery relevance — an agentic storefront is a poor fit for a 60-item weekly basket.
- **Demo/trial:** Demo request only; no public sandbox found.
- **Vs CAS:** A DIRECTIONAL WARNING, not a deal competitor — and the reported '2x conversion rate' figure is vendor-supplied, was not independently verified in either session, and should NOT be repeated in a LatentView deck. The durable point stands without it: Swap's bet is that the search box disappears and the storefront becomes the agent. CAS's whole design — results list, pagination, product detail pages, a separate Keyword Search page — assumes the search box survives. If a fashion client has seen Swap, 'we improved your search results' sounds incremental. What they have that CAS does not: session memory, voice input, virtual try-on, back-office forecasting agents. What CAS has: it integrates INTO an existing stack rather than replacing the storefront, which for an H&M-tier retailer with a large existing search investment is a far easier sell. That is the honest counter-position.

#### Miso.ai — https://miso.ai/

- **What it does:** VERIFIED, AND THEY HAVE LARGELY LEFT RETAIL. miso.ai now leads with 'Miso Answers,' a grounded LLM answer engine for PUBLISHERS and public-sector content, not an ecommerce search product. The retail positioning (in-session and post-checkout recommendations) survives only in older profiles and directory listings, not on the current homepage.
- **Key features:** Miso Answers — cited answers generated exclusively from the customer's own content catalogue; claims '99.99% true to source'; Anti-hallucination via content-specific grounding and citation verification; Claims 10-25x more engagement vs keyword search; Monetisation: ads built into answers, claimed up to 15x higher CTR — the only vendor here productising ads INSIDE generative answers; Legacy retail: in-session recommendations on web/mobile, post-checkout recommendations via email
- **Named clients:** O'Reilly; PCWorld; Macworld; TechHive; Tech Advisor; CIO
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED as figures, but the MODEL is stated and unusual: 'a simple monthly fee that gives you unlimited API requests and responses' with no token-based charges (https://docs.miso.ai/introduction/pricing). Flat-fee and uncapped — the opposite of the metered models everyone else uses.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** POOR / NOT APPLICABLE NOW. Every named client is a publisher or professional-media brand. No fashion or grocery retailer. Their retail past is real but they are not competing for H&M or Kroger.
- **Demo/trial:** YES and genuinely useful — a free sandbox where you submit your domain and it generates a private search engine over your content. Worth using as a reference implementation of a cited-answer UX.
- **Vs CAS:** DE-PRIORITISE as a competitor; do not build a slide around them. Two things worth borrowing: (1) their flat-fee/unlimited-requests pricing is a strong counter-position to Algolia's, Prefixbox's and Doofinder's per-request metering — directly relevant if LatentView prices CAS; (2) they are the only vendor here that has productised ADS INSIDE GENERATIVE ANSWERS, adjacent to CAS's relevance-gated sponsored layer, validating that ad monetisation of AI answers is a real revenue line. Their 'true to source' citation framing is also a better way to talk about CAS's per-result reason strings than 'explainability.'

#### Rebuy — https://www.rebuyengine.com/

- **What it does:** VERIFIED, pricing re-confirmed exactly. Shopify-ONLY personalization engine — smart carts, recommendations, post-purchase upsells, dynamic bundles — that has added 'Rebuy Smart Search' as an AI site-search module. The most app-shaped vendor on the list and the least enterprise.
- **Key features:** Smart Search — 'AI-powered product discovery,' sold as an a-la-carte component inside a 'Search & Collections' package rather than a core product; Smart Collections; Smart Cart, dynamic bundles, post-purchase upsell — their actual centre of gravity, and closest to CAS's cross-sell/upsell module; Rebuy Monetize — a FREE tier where the merchant EARNS $0.20-0.35+ per transaction (estimated ~$175/mo). A monetisation model, not a cost; Modular build-your-own plan — pay only for packages used
- **Named clients:** NONE ASSERTED. No named brands appear on the pricing page fetched in either session. Rebuy is widely used in the Shopify DTC ecosystem but I will not assert a logo list without a primary source
- **Pricing:** FULLY PUBLISHED AND THE CHEAPEST HERE — RE-VERIFIED EXACTLY from https://www.rebuyengine.com/pricing : Rebuy Monetize free (merchant earns $0.20-$0.35+ per transaction, est. $175/mo); Build Your Own Plan from $25/mo for one package, scaling with monthly order volume; Platform One $534/mo billed monthly for all products plus premium support, dedicated CSM and beta access; Premium Support add-on $499/mo. 14-day trial on standard packages, 30-day on Platform One.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** NEITHER, at the target tier. Shopify DTC only. Irrelevant to H&M/C&A/Zara and to Kroger/Albertsons/HEB. Include for landscape completeness and price-floor context only.
- **Demo/trial:** YES — free Monetize tier, 14-day trial on Build Your Own, 30-day on Platform One.
- **Vs CAS:** LOW as a competitor, useful in two ways. (1) Their cross-sell/upsell/bundle engine is the commoditised version of CAS's recommendations module, from $25/mo. If LatentView leans on cross-sell as a selling point, this is the counter-example; CAS's real differentiator there is that complements are LLM-PROPOSED then GROUNDED in the catalogue via embedding retrieval, whereas Rebuy is rules plus behavioural. (2) Their Monetize tier — where search and recommendations become a revenue source for the merchant rather than a cost — is the most interesting commercial idea in the segment and rhymes directly with CAS's sponsored-ads layer. Search that pays for itself is a stronger pitch to a grocery buyer than search that costs EUR 2,350/mo.

#### Daydream and the D2C agentic cohort (Phia, OneOff, Gensmo) — https://www.modernretail.co/technology/why-the-ai-shopping-agent-wars-will-heat-up-in-2026/

- **What it does:** CARRIED FORWARD WITH ONE FUNDING FIGURE QUARANTINED. Per Modern Retail: Daydream, founded by Julie Bornstein, launched in beta June 2025 after a $50M seed; shoppers describe what they want conversationally OR upload a photo and get personalized recommendations across nearly 2 million products from 10,000+ brands. Phia (co-founded by Phoebe Gates, $8M in Sept 2025) does price comparison; OneOff does celebrity/creator-look recommendations. QUARANTINED: the 'Gensmo raised a $60M seed, largest in AI shopping as of May 2026' claim came from a single aggregator blog seen only at search-result level and was never independently verified in either session. Do not use it.
- **Key features:** Conversational natural-language fashion discovery; Photo upload as a first-class query modality; Cross-brand aggregated catalogue (~2M products, 10,000+ brands for Daydream); Personalization across sessions; Fashion dominates the entire cohort
- **Named clients:** N/A — these are DIRECT-TO-CONSUMER destinations, not vendors selling into retailers. They have users, not clients
- **Pricing:** N/A — consumer products, not licensed software.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION-DOMINANT, consumer side. Not competing for an H&M procurement cycle, but competing for the H&M shopper's attention, which is arguably worse.
- **Demo/trial:** Publicly usable consumer apps — the fastest way to see what a 2026 fashion shopper now considers normal. Worth doing before any client meeting.
- **Vs CAS:** Not competitors, but they set the EXPECTATION BAR and they are much of the reason retailers are buying at all. The strongest opening for a LatentView deck is precisely that these aggregators are pulling discovery off the retailer's own site, and a retailer that cannot match the interaction quality loses the top of its funnel. Note the modality gap: every one of these takes a PHOTO as input. CAS has no visual search, and in fashion that is now a baseline consumer expectation rather than an advanced feature.

**Segment takeaways:**

- METHOD AND CONFIDENCE, STATED UP FRONT. This session's WebSearch budget was already exhausted, so every verification below was done by direct page fetch. Where a site blocked automated fetching (Luigi's Box, Rezolve, and Empathy's own blog index for the Ocado check) I say so rather than laundering the original researcher's claim into a verified one. Net result: 12 vendor claims independently re-confirmed exactly, 3 client claims killed, 1 major scope claim narrowed, 2 client names downgraded, 2 stats quarantined, and 5 vendors added. Anything below marked VERIFIED was read off the vendor's own live page in this session.
- THREE CLAIMS FROM THE ORIGINAL REPORT MUST NOT REACH A CLIENT DECK. (1) WEIS MARKETS as an Instacart Cart Assistant client — the 4 Nov 2025 announcement names only Kroger (Cart Assistant, iOS), Sprouts (web/app/Caper Carts), Good Food Holdings and McKeever's (Store View). Weis appears nowhere. (2) OCADO as an Empathy.co client, plus 'Ocado uses Related Tags to cut search noise' — Ocado is absent from empathy.co/customers and there is no Ocado post on the Empathy blog index. (3) EMPATHY.CO'S SPECIFIC MODEL NAMES (DeepSeek R1, Mistral, Qwen3) — their AI Mode doc says only 'Empathy.ai technology' with self-hosted open-weight models on private infrastructure. Also downgrade UNIQLO and GYMSHARK as Nosto logos (absent from the CXP page; they came from a Nosto competitive blog), cut the $599-1,099 Searchspring price range (third-party trackers only, unverifiable), and drop the Gensmo $60M seed and Swap's '2x conversion' figure (single aggregator source and vendor-supplied respectively). Ganesan asked for links on every stat; these are the ones with no link that survives a click.
- THE EMPATHY.CO-KROGER CLAIM IS REAL BUT WAS OVERSTATED, AND THE CORRECTION MATTERS BECAUSE IT IS THE CENTREPIECE OF THE GROCERY NARRATIVE. The original said Empathy serves 'all Search, Navigation and Keyword traffic on Kroger's ecommerce web AND iOS/Android apps.' Their own page says something narrower: collaboration began 'nearly five years ago, pre-pandemic,' and 'the Search & Discovery used by all Kroger brands on both desktop and mobile is a product of this partnership,' across Kroger's 20+ brands, with no traffic figures and no app-platform breakdown. Use that wording verbatim. It is still devastating enough — Empathy holds Kroger and Carrefour, both verified — but an overstated competitor claim is the kind of thing a Kroger-side stakeholder corrects live in the room.
- THE BIGGEST OMISSION WAS CONSTRUCTOR, AND IT BELONGS IN THE TOP THREE FOR THE FASHION PITCH. AI-native, exclusively product discovery, with Sephora, Gap, Under Armour, REI, Foot Locker, Bath & Body Works, Belk (+$35M revenue), Target Australia (+A$13M), Williams Sonoma, Rothy's and Bonobos — genuinely enterprise-apparel, unlike the Shopify DTC vendors that filled the original list. They ship three agents (AI Shopping Agent, Product Insights Agent, and a Merchant Intelligence Agent that explains search rankings to merchandisers), and publish 400+ billion requests annually, 8-week average implementation and 98.5% three-year client retention. Those last two are the de-risking numbers a retail CIO weighs a consultancy build against, and CAS has no answer to either. Grocery is their thin side (flaschenpost, BJ's), so they threaten the fashion pitch specifically.
- THE SECOND-BIGGEST OMISSION WAS LILY AI, AND IT IS POSITIONALLY THE MOST DANGEROUS BECAUSE IT IS LATENTVIEW'S EXACT PITCH. Their platform 'optimizes the product intelligence your feed manager, catalog system, and commerce stack already distribute, so there is no replatforming' — that is CAS's sell-into-the-existing-stack story, already shipping, with Coach, M&S, J.Crew, Kate Spade, UGG, HOKA, Foot Locker, Vuori and Fabletics as logos. Critically, they attack a different layer: instead of better ranking, they enrich the catalogue with shopper-language attributes so the retailer's existing search works better, and they prove it with built-in controlled A/B testing (+28.3% onsite search revenue for a luxury brand). The lesson for the CAS roadmap is uncomfortable and useful: they win the integrate-don't-replace argument by MEASURING LIFT, not by having better retrieval. An experimentation capability is worth more to CAS's commercial story right now than any further retrieval tuning.
- CONSOLIDATION IS WORSE THAN THE ORIGINAL REPORT SAID — FIVE VENDORS, NOT THREE. Klevu + Searchspring + Intelligent Reach into PSG-backed Athos Commerce (13 Jan 2025); Vantage Discovery into Shopify for a reported $59M (closed Mar 2025); Findify into Maropost for just over $4.45M (Jun 2022). NEW THIS PASS: groupbyinc.com now returns a 301 permanent redirect to rezolve.com — GroupBy, the leading Google Vertex AI Search for Commerce implementation partner and a grocery/B2B-leaning vendor, has been absorbed into Rezolve AI (redirect directly observed; the transaction itself needs a primary source, as rezolve.com blocks automated fetching). The independent mid-market search vendor is a shrinking category with modest exit multiples — which matters if anyone at LatentView is framing CAS as a productisable asset rather than a consulting capability.
- CONVERSATIONAL / AGENTIC SHOPPING IS TABLE STAKES IN 2026, AND TWO MORE CAS DIFFERENTIATORS DIED THIS PASS. Every serious vendor ships one: Athos Conversational Assistant, Luigi's Box Conversational Agent, Doofinder AI Assistant, Prefixbox AI Agent, Fast Simon AI Shopping Agent, Empathy.co AI Mode, Constructor AI Shopping Agent, Marqo Agentic Storefront, Nosto Huginn, Algolia Agent Studio. Beyond that: (a) Fast Simon's RANKING EXPLAINER ships on EVERY tier from $39.99/mo, so CAS's per-result reason string is commoditised, and Constructor's Merchant Intelligence Agent explains rankings to merchandisers too; (b) Empathy's AI Mode is an OPTIONAL BUTTON that appears on multi-word queries and 'ultimately resolves into accurate search queries' so ranking rules survive — which is materially the same design as CAS's keyword-first router with AI fallback, plus Algolia's NeuralSearch and Vantage's architecture were both hybrid keyword+vector. CAS's architecture is correct and validated, but it is not novel, and no slide should imply otherwise.
- PUBLISHED PRICE ANCHORS, ALL RE-VERIFIED THIS SESSION ON THE VENDOR'S OWN PAGE. Doofinder $49/$149/$349 per month (NOTE: the AI Assistant is a SEPARATE line item on Pro+, so the headline understates the true comparable); Fast Simon $39.99/$99.99/$299.99 on Shopify; Klevu $649 Site Search / $549 Category Merch / $449 Recommendations per module; Findify $499/$799/$1,399; Rebuy $25/mo to $534/mo; Prefixbox EUR 1,250/mo AI Search and EUR 2,350/mo for the full bundle at 600k-900k API requests. NEW ANCHOR FROM ALGOLIA, and it is the most quotable number in the report: incremental search requests cost $0.50 per 1K on the keyword tier versus $1.75 per 1K on the AI tier — the market prices AI relevance at roughly a 3.5x premium over keyword. Separately, Findify charges $499-$1,399/mo for a product with NO conversational assistant at all, which kills the assumption that an LLM layer automatically justifies a premium. Two pricing models are worth stealing as counter-positions to per-query LLM cost: Nosto prices on GMV with no query limits, and Miso charges a flat monthly fee with unlimited API requests.
- GROCERY IS THE MOST DEFENDED VERTICAL AND CAS'S FLAGSHIP FEATURE IS ALREADY DEPLOYED THERE — CONFIRMED AND SLIGHTLY WORSE THAN REPORTED. Empathy.co has powered Search & Discovery for all Kroger brands on desktop and mobile for nearly five years, and holds Carrefour. Prefixbox holds Carrefour, Auchan and Rossmann with a purpose-built FMCG multistore per-location availability feature — AND its A/B testing plus offline relevance measurement are included at EVERY paid tier, not gated to enterprise. Cooklist's agentic cart builder is live at Kroger (Fred Meyer, Ralphs, Baker's, Dillons, QFC, Smith's) and Wegmans across 700+ stores, expanding by ~700 more plus 10 unnamed banners, with a SIX-WEEK integration timeline. Instacart's whitelabeled Cart Assistant is being launched by Kroger in its iOS app and tested by Sprouts, with inventory AND loyalty integration and three-tier content moderation. CAS's recipe-to-shopping-list feature must be reframed from 'novel differentiator' to 'validated demand — two vendors already sell this pattern into Kroger.' Claiming novelty in front of a grocery buyer will not survive one question.
- FASHION IS LESS LOCKED BUT THE REAL COMPETITIVE SET IS BIGGER THAN THE ORIGINAL REPORT DREW IT. Adding the missed vendors, the genuine enterprise-fashion competitors are six, not four: Constructor (Sephora, Gap, Under Armour, Foot Locker, Belk), Athos Commerce (adidas, Rip Curl, River Island, AS Colour), Empathy.co (Pronovias, PDPAOLA, Tous, Toys R Us), Nosto (Muji, Belstaff, DVF, Figs, Vuori), Lily AI (Coach, M&S, J.Crew, UGG, HOKA) and Marqo (Mejuri, KICKS CREW). Syte (Prada, Farfetch, PrettyLittleThing, Decathlon) competes for the visual-search line item specifically. The Shopify-app vendors (Rebuy, Fast Simon, Findify/Maropost) own DTC apparel but will not appear in an H&M or C&A procurement.
- THE FOUR UNIVERSAL CAPABILITY GAPS ARE CONFIRMED AND ONE IS NOW CHEAPER TO REBUT THAN THOUGHT. (1) MERCHANDISER CONSOLE — Searchspring, Nosto, Fast Simon, Empathy's Playboard, Constructor's Merchant Intelligence Agent and Luigi's Box plain-language rules all ship one. (2) A/B TESTING — Fast Simon includes it at $299.99/mo on Shopify, Prefixbox includes it plus OFFLINE RELEVANCE MEASUREMENT at every paid tier, Nosto has a whole optimisation cloud, and Lily AI makes it the core proof mechanism. This is the most damaging gap because it is how every competitor converts a demo into a business case. (3) VISUAL/IMAGE SEARCH — Athos, Doofinder (Advanced), Fast Simon (Enterprise), Marqo, Syte as a whole company, and every D2C agent take photo input; Instacart even ingests handwritten lists. (4) MULTILINGUAL — Prefixbox does 90+ languages, Doofinder and Luigi's Box are multi-language by default. Pre-empt all four with a roadmap slide rather than discovering them live.
- GENERATIVE ENGINE OPTIMIZATION IS NOW A REAL CATEGORY WITH AT LEAST THREE VENDORS SELLING INTO IT, AND CAS HAS NOTHING. Athos launched a GEO Assistant on 10 Jun 2026 that syndicates catalogue data for visibility inside ChatGPT and Gemini, reaching 1,400+ marketplaces, social sites and AI platforms (verified on their homepage). Lily AI explicitly optimises product intelligence for 'AI discovery and agentic commerce' across ChatGPT, Gemini and AI shopping agents. Fast Simon puts 'Ask Claude / Ask ChatGPT / Ask Perplexity' buttons on its own pricing page. Instacart shipped agentic-commerce integrations with OpenAI, Google and Microsoft in Nov 2025. 'How do we show up when the shopper asks ChatGPT instead of visiting our site' is now a standard RFP question and an on-site search layer has no answer — worth flagging to Ganesan as a scope-expansion opportunity rather than pretending it is out of scope.
- WHAT CAS GENUINELY STILL OWNS IS NARROWER THAN THE ORIGINAL REPORT CLAIMED, BUT IT IS REAL. After this verification pass, four things survive: (a) THE RELEVANCE-GATED SPONSORED-AD LAYER held out of organic ranking, always labelled, bid never exposed — no vendor in this segment markets ad separation as an auditable design property, and it answers the retail-media monetisation question every grocer and fashion retailer now has (Miso's ads-in-answers and Rebuy's Monetize tier prove the revenue line is real, but neither offers the auditability); (b) TYPED HARD-CONSTRAINT EXTRACTION THAT CAPS THE RERANK SCORE CEILING rather than soft-boosting — more principled than the guided-selling quizzes Luigi's Box and Doofinder ship; (c) THE EVAL HARNESS — P@10/MRR/NDCG plus recipe-ingredient completeness plus injection and robustness stress tests; vendors publish outcome stats but none hands over a reproducible evaluation methodology, and for a consultancy the measurement rig is a more defensible deliverable than the retrieval code; (d) COMBINED LANGUAGE + VISION as a roadmap item — Syte proves vision-first is fundable and Marqo proves multimodal retrieval works, yet the market is still split between vision-first and language-first vendors. NOTE that the hybrid keyword-first router is NO LONGER on this list: Empathy's AI Mode button, Algolia's NeuralSearch and Vantage's architecture all do hybrid.
- TRANSLATE CAS'S METRICS INTO THE MARKET'S VOCABULARY BEFORE ANY CLIENT MEETING — THE EVIDENCE FOR THIS IS NOW OVERWHELMING. Every vendor reports business outcomes and not one publishes an IR metric: Constructor (Belk +$35M, Target Australia +A$13M, White Stuff +21% search conversion, Grove 20.07X ROI, Cromwell 21X ROI), Marqo (Mejuri +19.8% search revenue per user, Redbubble +$11M, KICKS CREW +17.7%, SwimOutlet +10.6% ATC), Prefixbox (Bauhaus +45% revenue, up to 40% click rate on chat-recommended products, up to $12,000/mo support saving), Athos (Michael Stars +951% revenue per visit, +15% AOV), Lily AI (+28.3% onsite search revenue), Findify (+26% search conversion, +7% AOV). Zero publish NDCG. Leading with NDCG 0.950 lands as a research artefact; the same evidence as revenue-per-visitor and add-to-cart lift lands as a business case. Two adjacent risks to pre-empt: CAS's ~7s warm AI path will surface in a live demo rather than a spec sheet (no vendor publishes latency, so nobody will ask until they see it), and Constructor's 8-week implementation plus Cooklist's 6-week integration are the delivery-timeline benchmarks any LatentView scoping estimate will be measured against.

### GROCERY retail search & discovery (P0 target vertical) — FACT-CHECKED AND EXPANDED. What Kroger, Albertsons, H-E-B, Tesco, Sainsbury's, Ahold Delhaize and Instacart actually run, plus the vendor landscape for recipe-to-cart, substitutions, basket building, dietary/allergen filtering, private-label promotion and retail media in grocery search. Includes a direct assessment of whether anyone already ships CAS's "recipe -> ingredient shopping list with brand alternatives" feature. VERIFICATION PASS NOTE: every figure below was re-checked by fetching the cited page. Claims that survived are marked VERIFIED; claims that did NOT survive have been corrected, downgraded or deleted and are listed explicitly in the final takeaway. Two vendors the original draft missed entirely (Empathy.co — which names KROGER as a customer — and Ocado Technology/OSP, which names Kroger, Morrisons, Asda, Coles and Sobeys) have been added.

#### Instacart (AI Solutions / Cart Assistant / Catalog Intelligence / Carrot Ads / Storefront Pro / Caper Carts) — https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/instacart-announces-new-enterprise-ai-solutions-to-democratize-ai-for-grocers-of-all-sizes-302603735.html

- **What it does:** VERIFIED (2025-11-04): Instacart announced 'Instacart's AI Solutions', licensing its own grocery AI stack to retailers. Search results and trade coverage describe five components: Agentic Commerce, AI Assistant (branded Cart Assistant), In-Store Intelligence, Catalog Intelligence and Agentic Analytics. Cart Assistant is 'a new generation of enterprise-grade, white-label agentic tool purpose-built for grocery that can be integrated through Storefront Pro online or embedded into Caper Carts in-store', supporting meal planning, budgeting and nutrition while 'learning from each customer's preferences and purchase history'. Launch retailers named: Kroger, Sprouts Farmers Market, Good Food Holdings. Separately Instacart runs its own consumer search stack (LLM intent engine + embedding retrieval + ranking) and Carrot Ads, its white-label retail media platform. This is the single most important competitor in the segment: simultaneously the benchmark grocery search experience AND a vendor selling that experience to the exact retailers CAS targets.
- **Key features:** VERIFIED, quotes re-checked against the source: LLM 'Intent Engine' for query understanding — query rewrites and Semantic Role Labeling. 'This structured approach increased our query rewrite coverage to over 95% with 90%+ precision across all three types.' Real-time model: 'we fine-tuned an open-source Llama-3-8B model using LoRA'. Production model 'has higher precision 96.4% vs 95.4%, lower recall 95% vs 96.2%'. Latency: 'The out-of-the-box latency was nearly 700ms with A100 GPU… Merging the LoRA adapter weights directly into the base model and upgrading to H100 GPUs got us to our 300ms target.' Outcomes: 'we reduce average scroll depth by 6%' and 'reducing user complaints related to poor search results for tail queries by 50%' (https://company.instacart.com/tech-innovation/building-the-intent-engine-how-instacart-is-revamping-query-understanding-with-llms); CORRECTION — the original draft's claim that an offline 'teacher' model covers ~98% of queries is NOT stated in that post as fetched, and the '50%' rewrite-coverage baseline was not confirmed either. Do not use either number. The verified architecture is: offline frontier-model generation cached for head/torso queries + a real-time LoRA-tuned 8B for cold-start/tail queries 'serving millions of cold-start queries weekly'.; VERIFIED (paper abstract quoted directly): 'An Embedding-Based Grocery Search Model at Instacart' — two-tower transformer query/product encoders, self-adversarial learning + cascade training, content-based cold-start features. 'On an offline human evaluation dataset, we achieve 10% relative improvement in RECALL@20, and for online A/B testing, we achieve 4.1% cart-adds per search (CAPS) and 1.5% gross merchandise value (GMV) improvement.' (https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.05555); VERIFIED: LLM-generated discovery content grounded in the real catalog — 'we take each item in the list generated above, treat it like a search query, and invoke our existing search engine to get the best product matches', followed by 'a diversity based reranking algorithm to ensure that users can see a variety of options'. Result: 'this methodology led to an 18% improvement in engagement rate with the inspirational content' (attributed specifically to using next-converted-search-terms to inform generation) (https://tech.instacart.com/supercharging-discovery-in-search-with-llms-556c585d4720); Out-of-stock replacement model (cited from the Instacart engineering post; architecture description retained from the original draft, not independently re-fetched this pass): Siamese two-tower network over BERT title embeddings + categorical features + binary dietary flags, ensembled with a per-pair engagement/memorization model; candidate generation recalls >95% of shopper-picked replacements (https://tech.instacart.com/how-instacart-uses-machine-learning-to-suggest-replacements-for-out-of-stock-products-8f80d03bb5af); VERIFIED with corrections — Smart Shop (2025-03-18): '30 Health Tags'; 'Health Tags provide transparent easy-to-digest details on approximately half a million items'; 'Instacart has tagged over 1.3 billion data points across food and beverage products'; built by VLMs — 'leverages vision-language models (VLMs) to extract rich product attributes from text and images'. CORRECTION: the release does NOT mention human-in-the-loop review; drop that. The 14 user-selectable dietary preferences are exactly: 'gluten free, high protein, high fiber, lactose free, low calorie, low carb, low sodium, low sugar, low fat, organic, pescatarian, preservative free, vegan and vegetarian' — note FAT FREE and KOSHER are NOT in this list (https://company.instacart.com/pressreleases/instacart-launches-ai-powered-smart-shop-and-new-features-that-make-healthy-choices-easy); VERIFIED: Recipe-to-cart via the Developer Platform. Best practice is explicitly 'Specify the generic product name without any weight, quantity, or brand names. This provides the broadest match possible.' Optional brand_filters ('spell the brand name exactly as it appears on Instacart to ensure a match') and health_filters with allowed values ORGANIC, GLUTEN_FREE, FAT_FREE, VEGAN, KOSHER, SUGAR_FREE, LOW_FAT. CORRECTION: the docs do NOT describe shoppers being shown multiple brand alternatives per ingredient, and do not describe the matching algorithm at all (https://docs.instacart.com/developer_platform_api/guide/concepts/recipe); VERIFIED on Instacart's own Carrot Ads page: 'demand from 9,000+ CPG brands' and the case-study headline 'Schnucks increases Retail Media revenue by 7X with Carrot Ads'. MRC-accredited ad formats and the '240+ ecommerce partners / 7,500+ CPG advertisers' figure come from the 2025-11-06 PRNewswire release (https://company.instacart.com/enterprise-platform/retail-media/carrot-ads)
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED as AI Solutions launch retailers: Kroger, Sprouts Farmers Market, Good Food Holdings; VERIFIED scope detail: Sprouts is first to deploy Cart Assistant, across web + app (Storefront Pro) and Caper Carts; Kroger will launch Cart Assistant within its first-party Kroger iOS app; VERIFIED on the Carrot Ads page itself — 10 named retailer partners: Price Chopper, Save Mart, Coburn's, Harmon's, GoodFood, Schnucks, Thrive Market, Woodman's Markets, The Fresh Market, Hy-Vee; DOWNGRADED: 'McKeever's (Store View)' could not be verified this pass — do not put it in a deck. 'Good Food Holdings (Store View live)' is confirmed only as an AI Solutions launch retailer, not specifically for Store View.; DOWNGRADED: Carrot Tags customers (Schnucks, Aldi, Gelson's, Hornbacher's) come from trade coverage that was not re-fetched this pass; cite the trade article, not Instacart, if used.
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED. Carrot Ads launched 2022-03-24 on an explicit revenue-sharing model with Instacart (percentage undisclosed); pilot retailers were Schnuck Markets, Good Food Holdings and Plum Markets (https://www.marketingdive.com/news/instacarts-new-tech-platform-lets-retailers-sell-ads-on-their-sites/620952/). AI Solutions pricing is disclosed nowhere public. INFERENCE ONLY: enterprise contract plus rev-share hybrid. Do not quote a number.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery-native, best-in-class. Effectively zero fashion fit (no size/fit/visual attributes). For CAS this is the reference implementation to benchmark against, not a fashion competitor.
- **Demo/trial:** Yes — free public demo of the consumer experience at instacart.com and in the Instacart app (Smart Shop dietary filters, Health Tags, shoppable recipes). No sandbox for enterprise AI Solutions. Developer Platform docs are public and free to read at https://docs.instacart.com/developer_platform_api/guide/concepts/recipe
- **Vs CAS:** HIGHEST OVERLAP OF ANY VENDOR AND THE BIGGEST COMMERCIAL THREAT. Overlaps CAS almost feature-for-feature: LLM query understanding, embedding retrieval, LLM-generated complements grounded back through the real catalog (architecturally identical to CAS's cross-sell — they also could not use market-basket data and fell back to LLM+retrieval), dietary constraint handling, sponsored products kept separate from organic. WHAT THEY DO THAT CAS DOES NOT: (1) a production out-of-stock substitution model — CAS has none, and this is a top-3 grocery requirement; (2) store-level availability; (3) 30 Health Tags built by VLM extraction over 1.3B data points on ~500K items — i.e. they solved the CATALOG ATTRIBUTE problem, which is the real bottleneck for dietary filtering, whereas CAS only parses the constraint out of the query; (4) explicit diversity-based reranking in the LLM discovery pipeline — a documented, published fix for CAS's open '70 paneer sellers' P2; (5) a 300ms tail-query latency target vs CAS's ~7s warm; (6) purchase-history personalization and reorder; (7) an MRC-accredited ad server. WHAT CAS DOES THAT THEY DO NOT PUBLICLY DESCRIBE: per-result human-readable reason strings, an explicit hard-constraint score-ceiling mechanism, and a relevance-gated ad policy that never blends into organic. CRITICAL SALES RISK: Instacart is already selling Cart Assistant into Kroger and Sprouts — the CAS buyer may already have this vendor in the building.

#### Google Cloud — Conversational Commerce agent on Vertex AI (+ Gemini Enterprise for CX) — https://www.googlecloudpresscorner.com/2025-09-10-Google-Cloud-Launches-Conversational-Commerce-Agent,-Delivering-AI-Enabled,-Personalized-Shopping-Experiences-for-Customers

- **What it does:** VERIFIED (launched 2025-09-10): an off-the-shelf conversational product-discovery agent that sits on the retailer's search bar, built on Vertex AI. An intent classifier routes simple queries to traditional search and escalates complex/ambiguous ones to multi-turn Gemini-powered guidance grounded in the retailer's catalog, with admin controls to boost, bury or restrict products and categories. NOTE FOR ACCURACY: Google also sells a separate, older product — Vertex AI Search for commerce (Retail Search / Browse / Recommendations) — which is the underlying ranking engine rather than the conversational layer. Treat the agent and the ranking engine as two distinct SKUs when scoping against CAS; I was not able to fetch a clean Google page enumerating the commerce suite this pass, so do not quote suite composition as verified.
- **Key features:** Intent classifier that routes between keyword search and the conversational agent — architecturally the SAME idea as CAS's keyword-first router that falls back to the AI pipeline on a lexical miss; Multi-turn dialogue with context retained across site interactions and devices; Catalog grounding, product Q&A and contextual information; Merchandiser controls: boost / bury / restrict — a console CAS does not have; VERIFIED at Kroger (2026-01-11): Gemini Enterprise for CX deployed nationwide, including a personal shopping assistant that can 'complete complex tasks from a single instruction—such as explore meal ideas, build complex carts' and converts requests into guided recipes with ingredient lists, plus a Customer Experience Agent Studio for call analysis. Grounding language is explicit: recommendations are 'grounded in actual assortment, pricing, and availability' (https://ir.kroger.com/news/news-details/2026/Kroger-Scales-Generative-AI-Strategy-with-Google-Cloud-to-Drive-Digital-Growth-and-Personalization/default.aspx)
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED — Albertsons Cos.: first retailer to debut the Conversational Commerce agent, shipped as the 'Ask AI' tool in the search bar of Albertsons banner apps (Albertsons, Safeway, Shaw's and other banners); VERIFIED — Kroger: Gemini Enterprise for CX nationwide with a shopping assistant, announced 2026-01-11
- **Pricing:** Not published — 'contact sales'; consumed via the Vertex AI console. Do not quote a figure.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Vertical-agnostic, but grocery is the proof point (Albertsons, Kroger). Would work for fashion too.
- **Demo/trial:** Yes, two ways: (a) anyone can try Albertsons/Safeway 'Ask AI' live in the search bar on the public sites/apps — the best free competitive benchmark available to LatentView; (b) the agent is GA in the Vertex AI console for any GCP account.
- **Vs CAS:** This is the commoditisation threat, and it is the most important slide in the deck. Google now sells, off the shelf, the exact layer CAS is: an LLM conversational/context-aware discovery agent that grounds in the retailer's existing catalog and coexists with the incumbent keyword search. Two of CAS's three named US target accounts (Kroger, Albertsons) have already bought Google AI for this job. THE BENCHMARK NUMBER, WITH ITS CORRECT SCOPE: Albertsons' CEO said on an earnings call, 'Our Ask AI search capability is already delivering a 10% increase in basket size for those customers using it, signaling a meaningful upside as adoption scales.' IMPORTANT CORRECTION — this is a +10% basket for USERS OF THE FEATURE, not a +10% chain-wide basket, and it is an earnings-call statement, not an audited A/B result. Present it with that scoping or a sharp CFO will take the deck apart (https://www.cxtoday.com/ai-automation-in-cx/albertsons-uses-google-ai-search-to-drive-10-bigger-grocery-baskets/). What CAS has that Google does not publicly claim: per-result reason strings, typed hard-constraint extraction with score capping, and an auditable relevance-gated sponsored layer. What Google has that CAS lacks: merchandiser boost/bury/restrict console, cross-device session memory, and enterprise SLAs.

#### OpenAI (as the model vendor behind the Albertsons AI Shopping Assistant) — https://www.albertsonscompanies.com/newsroom/press-releases/news-details/2025/Albertsons-Companies-Accelerates-Digital-Transformation-with-the-Albertsons-AI-Shopping-Assistant-Redefining-the-Grocery-Shopping-Experience/default.aspx

- **What it does:** VERIFIED (2025-12-03): Albertsons launched the Albertsons AI Shopping Assistant, powered by OpenAI models — a SEPARATE deployment from the Google-powered 'Ask AI' search-bar agent. Broken out as its own entry here because the original draft folded it into a takeaway and understated it: Albertsons is running TWO different AI assistant vendors, which tells you the account is still shopping and that a consultancy-delivered layer is not obviously excluded.
- **Key features:** VERIFIED capability list, quoted exactly. 'Rapid Restock: Quickly reorder weekly essentials and frequent purchases.' — the original draft MISSED this, and it is the single most grocery-native capability on the list; 'Plan Meals: Generate a practical weekly meal plan and shopping list with de-duplicated ingredients added to cart.' — cross-recipe ingredient de-duplication, which CAS does not do; 'Shop Lists: Type or upload an image of a written grocery list, and the assistant will find personalized products for everything on the list.'; 'Fridge Cleaner: Discover recipes using what's already in the fridge and pantry, saving money and reducing food waste.' — pantry awareness, which CAS does not have; 'Shop Recipe: Instantly import an online recipe or image and add ingredients to cart.'; 'Event-Ready: Choose a theme, or let the assistant use the next upcoming holiday, and see curated product ideas with offers.' — also missed in the original draft; Dietary handling is conversational rather than a named feature: the assistant accepts context such as one household member being vegetarian during two-way dialogue
- **Named clients:** Albertsons Companies (Albertsons, Safeway, Vons, Jewel-Osco and other banners)
- **Pricing:** Not published; model/API consumption plus internal build cost. Do not quote a figure.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Vertical-agnostic model vendor; this specific deployment is grocery.
- **Demo/trial:** Yes — publicly usable in the Albertsons/Safeway apps. Free, and the closest live analogue to what CAS's grocery demo should look like.
- **Vs CAS:** The clearest statement of what a Tier-1 US grocer actually buys, feature by feature. Score CAS honestly against these six: Rapid Restock (CAS: none — no purchase history), Plan Meals with de-duplicated ingredients (CAS: partial — has ingredient cards, no de-dup across recipes), Shop Lists from an image (CAS: none — no OCR/vision), Fridge Cleaner (CAS: none — no pantry model), Shop Recipe (CAS: partial — generates from a query, cannot import a URL or image), Event-Ready (CAS: none). CAS covers roughly one and a half of six. That is the honest gap chart, and it is far more persuasive to a client lead than a NDCG number.

#### Empathy.co — https://empathy.co/customers/

- **What it does:** MAJOR OMISSION FROM THE ORIGINAL DRAFT — ADDED. Privacy-first commerce search and discovery platform (Empathy Platform SaaS, plus 'Motive'), positioned on 'Ethical & Responsible AI Search & Discovery' and explicitly on not tracking shoppers: 'We design beautiful, expressive Search and Discovery experiences that deliver relevant results but without tracking, spying or sharing customer data.' It offers a composable/private-cloud deployment model.
- **Key features:** AI search and discovery delivered as a composable platform ('Leverage Empathy.AI's Private Cloud and Empathy Platform composable technology with a variety of options'); Privacy-by-design / no-tracking positioning — a genuine differentiator in EU grocery where GDPR scrutiny of shopper data is high; Stated AI governance framing: 'AI in line to a company's governance'; CAVEAT: Empathy.co's public product page does NOT enumerate recommendations, personalization, merchandising controls or LLM features in detail; do not assert specific feature parity without a demo
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED on Empathy.co's own customers page: Kroger, Carrefour, BSH, Casa del Libro, Connect, Tous, Vodafone; GROCERY RELEVANCE: Kroger and Carrefour are both named. This directly complicates the original draft's framing that Kroger's search is purely an in-house 84.51° affair.; CAVEAT: these are logos on the vendor's own customers page. I could not find a Kroger or Carrefour case study describing scope, so do NOT claim Empathy.co powers Kroger's main site search. Claim only that Empathy.co lists Kroger as a customer.
- **Pricing:** Not published — enterprise sales.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Both. Grocery (Kroger, Carrefour) and general retail (Tous). Relevant to both CAS verticals, and the strongest European reference of any pure search vendor found.
- **Demo/trial:** Public docs at docs.empathy.co; open-source front-end components (Empathy X components) exist on GitHub. No self-serve trial found.
- **Vs CAS:** THIS IS THE ENTRY THE ORIGINAL REPORT MOST NEEDED. Three consequences: (1) a search-specialist vendor already lists KROGER — CAS's #1 named target — as a customer, so the 'Kroger has no external search vendor' assumption in the draft is wrong; (2) their privacy/no-tracking angle is a real competitive axis CAS has not considered, and it is one CAS could credibly beat them on differently (CAS's reranker reasons make ranking explainable, which is the other half of the trust story); (3) Carrefour is a European grocery reference, which matters if LatentView pitches Tesco/Sainsbury's/Ahold in Europe. Before any Kroger conversation, someone should establish what Empathy.co actually powers there.

#### Ocado Technology — Ocado Smart Platform (OSP) — https://www.ocadogroup.com/about-us/osp-partners

- **What it does:** OMITTED FROM THE ORIGINAL DRAFT — ADDED. OSP is the end-to-end online-grocery platform (automated fulfilment plus 'a configurable ecommerce solution that acts as a growth engine for your online grocery business') licensed to major grocers worldwide. VERIFIED scale: OSP 'powers online grocery for 13 leading grocery partners across the globe, totaling 25 live sites and 1,000+ stores'. It is not primarily a search vendor, but it owns the storefront layer at several of CAS's target-tier accounts, which makes it a gatekeeper and a potential channel.
- **Key features:** Configurable e-commerce storefront for online grocery, bundled with automated fulfilment (CFCs) and in-store picking; VERIFIED scale: 13 grocery partners, 25 live sites, 1,000+ stores; CAVEAT: the OSP pages I fetched do NOT detail search, ranking, personalization or recommendation capabilities. Do not assume OSP has weak search — assume it is unpublished, and treat 'what search does OSP actually ship?' as a discovery question for any pitch into an OSP retailer.
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED on Ocado Group's own OSP partners page: Aeon, Alcampo, Asda, Auchan Retail Poland, Bon Preu, Coles, Groupe Casino, ICA, Kroger, Lotte, Morrisons, Ocado (Ocado Retail), Panda, Sobeys
- **Pricing:** Not published — long-term platform partnership contracts with upfront fees and ongoing fees typically tied to capacity/volume. Do not quote figures.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery-only, and specifically online-grocery-at-scale.
- **Demo/trial:** No sandbox. Ocado.com (UK) is the public reference implementation and is free to use as a benchmark.
- **Vs CAS:** Strategically important in two ways the draft missed. (1) KROGER IS AN OCADO PARTNER — so the Kroger stack is not just 84.51° plus Google; there is a platform layer with its own roadmap and its own integration constraints. Any 'drop CAS into Kroger's existing search' pitch has to answer where it sits relative to OSP. (2) OSP is a distribution channel: 13 grocers across the UK, Spain, Sweden, Poland, France, Australia, Canada, Japan, Korea and Saudi Arabia run their online grocery on it. A search/discovery layer that integrates cleanly with OSP addresses a dozen Tier-1 grocers at once, which is a far more capital-efficient GTM than direct enterprise sales. Add OSP (alongside Wynshop, Mercatus and Swiftly) to the partner-channel list.

#### Algolia — Intelligent Grocery Solution — https://www.algolia.com/about/news/algolia-unveils-intelligent-grocery-solution

- **What it does:** VERIFIED, with a date correction: published 2025-09-24 (the original draft said '2025-09-22/26'). A pre-packaged grocery vertical solution explicitly targeting substitutions, out-of-stock and repetitive reordering, layered on Algolia's horizontal search platform.
- **Key features:** VERIFIED capability pillars, as stated: real-time search with natural language interpretation; personalized recommendations; inventory-aware discovery; retail media and own-brand promotion; reduced operational strain through automation; VERIFIED vendor statistics from the release: '138 million online grocery users in the US (2025)'; digital grocery sales growing '5.2x faster than in-store'; '80% of carts are reorders'; Algolia scale figures '1.75 trillion searches annually', '18,000+ businesses', '500,000+ developers'; CORRECTION — the '39% boost in conversion rates' for a UK customer from personalised out-of-stock recommendations is NOT on Algolia's own release; it comes from FoodNavigator trade coverage (https://www.foodnavigator.com/Article/2026/02/18/ai-is-reshaping-grocery-search-merchandising-and-loyalty/). Attribute it to the trade article, not to Algolia, or drop it.; CORRECTION — 'Co-op' as an Algolia grocery partnership could NOT be verified on Algolia's own release. Remove it from any client-facing deck.
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED with exact quotes — Auto Mercado (Costa Rica), Felipe Alonso Valverde, Head of Ecommerce: 'Not only does Algolia pay for itself… it makes it easier to promote our private label brands and personalized impulse products.' Release also states a 50%+ conversion lift and online sales at double the industry average.; VERIFIED with exact quote — MILKRUN, Venky Kumar, Chief Product and Technology Officer: 'Around 65% of our customers use search for their shopping...every millisecond matters, and Algolia's average response time of ~20 milliseconds delivers a great customer experience.'; KILLED: Co-op — unverifiable on the source, do not use.
- **Pricing:** PUBLISHED — rare in this segment, and VERIFIED directly on https://www.algolia.com/pricing. Free: 10K search requests/month, 50K records, 5K recommendation requests/month, 5K crawls/month, no card. Grow: 10K search requests/month included then $0.50 per additional 1K search requests; 100K records included then $0.40 per additional 1K records. Grow Plus: same records pricing, but $1.75 per additional 1K search requests. Elevate: enterprise, annual contract required, volume-based discounts, custom search-request and record allowances, custom quote.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Both. Algolia is horizontal but has now built an explicit grocery SKU, and is equally established in fashion. A direct competitor in BOTH of CAS's target verticals.
- **Demo/trial:** Yes — a genuine free tier (10K searches/month, no credit card) plus public docs and demo sites. The easiest vendor for LatentView to bench CAS against head-to-head, and the only one where you can build a real comparison over a weekend.
- **Vs CAS:** The closest productised analogue to CAS's pitch, and the one that most directly undercuts it on price and time-to-value — a retailer can start free and be live in days. Overlaps: NL query understanding, dietary carts, recommendations, retail media, private-label promotion. Does things CAS does NOT: inventory-aware ranking, OOS substitution, reorder personalisation, own-brand boosting controls, ~20ms latency, published pricing. Does NOT publicly do: multi-intent scatter-gather expansion, LLM reranking with per-item reasons, or a recipe->ingredient-card experience. The '80% of carts are reorders' claim is the single most strategically damaging stat in this report for CAS — if true, grocery search is a repeat-purchase problem and CAS's no-session-personalization gap is disqualifying, not cosmetic.

#### Constructor — https://constructor.com/customers

- **What it does:** AI-native search, browse, recommendations and merchandising with an explicit grocery vertical page. Positions on optimising business KPIs (revenue, AOV, LTV, margin) directly in the ranking rather than relevance alone.
- **Key features:** Intent-nuanced grocery search (distinguishing e.g. 'pepper' vs 'peppers'); Dynamic ranking against time-sensitive trends and inventory; Attribute enrichment with automatic normalisation of quantity/volume data — a real grocery pain (pack sizes); Real-time stock availability display with substitute suggestions; Personalised landing pages for frequently repurchased items; mission-driven collections; AI Shopping Agent for conversational guidance; Retail Media module for sponsored placement; Local store / fulfilment-centre inventory alignment
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED on constructor.com/customers with results attached: Flaschenpost '4% search CVR'; Target Australia '+A$13M in search revenue'; Grove Collaborative '20.07X in overall ROI'; Belk '$35M in revenue'; Petco '+13% in site conversions'; White Stuff '+21% in search conversion rate'; Cromwell '21X ROI upon go-live'; AG Jeans '+16% conversion lift'; Chubbies '+10% add-to-cart conversions'; Home24 'Double-digit lift in search conversion rate'; Monica Vinader '5% conversion lift'; Saatchi Art '+47.29% RPV'; Lumens '+6% search RPV'; Fisheries Supply '+15% in revenue per user'; Finnish Design Shop '+5.9% RPV'; VERIFIED logos also on the page: Sephora, Under Armour, Gap, REI, Tractor Supply, QVC, Bath & Body Works, Foot Locker, BJ's, Bombas, Crate and Barrel, Journeys, Paperless Post, Plow & Hearth, Rothy's, Serena & Lily, West Elm, Williams Sonoma, Bonobos, Ashley; CORRECTION TO THE ORIGINAL DRAFT: it claimed flaschenpost was 'the only clearly-grocery named account'. BJ'S WHOLESALE CLUB is also on the customer list — a US warehouse club with a large grocery business. Grove Collaborative is household/CPG. So Constructor's food-adjacent references are flaschenpost + BJ's + Grove, not one.
- **Pricing:** Not published — demo/contact only. Corporate growth claims (82% customer growth in FY26, 322 billion shopping interactions) come from a PRNewswire release and are vendor-stated.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Both, and notably strong in fashion (Gap, Under Armour, Foot Locker, Belk, Bonobos, White Stuff). Grocery logos remain thin relative to the vertical page's ambition.
- **Demo/trial:** Demo request only; no self-serve trial found. Public case-study library at https://constructor.com/customers.
- **Vs CAS:** Direct competitor in both CAS verticals. The genuinely differentiating idea CAS should steal: Constructor ranks on MARGIN and AOV, not just relevance — grocery merchandisers buy that, and CAS already has the mechanism (it blends a rating signal at weight 0.05; a private-label/margin term is the same code path). Also does quantity/volume attribute normalisation, which CAS does not — grocery catalogs are full of '2 x 500g' vs '1kg' and this breaks recipe quantity handling badly. Weak on the recipe/meal side: no shoppable-recipe capability found.

#### Wynshop + Halla ('Taste Intelligence') — https://wynshop.com/wynshop-acquires-grocery-ai-pioneer-halla/

- **What it does:** VERIFIED (2024-03-11): Wynshop acquired Halla, described in trade coverage as 'the leader in AI-driven personalization solutions specifically for grocery e-commerce', for an undisclosed amount; Halla's founders joined Wynshop. Halla launched in 2020 and its technology 'delivers recommendations, substitutions, and searches for thousands of grocery e-commerce storefronts', drawing on 'more than 100 billion data points to predict shoppers' needs'. Halla shipped three products: Halla Search, Halla Substitute and Halla Recommend.
- **Key features:** Halla Search — grocery-specific relevance; Halla Substitute — out-of-stock replacement built to avoid category-collision errors (the cited example is distinguishing 'bananas' from 'banana bread'); Halla Recommend — basket/next-item recommendations; Wynshop DXP for mid-size grocers: storefront, fulfilment, personalization; Trade coverage notes Wynshop's rationale was Halla's strength at predicting purchases from purchase history, with recommendations that 'helped significantly increase overall basket sizes'; Cited justification for substitution quality: bad substitutions can drive up to 20% of grocery shoppers to competitors (McKinsey data cited in the Wynshop release)
- **Named clients:** Wynshop customer base per trade coverage: Cub Foods, Brookshire Grocery Co., United Supermarkets (Albertsons-owned) and Wakefern Food Corp (https://www.grocerydive.com/news/wynshop-acquires-halla-e-commerce-grocery-personalization/709882/); CAVEAT: these are WYNSHOP platform customers. Halla usage at each is NOT confirmed. Do not claim 'Halla powers Wakefern's substitutions'.
- **Pricing:** Not published.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery-pure. Zero fashion relevance. The clearest proof that 'grocery search is its own discipline' is a fundable, exitable thesis.
- **Demo/trial:** No public demo or trial found; enterprise sales motion only.
- **Vs CAS:** Strategically the most instructive competitor: a company that built its entire product around the three grocery discovery jobs — search, substitute, recommend — and got acquired for it. CAS covers 'search' and partially 'recommend' (cross-sell/upsell) and has NOTHING for 'substitute'. The 20%-of-shoppers-lost figure is the sharpest available argument that substitution is a P0 grocery feature CAS is missing. Also note the failure mode Halla markets fixing — 'bananas' vs 'banana bread' — is exactly the error pure embedding cosine similarity makes, and CAS's eval set contains no such case. Add it.

#### Chicory — https://chicory.co/

- **What it does:** Contextual/shoppable-recipe advertising network. Embeds a 'Get Ingredients' capability in publisher recipe content, then routes the ingredient list into a chosen retailer's cart. Monetises as retail media (CPG-funded) rather than as retailer software.
- **Key features:** VERIFIED on chicory.co: '150M Monthly Unique Shoppers', '7,000+ Food & Grocery Publishers', '70+ Retailer Integrations'; Positioned to reach shoppers while 'looking up recipes, planning meals, and creating shopping lists' in brand-safe contextual environments; Partner-quoted framing: 'Their shoppable technology...seamlessly drives add-to-carts via highly-relevant recipe content.'; CORRECTION — the original draft's campaign metrics (8.96X iROAS, $600K incremental sales, 62% of purchases from new & lapsed shoppers) were NOT visible on the homepage I fetched. They may sit in gated case studies. Do not put them in a client deck without a direct link to the page they appear on.; STRUCTURAL LIMIT (per SideChef's competitor comparison, vendor-authored): Chicory 'does not have its own digital cart', which limits control over the ingredient experience
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED on chicory.co: Giant Eagle, General Mills, Primal Kitchen, Alaska Seafood Marketing Institute; VERIFIED via trade coverage: Ahold Delhaize USA partnered with Chicory in 2025 as the first of three content-to-cart vendors (https://theshelbyreport.com/2026/07/29/ahold-delhaize-usa-adds-shoppable-recipes-product-pages/)
- **Pricing:** Not published — CPG-media-funded model (brands pay; retailers integrate). INFERENCE: no licence fee to the retailer, which is why it spreads fast. Flag as inference.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery/CPG only.
- **Demo/trial:** No sandbox. Free de facto demo: any Chicory-enabled recipe blog shows the flow live.
- **Vs CAS:** Directly contests the recipe territory CAS built, and does it for free to the retailer. THE COMMERCIAL POINT THAT MATTERS: a retailer asking 'why would I pay for recipe-to-cart?' already has a zero-cost answer funded by CPG budgets. What Chicory does NOT do is give the SHOPPER a choice of brand alternatives per ingredient — its ingredient-level brand influence is sold to advertisers. That is the honest wedge for CAS's feature, and it is also an uncomfortable one to pitch to a retail-media team.

#### Northfork — https://northfork.ai/

- **What it does:** Shoppable-recipe infrastructure. VERIFIED positioning, quoted exactly: 'Translate recipes and products into in-stock retailer items with intelligent, proprietary AI that learns from real shopping behavior.' Marketed as an 'AI Matching Engine' within a 'Recipe Shopping Foundation' product, with real-time connections to retailer product, price and inventory systems.
- **Key features:** AI ingredient->product matching engine trained on real shopping behaviour; Real-time product, pricing and inventory integration; Multi-retailer cart handoff; Pantry-item support — singled out by SideChef's comparison: Northfork 'includes pantry item support, which is critical for long-tail grocery needs'; VERIFIED scale figures on northfork.ai: '25+ markets', '20,000+ retailers', '1,000,000+ products'; VERIFIED ABSENCE: the site does not describe showing multiple brand alternatives per ingredient. This is a load-bearing negative finding for CAS's differentiation claim and it held up under direct fetch.
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED on northfork.ai: Walmart, Sainsbury's, Unilever's Vegetarian Butcher, Tasty, Mindshare, Maybelline, Today.com; Sainsbury's is the only UK grocer found with a named shoppable-recipe vendor — directly relevant if LatentView pitches a UK grocer
- **Pricing:** Not published; API integration required, enterprise sales.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery/food only.
- **Demo/trial:** No public sandbox. Walmart's shoppable recipe pages are a live public example, free to inspect.
- **Vs CAS:** The most technically credible recipe-to-cart competitor and a Sainsbury's incumbent. Its public materials describe ingredient->single-best-product matching; I confirmed by direct fetch that it does NOT describe presenting multiple brand alternatives to the shopper. So Northfork validates the demand for CAS's recipe feature while leaving the specific 'brand alternatives carousel' UX genuinely open — this is the strongest single support for CAS's differentiation claim in the whole report, and it is a negative finding, which is the weakest kind of evidence. Say so.

#### Samsung Food (formerly Whisk) — https://www.grocerydive.com/news/kroger-whisk-team-up-on-shoppable-lists/587247/

- **What it does:** VERIFIED (article published 2020-10-19, soft-launched April 2020): consumer recipe/meal-planning app plus a B2B shoppable-list API. Whisk uses 'artificial intelligence and natural language processing technology' to verify ingredient availability at local Kroger stores; its API 'ensures accurate inventory and product matching' while considering 'different terms to describe the same products'.
- **Key features:** VERIFIED quantity reasoning, quoted from Whisk's Nick Holzherr: 'If you need for a recipe three tomatoes, you don't want a box of 22 tomatoes being delivered.' — CAS has no unit/quantity handling at all; Ingredient->product matching considering availability, naming variation and the user's purchase history; DOWNGRADED: the original draft's claim that Whisk 'greys out and unlists likely pantry staples (olive oil, salt, pepper)' was NOT confirmed in the source fetched this pass. It is plausible and consistent with the product, but treat it as unverified — either find a live screenshot from the Samsung Food app or drop it.; Serving-size scaling, cross-recipe ingredient merging and integrated nutrition data (product-page claims, not re-verified this pass)
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED — Kroger: shoppable lists available to users in 35 states, soft-launched April 2020, announced October 2020; Walmart, Amazon Fresh, Ahold Delhaize and Instacart are cited as integrated partners in trade coverage; Samsung Family Hub refrigerators via the Samsung acquisition
- **Pricing:** Not published for the B2B API. Consumer app is free.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery/food only.
- **Demo/trial:** Yes — samsungfood.com and the Samsung Food app are free; you can run the full recipe->matched-products->cart flow yourself against a real retailer. Best free hands-on benchmark for CAS's recipe feature.
- **Vs CAS:** The most direct functional comparison to CAS's 'shopping list' experience, and it has been live at Kroger since 2020 — the target buyer has had recipe-to-cart for over five years. Its quantity reasoning is a capability CAS lacks outright. Like every other vendor checked, it auto-selects ONE product per ingredient (personalised to what you usually buy) rather than presenting brand alternatives up front. NOTE FOR THE ADS SLIDE: SideChef's comparison alleges Samsung Food 'does not have a clear distinction between sponsored and non-sponsored items' — if true, that is a direct argument FOR CAS's always-labelled, never-blended ad policy, though it is a vendor-authored allegation about a competitor.

#### SideChef — https://www.sidechef.com/business/recipe-platform/shoppable-recipe-button-comparison

- **What it does:** Recipe platform with a shoppable-recipe button product. Publishes the most useful public head-to-head comparison of the whole shoppable-recipe category, covering eight vendors: Adimo, Chicory, Samsung Food, Pear Commerce, Northfork, SmartCommerce (Click2Cart), Destini and SideChef itself.
- **Key features:** SELF-AUTHORED CLAIM, quoted exactly and verified on the page: SideChef is 'currently the only platform that supports a true multi-button interface, alcohol-specific product routing, quantity precision, and intelligent product substitutions', and 'non-sponsored product availability and recipe integrity are maintained through smart substitution logic, real-time quantity control, and pantry recognition'; Its verified competitive assertions, all vendor-authored and all to be cited as positioning rather than fact: Pear Commerce — 'users cannot swap sponsored products even if the product is out of stock'; Destini — 'non-sponsored product matching is minimal' and 'does not support ingredient swaps, pantry recognition, or quantity adjustment'; Samsung Food — 'does not have a clear distinction between sponsored and non-sponsored items'; Adimo — support for quantity matching or ingredient swaps 'is minimal or not visible'; Chicory — 'does not have its own digital cart'; SmartCommerce — limited ability to 'suggest alternative products or handle non-sponsored items'; Northfork — 'includes pantry item support, which is critical for long-tail grocery needs'
- **Named clients:** CORRECTION TO THE ORIGINAL DRAFT, which said 'none verified'. SideChef DOES name clients on this page: Ocean Spray, Bertolli, Del Monte.; These are CPG brands, not grocery retailers. No grocery-retailer logo was found.
- **Pricing:** Not published.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery/food only.
- **Demo/trial:** Consumer SideChef app is free; B2B is contact-sales.
- **Vs CAS:** This is the closest anyone comes to claiming CAS's feature — 'intelligent product substitutions' per ingredient. But: it is a self-authored comparison table, its own named clients are CPG brands rather than retailers, and 'substitution' means swap-when-unavailable, not PRESENTING three ranked brand alternatives up front. There is no public demo of the mechanism. Use this page as the single best public map of the recipe-to-cart category on CAS's competitive slide, cite its STRUCTURE, and explicitly flag its bias — a client lead who notices you quoted a vendor's own scorecard about its competitors without flagging it will discount the whole deck.

#### Pear Commerce — https://theshelbyreport.com/2026/07/29/ahold-delhaize-usa-adds-shoppable-recipes-product-pages/

- **What it does:** VERIFIED (2026-07-29): retailer-connected commerce layer turning CPG content — recipes, brand product pages, store locators — into add-to-cart at the grocer. Ahold Delhaize USA partnered with Pear Commerce to enable shoppable recipes and product pages across its five omnichannel banners. NOTE: the GlobeNewswire release URL cited in the original draft returned a connection error on re-fetch; use the Shelby Report URL above instead, which I did fetch successfully.
- **Key features:** Shoppable recipes: browse a meal, add ingredients straight to the retailer cart; Shoppable product pages and store-locator tools; Dynamic inventory adjustment and two-click add-to-cart (per SideChef's vendor-authored comparison, not independently verified); VERIFIED quote from the launch, Keith Nicks, Chief Commercial and Digital Officer, Ahold Delhaize USA: 'By creating more intuitive connections between inspiration and purchase, we're helping shoppers move from discovery to cart with fewer steps'
- **Named clients:** VERIFIED — Ahold Delhaize USA, across all five banners: Food Lion, The Giant Co., Giant Food, Hannaford and Stop & Shop, from July 2026
- **Pricing:** Not published. INFERENCE: CPG-funded like Chicory. Flag as inference.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery/CPG only.
- **Demo/trial:** None found.
- **Vs CAS:** Evidence — now verified from a single article naming all three — that Ahold Delhaize solved recipe-to-cart by BUYING three separate point vendors: Chicory (2025), SmartCommerce Click2Cart (April 2026) and Pear Commerce (July 2026). That is the buying pattern CAS must fit into or displace: grocers treat content-to-cart as a cheap, stackable bolt-on, not a search-platform decision. If CAS's recipe feature is pitched as a reason to buy a search platform, this account will say no three times over.

#### SmartCommerce (Click2Cart) — https://progressivegrocer.com/ahold-delhaize-usa-rolls-out-smartcommerces-click2cart-capability

- **What it does:** Single-click 'add these products to my cart at my retailer' technology embedded in brand and publisher content. Sourced from trade coverage and SideChef's comparison; I did not fetch SmartCommerce's own site this pass.
- **Key features:** One-click multi-item add-to-cart at the retailer; Dual button placement; Per SideChef's vendor-authored comparison: limited ability to 'suggest alternative products or handle non-sponsored items'
- **Named clients:** Ahold Delhaize USA — Click2Cart rolled out April 2026, corroborated by the Shelby Report piece which names SmartCommerce as the second of Ahold's three content-to-cart vendors
- **Pricing:** Not published.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery/CPG only.
- **Demo/trial:** None found.
- **Vs CAS:** Low technical overlap — a cart-handoff mechanism, not search. Included because it is the third recipe/content-to-cart vendor inside one CAS target account, reinforcing that this territory is crowded, cheap and already solved to the buyer's satisfaction.

#### Criteo (retail media inside AI conversational search) — https://www.grocerydive.com/news/albertsons-media-collective-criteo-ai-search-brand-ads-ecommerce-omnichannel/823557/

- **What it does:** VERIFIED (2026-06-24): Albertsons Media Collective partnered with Criteo to integrate sponsored product placement into Albertsons' AI-powered conversational search, enabling branded ads to appear in search product carousels. 'Select sponsored products now appear within Albertsons' search product carousels relevant to shoppers' search queries, helping brands connect with consumers as they plan meals, discover products, and build shopping baskets.'
- **Key features:** Sponsored product placement INSIDE AI/conversational search carousels, not just classic PLA slots; Relevance-conditioned: ads surface when relevant to the shopper's query; VERIFIED quote, Jill Pavlovich, SVP of Digital Customer Experience, Albertsons: 'This integration is about creating retail media that helps customers along their shopping journey, showing up in ways that are useful and additive to their experience, while giving advertisers a new path to engage closer to the moment of purchase.'; NOT DISCLOSED in the article: whether sponsored items are visually labelled or separated from organic results, and no performance metrics. Do not assert either way.
- **Named clients:** Albertsons Media Collective (Albertsons, Safeway, Vons, Jewel-Osco and other banners)
- **Pricing:** Not published (retail-media platform fees / revenue share).
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Both, but this specific launch is grocery.
- **Demo/trial:** Observable free: use Albertsons/Safeway 'Ask AI' publicly and inspect what appears in the carousels. Worth someone actually doing this before the ads slide is written.
- **Vs CAS:** The single most important finding for CAS's sponsored-ads design. The market is moving to INJECT paid product into the AI answer surface — the opposite of CAS's decision (relevance-gated, always labelled, NEVER blended into organic, bid never exposed). CAS's stance is more defensible on auditability and on the project's own safety rules, but it is commercially weaker than what Albertsons just bought. RECOMMENDATION: position CAS's ad separation as a CONFIGURABLE GOVERNANCE POLICY — with the auditable relevance gate as the differentiator — rather than a hard architectural constraint. Otherwise the retail-media P&L owner, who controls the budget, will read CAS as revenue-suppressing and veto it.

#### 84.51° / Kroger Precision Marketing — Boosted Products in Search + Dynamic Positioner — https://ir.kroger.com/news/news-details/2018/Kroger-Precision-Marketing-Launches-Boosted-Products-in-Search/default.aspx

- **What it does:** VERIFIED (2018-06-04): Kroger's in-house data-science arm launched Boosted Products in Search, letting brands promote products through PLAs by 'balancing cost-per-click bid pricing, customer relevancy, and in-flight performance optimization'.
- **Key features:** VERIFIED quote — the ML quality assessment 'occurs in real-time, accounting for store-level inventory, promotions, shifting consumer preferences, and seasonality' and works to 'power the search results algorithm and influence the priority ranking of boosted products for customers'. This is the closest published analogue anywhere to CAS's SPONSORED_REL_RATIO relevance gate — and it shipped in 2018.; Featured products appear 'in high visibility slots for the customer' with real-time performance reporting for brands; 'Dynamic Positioner': a proprietary two-phase system that offline decouples positional effects from item-relevancy effects using historical clickstream and search-result data, then online searches over auction-returned ad positions to place ads optimally (https://www.8451.com/knowledge-hub/technology/creating-a-better-search-experience-with-dynamic-and-personalized-sponsored/); 84.51° reports working with 62M+ US households and 1,500+ CPG/agency partners (vendor-stated)
- **Named clients:** Kroger and all its banners (84.51° is Kroger-owned)
- **Pricing:** CPC auction for advertisers; not applicable as a software licence.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery-only, captive to Kroger.
- **Demo/trial:** No. Observable on kroger.com search results pages.
- **Vs CAS:** CAS's relevance-gated ad idea is NOT novel — Kroger has shipped a relevance-and-inventory-aware ad quality score since June 2018, and 84.51° has published a position-optimisation model on top of it. Two consequences: (1) do not pitch relevance-gated ads as an innovation, least of all to Kroger; (2) 84.51° is a large in-house data-science organisation, which makes Kroger a hard analytics-consulting sale — CAS's realistic entry point there is the LLM reranking and reason-generation layer, not ad ranking. The Dynamic Positioner work is also a methodological warning: CAS's eval has no notion of position bias, so any placement or ad-effect claim CAS makes today is unmeasurable.

#### Mercatus (DXPro + AisleOne) — https://www.mercatus.com/platform-overview/

- **What it does:** Grocery-purpose-built digital experience platform for regional grocers ('5 to 500 stores') covering storefront, order management, fulfilment, loyalty and retail media. AisleOne Intelligence is its intent-based personalization engine spanning search results, product recommendations and promotions.
- **Key features:** AisleOne: real-time shopping behaviour driving search results, recommendations and promotions; Commerce engine explicitly built to handle grocery basket, substitution and order-flow complexity; Retail media via proprietary widget technology for brand-sponsored campaigns on first-party channels; eMarketing automation with journey mapping; Two tiers: DX Plus and DXP
- **Named clients:** NONE VERIFIED. No customer logos on the platform overview page, and I could not confirm specific grocery logos elsewhere. Do NOT assert Mercatus references in a client-facing deck.
- **Pricing:** Not published; two tiers exist but no costs disclosed.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery-only. No fashion.
- **Demo/trial:** Demo on request via mercatus.com/get-demo/. No self-serve trial.
- **Vs CAS:** Relevant as a CHANNEL rather than a competitor. Mercatus is a platform CAS's search layer could plug into for the regional-grocer tier, and it is thin on published search IP — AisleOne is described only in general terms, with no metrics and no named customers. If CAS wants a grocery beachhead below the Kroger/Albertsons tier, platform partnerships (Mercatus, Wynshop, Swiftly, Ocado OSP, Local Express) are a faster route than direct enterprise sales.

#### Swiftly — https://www.swiftly.com/news/key-food-stores-co-operative-inc-partners-with-swiftly-to-modernize-digital-shopper-experience-and-unlock-retail-media-growth

- **What it does:** App/web plus retail media platform aimed at independent and regional grocers and wholesalers — digital circular, shopper engagement, retail media monetisation. Sourced from press releases; product pages not fetched this pass.
- **Key features:** App and web platform with integrated retail media network build-out; SmartCircular — AI-powered digital circular converting weekly circular PDFs into an interactive, measurable shopper experience; Alcohol Cashback; Programmatic DOOH via a Vistar Media partnership
- **Named clients:** Key Food Stores Co-Operative (460+ supermarkets across 8 states); Merchants Distributors / MDI (wholesale); UNFI (retail media network launch); Alliance Retail Group; Webstop; CAVEAT: these come from Swiftly's own press releases; treat as vendor-stated.
- **Pricing:** Not published. INFERENCE: platform fee plus retail-media revenue share.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery-only, independent/regional tier.
- **Demo/trial:** None found publicly.
- **Vs CAS:** Not a search competitor — Swiftly is circular, loyalty and retail media. Its value to LatentView is as a distribution partner for the long tail of US grocers (Key Food, UNFI, MDI, Alliance) that will never commission a bespoke consultancy build. If LatentView needs grocery references fast, embedding CAS behind a platform like Swiftly, Mercatus or Wynshop is more realistic than a Kroger direct sale.
