#### Firework (AVA) — https://www.grocerydive.com/news/the-fresh-market-generative-artificial-intelligence-video-commerce-firework/649678/

- **What it does:** Video commerce platform; AVA is its AI 'digital human' shopping assistant, answering product questions and demoing products. Sourced from trade coverage; firework.com product pages not fetched this pass.
- **Key features:** Shoppable short-form video embedded on retailer sites; AVA virtual assistant answering viewer questions on shoppable videos in real time; Video-commerce retail media network angle
- **Named clients:** The Fresh Market — piloting AI live-shopping on its site and video-commerce retail media network; Lowe's cited in Firework's own material (vendor-stated, non-grocery)
- **Pricing:** Not published.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Both; grocery presence limited to The Fresh Market pilot.
- **Demo/trial:** Observable on The Fresh Market's site.
- **Vs CAS:** Adjacent, low overlap. A different modality (video/avatar) for the same job-to-be-done: guided discovery. Relevant to CAS only as evidence that grocers are experimenting with conversational assistants at the specialty tier too, and as a possible presentation-layer partner rather than a competitor. Keep it off the main competitive slide.

#### NIQ (Label Insight + NIQ Brandbank) — product attribute data for dietary/allergen filtering — https://nielseniq.com/global/en/landing-page/label-insight/

- **What it does:** Product-attribute data supply: structured nutrition, ingredient, claim and allergen attributes syndicated into retailer search, filter and recommendation engines. NielsenIQ acquired Label Insight in 2021. Figures below are vendor-stated and were not re-fetched this pass — treat the specific counts as vendor marketing, and verify before quoting.
- **Key features:** Label Insight: attribute coverage claimed across the large majority of US food, pet and personal-care products, with large counts of nutrients, ingredients and product claims (vendor-stated); Derived allergen/intolerance views covering the major allergen classes (https://nielsenbrandbank.freshdesk.com/en/support/solutions/articles/75000118697-label-insight-view-allergens-intolerances-derived-); NIQ Brandbank: several hundred attributes per product, syndicated to third-party applications, explicitly to power dietary/lifestyle filtering and online search; Competitors in the same data layer: Pinto (SPINS), GreenChoice, Vestcom (https://about.greenchoicenow.com/food-data-providers-comparison). Also worth scoping: Syndigo and Salsify on the PIM/syndication side.
- **Named clients:** United Supermarkets (named on the NIQ Brandbank landing page). Treat other names as unverified.
- **Pricing:** Not published — content licensing agreements.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Grocery/CPG only.
- **Demo/trial:** None public.
- **Vs CAS:** The most under-appreciated dependency for CAS in grocery, and the one with legal exposure. CAS extracts dietary constraints FROM THE QUERY, but correctly enforcing 'no gluten', 'nut-free' or 'halal' requires trustworthy per-SKU attributes — a data problem, not a ranking problem, and one where being wrong about an allergen is a safety incident, not a relevance miss. Instacart solved it with VLM extraction over 1.3 billion data points; retailers solve it by licensing NIQ or SPINS. CAS must either (a) add a VLM/LLM attribute-extraction stage over the catalog with measured precision, or (b) explicitly scope allergen claims to licensed attribute data and never infer them. CAS's own safety rules point to (b). Whichever is chosen, it needs to be stated in the deck before a grocer's compliance team asks.

#### Tomoro AI (the consultancy behind Tesco's assistant) — https://www.retailgazette.co.uk/blog/2026/04/tesco-trials-ai-shopping-assistant-with-280000-colleagues-ahead-of-customer-rollout/

- **What it does:** VERIFIED (2026-04-09): Tesco's in-house app, data science and engineering teams built its AI shopping assistant in partnership with UK AI consultancy Tomoro AI. NOTE: the URL is the source article, not the vendor's own site — tomoro.ai was not fetched, so make no claims about Tomoro's other clients or capabilities.
- **Key features:** Co-delivery model: an AI consultancy embedded alongside the retailer's own app, data-science and engineering teams — precisely LatentView's intended motion; VERIFIED delivered scope: meal planning with conversational prompts for recipe suggestions based on preferences and dietary needs; adding ingredients to the basket in the Tesco app using shopping history and product preferences; reducing time, cost and friction; helping customers minimise food waste; Rolled out to 280,000 Tesco colleagues as a beta ahead of a customer launch; VERIFIED quote, Ken Murphy, Chief Executive: the assistant 'had the potential to reshape how customers shop with Tesco over time, particularly through more personalised support built into the app'
- **Named clients:** Tesco
- **Pricing:** Not published (consultancy engagement).
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** This engagement is grocery.
- **Demo/trial:** Not public — colleague beta only.
- **Vs CAS:** LatentView's direct competitor in DELIVERY MODEL rather than in product: an AI consultancy that won the flagship grocery assistant build at a Tier-1 European grocer. It is the strongest single proof that CAS's go-to-market — a consultancy selling a capability into the retailer's existing stack — is a live, winnable motion in grocery. It also pins down the scope grocers actually buy: meal planning + dietary-preference dialogue + history-aware basket building + food-waste reduction. CAS does one of those four well.

#### Bloomreach — CHECKED, NO VERIFIABLE GROCERY REFERENCE — https://www.bloomreach.com/en/customers

- **What it does:** Commerce search, merchandising and personalization platform with an agentic layer (Loomi). Included here as a negative finding so the client lead can see it was considered rather than overlooked.
- **Key features:** Commerce search and merchandising plus marketing automation/personalization; Loomi agent branding across the product line; Lists 'Food & Beverages' among its solution industries
- **Named clients:** NONE GROCERY VERIFIED. The customers page I fetched surfaced only one named case study — '260 Sample Sale Doubles CVR With Loomi Agent', a fashion/sample-sale retailer. No grocery or food retailer was named. Bloomreach's dedicated grocery URL (bloomreach.com/en/solutions/industry/grocery) returned HTTP 404.; DO NOT assert Bloomreach grocery references in a CAS deck.
- **Pricing:** Not published.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Established in fashion and general retail; grocery presence unverifiable from public pages.
- **Demo/trial:** Demo request only.
- **Vs CAS:** Include on the fashion competitive slide, not the grocery one. Same treatment applies to Coveo, Lucidworks and Klevu: all are credible commerce-search vendors, but I could not verify a named grocery retailer for any of them within this session's budget, so none should be asserted as grocery competitors. If the client lead wants them covered, that is a follow-up research task, not a claim to make now.

#### Lily AI — CHECKED AND EXCLUDED FROM THIS SEGMENT — https://www.lily.ai/

- **What it does:** Product-attribute enrichment for commerce search and ads: 'Lily Max uses goal-based AI agents to optimize product intelligence across paid, organic, onsite, and agentic commerce surfaces.' Included as an explicit exclusion because attribute enrichment is exactly the capability grocery search needs, so a reader will reasonably ask why it is not here.
- **Key features:** Catalog attribute enrichment feeding onsite search, paid and agentic commerce surfaces; Controlled tests measuring performance lift
- **Named clients:** Named on lily.ai with testimonials: M&S, Shiseido, Tapestry, Bombas. Logos also shown for Coach, NARS, Vuori, Fabletics, Beyond Yoga, J.Crew, HOKA, Tecovas, Foot Locker, Kate Spade, UGG, Drunk Elephant.; VERIFIED NEGATIVE: the site contains no mention of grocery, food or beverage verticals. It is positioned for apparel, footwear, beauty and luxury.
- **Pricing:** Not published.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Fashion and beauty only — verified. Zero grocery positioning.
- **Demo/trial:** Demo request only.
- **Vs CAS:** Move this vendor to the FASHION segment report, where it is a first-tier competitor on the attribute-enrichment axis. For grocery, the equivalent capability is bought from NIQ Label Insight, SPINS/Pinto or built in-house with VLMs as Instacart did. Noting the exclusion here prevents the fashion researcher and the grocery researcher from both assuming the other covered it.

#### Bringg — OUT OF SCOPE, RECOMMEND DROPPING — https://www.bringg.com/

- **What it does:** Enterprise last-mile delivery orchestration — routing, dispatch, driver management and tracking across owned fleets and third-party carriers. No search or discovery functionality.
- **Key features:** Delivery orchestration and route optimisation; Carrier network integration; Live tracking and event-based customer updates
- **Named clients:** Walmart, Best Buy, Coca-Cola cited in third-party coverage — not re-verified this pass, and immaterial since the vendor is out of scope.
- **Pricing:** Not published.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Both, fulfilment-side.
- **Demo/trial:** Contact sales.
- **Vs CAS:** OUT OF SCOPE. Bringg competes on fulfilment, not relevance. Retained only as a one-line closure so the client lead knows it was assessed and deliberately excluded — it should not appear on a CAS competitive slide.

**Segment takeaways:**

- DIRECT ANSWER ON THE RECIPE FEATURE — THE CLAIM SURVIVES VERIFICATION, BUT IT IS NARROWER THAN IT LOOKS. Recipe-to-cart is commoditised, but CAS's specific UX — one card per INGREDIENT with up to three ranked BRAND ALTERNATIVES in a carousel — is still not something I could find shipped by anyone, and I re-checked the three strongest candidates by direct fetch this pass. Instacart's Recipe Page API best practice is verbatim: 'Specify the generic product name without any weight, quantity, or brand names. This provides the broadest match possible', with brand_filters and health_filters set by the PUBLISHER, not the shopper (https://docs.instacart.com/developer_platform_api/guide/concepts/recipe). Northfork's own site describes only 'Translate recipes and products into in-stock retailer items with intelligent, proprietary AI' and never mentions per-ingredient brand alternatives (https://northfork.ai/). Samsung Food/Whisk auto-matches one product per ingredient using availability, quantity and purchase history. Albertsons adds 'de-duplicated ingredients' straight to cart. The only near-claim is SideChef's self-authored table asserting it is 'currently the only platform that supports a true multi-button interface, alcohol-specific product routing, quantity precision, and intelligent product substitutions' — vendor-written, about its own competitors, and describing substitution-on-demand rather than up-front alternatives. VERDICT UNCHANGED: the differentiation is real, but it is UX-level and it rests on a NEGATIVE finding (nobody says they do it), which is the weakest evidence class. Do not build the grocery pitch on it alone, and do not overstate it — if a grocer's team has seen a competitor demo it, the whole deck loses credibility.
- THE RECIPE MOAT IS ALREADY BREACHED AT ALL THREE US TARGET ACCOUNTS, AND EVERY DATE BELOW IS NOW VERIFIED BY DIRECT FETCH. Kroger has had Whisk shoppable lists across 35 states since a April 2020 soft-launch, announced 2020-10-19, and announced Gemini Enterprise for CX nationwide on 2026-01-11 with an assistant that can 'complete complex tasks from a single instruction—such as explore meal ideas, build complex carts', grounded in 'actual assortment, pricing, and availability'. Albertsons launched an OpenAI-powered AI Shopping Assistant on 2025-12-03 with six named capabilities including Rapid Restock, Fridge Cleaner and Shop Lists from an uploaded image. H-E-B launched shoppable chef-created recipes on 2025-03-20, and 'ingredient replacements' is explicitly listed as a FUTURE feature — that is a genuine, dated opening at a P0 account. Ahold Delhaize bought recipe-to-cart three times over: Chicory (2025), SmartCommerce Click2Cart (April 2026), Pear Commerce (2026-07-29 across Food Lion, The Giant Co., Giant Food, Hannaford and Stop & Shop). Tesco is in a 280,000-colleague beta of a meal-planning assistant, built with Tomoro AI, that respects dietary preferences and adds ingredients to basket. Pitch CAS's recipe feature as a QUALITY upgrade to an existing capability, never as a new one — and lead with H-E-B, the only account with a published gap that CAS fills.
- TWO VENDORS THE FIRST DRAFT MISSED ENTIRELY, BOTH OF WHICH CHANGE THE KROGER PICTURE. (1) EMPATHY.CO names KROGER and CARREFOUR on its own customers page (https://empathy.co/customers/). The draft asserted Kroger's search is essentially an in-house 84.51° affair; that is now unsafe. Empathy.co is a privacy-first search and discovery platform — 'relevant results but without tracking, spying or sharing customer data' — which is both a competitor and a different trust axis than CAS has considered. Caveat honestly: these are logos on a vendor's own page and I found no Kroger case study, so claim only that Empathy.co LISTS Kroger, never that it powers Kroger's site search. (2) OCADO TECHNOLOGY's Smart Platform 'powers online grocery for 13 leading grocery partners across the globe, totaling 25 live sites and 1,000+ stores', and its own partner page names KROGER, Morrisons, Asda, Coles, Sobeys, ICA, Aeon, Auchan Retail Poland, Alcampo, Bon Preu, Groupe Casino, Lotte and Panda (https://www.ocadogroup.com/about-us/osp-partners). OSP owns the storefront layer at several target-tier grocers, which makes it both an integration constraint and the single highest-leverage channel partner in this segment — one integration reaches thirteen Tier-1 grocers.
- THE #1 CREDIBILITY GAP IS SUBSTITUTION, AND IT IS NOT OPTIONAL IN GROCERY. CAS has no out-of-stock replacement engine at all. Every serious grocery player has one. Instacart runs a Siamese two-tower model over BERT title embeddings plus brand/size/aisle categoricals and binary dietary flags, ensembled with a per-pair engagement model, with candidate generation recalling >95% of shopper-picked replacements. Halla built an entire company on search + substitute + recommend and was acquired by Wynshop on 2024-03-11, with the release citing McKinsey that bad substitutions can drive up to 20% of grocery shoppers to competitors. Algolia's grocery SKU leads with substitutions and out-of-stock as named pillars. H-E-B has 'ingredient replacements' on its published roadmap. RECOMMENDATION: a substitution mode is the highest-ROI addition to CAS for grocery and it is cheap to build, because CAS already has embedding neighbours, typed hard constraints and a reranker that emits reasons. A substitution is an upsell-style neighbour search executed under hard constraints — and the REASON STRING ('same brand, 2% larger pack, also gluten free') is a genuine differentiator, because nobody in this landscape explains their swaps.
- THE #2 GAP IS THAT GROCERY SEARCH IS A REPEAT-PURCHASE PROBLEM, NOT A DISCOVERY PROBLEM. Algolia states '80% of carts are reorders' and MILKRUN's CPTO says 'Around 65% of our customers use search for their shopping' (both verified on https://www.algolia.com/about/news/algolia-unveils-intelligent-grocery-solution). Albertsons' assistant leads with 'Rapid Restock: Quickly reorder weekly essentials and frequent purchases.' Instacart's Cart Assistant 'learns from each customer's preferences and purchase history'. Tesco's assistant adds ingredients 'using shopping history and product preferences'. Instacart's Smart Shop, Kroger's 84.51° household-level personalization and Ahold's and Sainsbury's loyalty engines are all purchase-history-driven. CAS's admitted 'no session personalization' is a minor weakness in fashion and close to a disqualifier in grocery. Every retailer conversation will open with 'does it know what I bought last week?' — have an answer, even if the answer is 'we consume your existing loyalty/purchase-history feature store rather than rebuilding it'.
- THE #3 GAP IS LATENCY AND INVENTORY AWARENESS, AND INSTACART HAS PUBLISHED THE EXACT ARCHITECTURE TO CLOSE IT. Verified from Instacart's own post: 'The out-of-the-box latency was nearly 700ms with A100 GPU… Merging the LoRA adapter weights directly into the base model and upgrading to H100 GPUs got us to our 300ms target', with an offline frontier-model pipeline handling head/torso queries and the real-time LoRA-tuned Llama-3-8B reserved for cold-start/tail. Algolia's MILKRUN quotes ~20ms. CAS's ~7s warm AI path is roughly 20-350x off grocery norms for a session where a shopper builds a 40-item basket. The fix is literally Instacart's: precompute head and torso queries offline into a cache and reserve live LLM calls for the tail. CAS already has page_cache.py, so extend it into an offline batch pre-warm keyed on query logs — this is the cheapest large win available. Separately, no grocery ranking is credible without store-level availability: Constructor, Algolia and Kroger's 2018 ad quality score all rank on real-time or store-level inventory, while CAS ranks a static CSV. CORRECTION FOR THE RECORD: the first draft's claim that Instacart caches ~98% of queries offline is NOT supported by the post as fetched — do not use that number.
- RETAIL MEDIA IS WHERE THE GROCERY BUDGET IS, AND THE MARKET IS MOVING AGAINST CAS'S AD POSTURE. Verified 2026-06-24: Albertsons Media Collective partnered with Criteo so that 'Select sponsored products now appear within Albertsons' search product carousels relevant to shoppers' search queries', framed by Albertsons' SVP Digital CX as retail media that is 'useful and additive'. Verified 2018-06-04: Kroger's Boosted Products in Search already balances 'cost-per-click bid pricing, customer relevancy, and in-flight performance optimization' with an ML quality score that 'occurs in real-time, accounting for store-level inventory, promotions, shifting consumer preferences, and seasonality'. Instacart's Carrot Ads page claims 'demand from 9,000+ CPG brands' and 'Schnucks increases Retail Media revenue by 7X with Carrot Ads'. Two implications, both uncomfortable: (a) CAS's relevance gate is NOT novel — Kroger shipped the concept eight years ago, so never present it as an innovation; (b) CAS's hard rule that ads are never blended into organic is commercially weaker than what buyers are actively purchasing. Reframe it as a configurable, AUDITABLE governance policy — the gate is inspectable, the bid never reaches the ranking client, ads are always labelled — rather than an architectural refusal. One supporting argument is available: SideChef alleges Samsung Food 'does not have a clear distinction between sponsored and non-sponsored items', which is exactly the failure mode CAS's labelling prevents, though it is a vendor-authored allegation.
- PRIVATE LABEL AND MERCHANDISER CONTROL ARE TABLE STAKES AND CAS HAS NEITHER. Algolia sells 'retail media and own-brand promotion' as a named pillar and quotes Auto Mercado's head of ecommerce saying it 'makes it easier to promote our private label brands and personalized impulse products'. Constructor ranks explicitly on margin and AOV, not relevance alone. Google's Conversational Commerce agent gives admins boost/bury/restrict controls. Grocery gross margins are thin and private label is the main margin lever, so a search system with no merchandising console, no boost/bury, no margin term and no synonym or business-rule editor is not deployable — these are already on CAS's admitted gap list, but in grocery they move from 'nice to have' to blockers. The margin term is the cheapest to add: CAS already blends a rating signal at weight 0.05, and a private-label/margin term is the same mechanism and the same code path.
- THE COMPETITIVE SET IS SHIFTING FROM 'SEARCH VENDORS' TO 'AI STACK LICENSORS', WHICH SQUEEZES CAS FROM ABOVE. Verified 2025-11-04: Instacart launched AI Solutions — Agentic Commerce, AI/Cart Assistant, In-Store Intelligence, Catalog Intelligence, Agentic Analytics — with Kroger, Sprouts and Good Food Holdings as launch retailers, Cart Assistant described as an 'enterprise-grade, white-label agentic tool purpose-built for grocery'. Verified 2025-09-10: Google launched its Conversational Commerce agent with Albertsons as the marquee retailer. Both sell precisely CAS's layer, pre-integrated, with enterprise SLAs, to CAS's own target accounts. LatentView cannot win on 'we have an LLM search pipeline' — that is now a purchasable SKU from two of the largest vendors in the market. The three defensible positions are: (1) the CLIENT'S OWN catalog and data, where constraint extraction, reranker reasons and the eval harness are delivered as bespoke IP on the retailer's stack rather than as a shared vendor model; (2) EVALUATION AND MEASUREMENT, which no vendor publishes in a client-auditable form and which is native consultancy work; (3) the integration story with the incumbent keyword stack, which CAS already has via the BM25 router — and which is architecturally the same idea as Google's own intent classifier, so it is a defensible, market-validated design rather than an odd one.
- THE DELIVERY-MODEL PRECEDENT IS GOOD NEWS, AND IT IS THE STRONGEST SLIDE IN THE DECK. Tesco's assistant was built by its in-house app, data-science and engineering teams together with UK AI consultancy Tomoro AI (verified 2026-04-09), with Ken Murphy saying it 'had the potential to reshape how customers shop with Tesco over time'. Albertsons publicly names Google, OpenAI and Databricks as transformation partners — which aligns with CAS's own Databricks migration. Across Tesco, Albertsons and Kroger the scope grocers actually buy is remarkably consistent: multi-turn meal planning, dietary-preference dialogue, history-aware basket building, and food-waste/pantry awareness. CAS covers roughly one and a half of those four. Reshaping the grocery demo around 'plan the week, respect the diet, reuse what's in the fridge, fill the basket' — rather than 'type a clever query, get better results' — matches the observed buying pattern far more closely than the current demo does.
- GROCERY-SPECIFIC RETRIEVAL PROBLEMS TO FIX BEFORE ANY DEMO, ALL FOUR NOW SOURCED. (a) DIVERSITY: Instacart explicitly applies 'a diversity based reranking algorithm to ensure that users can see a variety of options' after mapping LLM-generated items back to the catalog — a published fix for CAS's open '70 paneer sellers' P2, which belongs in the pipeline, not the backlog. (b) UNIT/PACK NORMALISATION: Constructor sells automatic normalisation of quantity/volume data, and Whisk's Nick Holzherr framed the problem exactly — 'If you need for a recipe three tomatoes, you don't want a box of 22 tomatoes being delivered.' CAS has no unit handling, which breaks recipe quantities badly and is immediately visible in a demo. (c) PANTRY SUPPRESSION: SideChef's comparison singles out Northfork for pantry-item support 'critical for long-tail grocery needs', and Albertsons ships 'Fridge Cleaner'. CAS puts every ingredient on an equal card, which looks naive beside either. NOTE: the earlier claim that Whisk greys out staples like olive oil and salt could not be verified this pass — verify in the Samsung Food app before repeating it. (d) NEAR-MISS CATEGORY COLLISIONS: Halla markets its ability to distinguish 'bananas' from 'banana bread', precisely the error pure cosine similarity makes. Add all four as named cases in data/recipe_eval.json and data/stress_queries.json before the grocery demo — they are cheap to add and they are the exact failures a grocery buyer will probe for.
- BENCHMARK NUMBERS TO QUOTE, WITH CORRECTED SCOPING. Albertsons: the CEO said on an earnings call, 'Our Ask AI search capability is already delivering a 10% increase in basket size for those customers using it' — note the scope, this is a lift among FEATURE USERS from an earnings call, not an audited chain-wide A/B (https://www.cxtoday.com/ai-automation-in-cx/albertsons-uses-google-ai-search-to-drive-10-bigger-grocery-baskets/). Instacart embedding retrieval, quoted verbatim from the paper abstract: 'we achieve 10% relative improvement in RECALL@20, and for online A/B testing, we achieve 4.1% cart-adds per search (CAPS) and 1.5% gross merchandise value (GMV) improvement' (https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.05555). Instacart LLM query understanding: rewrite coverage 'to over 95% with 90%+ precision', 'average scroll depth' down 6%, tail-query complaints down 50%, production model precision 96.4% vs 95.4%. Instacart LLM discovery content: '18% improvement in engagement rate'. Algolia: Auto Mercado 50%+ conversion lift; the '+39% UK OOS conversion' figure is FoodNavigator trade coverage, not Algolia's own release — attribute it correctly or drop it. Constructor: flaschenpost '4% search CVR', Belk '$35M in revenue', Target Australia '+A$13M in search revenue'. USE THE INSTACART OFFLINE/ONLINE PAIR AS THE HONEST INTERNAL BENCHMARK: a full embedding-retrieval rebuild by a world-class grocery team produced +1.5% GMV. That is the realistic order of magnitude CAS should promise, and it is an essential counterweight to leading with an NDCG 0.950 headline that a retailer cannot bank.
- WHAT THE FIRST DRAFT GOT WRONG — CORRECTIONS THE CLIENT LEAD MUST SEE BEFORE ANYTHING SHIPS. KILLED: 'Co-op' as an Algolia grocery partner (not on Algolia's release); Instacart's '~98% of queries served from an offline cache' and the '50%' rewrite-coverage baseline (neither in the post as fetched); Chicory's 8.96X iROAS, $600K incremental sales and 62% new/lapsed figures (not on chicory.co); Instacart Smart Shop 'human-in-the-loop' review (the release says vision-language models only); 'McKeever's (Store View)' (unverifiable). CORRECTED: Algolia's grocery launch date is 2025-09-24, not 22/26; SideChef DOES have named clients — Ocean Spray, Bertolli, Del Monte — the draft wrongly said none; Constructor's grocery-adjacent logos include BJ'S WHOLESALE CLUB, not just flaschenpost; Instacart's 14 dietary preferences do NOT include 'fat free' or 'kosher' as user-selectable options (those are recipe-API health_filters, a different list) — the user list is gluten free, high protein, high fiber, lactose free, low calorie, low carb, low sodium, low sugar, low fat, organic, pescatarian, preservative free, vegan, vegetarian; Whisk's pantry-staple greying-out is unverified; the Albertsons +10% basket is scoped to feature users on an earnings call; the Pear Commerce GlobeNewswire URL failed on re-fetch, so cite the Shelby Report piece instead. STILL UNVERIFIED AND SAFE ONLY AS CAVEATED: NIQ Label Insight's specific coverage counts; 84.51°'s 62M households / 1,500 partners; Swiftly's customer list; Firework's Lowe's 2x claim; Instacart Carrot Tags customers. UNCHANGED CAVEATS THAT HELD UP: H-E-B publishes almost nothing about its search technology — treat it as unknown-stack and greenfield, and note its published 'ingredient replacements' roadmap gap; Sainsbury's has no publicly named site-search vendor, though Northfork names it for shoppable recipes; Tesco's search relevance stack is unpublished; SideChef's comparison is the best public map of recipe-to-cart but is written BY a vendor ABOUT its competitors, so cite its structure and never its verdicts; I could verify NO grocery customer for Mercatus, and Bloomreach's grocery URL 404s with only a fashion case study on its customers page — the same unverified status applies to Coveo, Lucidworks and Klevu, which should not be asserted as grocery competitors without further research; Bringg has no search functionality and should be dropped from the competitive set. FINALLY, A PROCESS NOTE: this session's web-search budget was exhausted partway through verification, so the checks above were completed by direct page fetches of the cited URLs. Coverage of second-tier European grocery search vendors (Luigi's Box returned HTTP 403, plus Attraqt/Crownpeak, Findologic, Nosto, Netcore Unbxd and Miso.ai) remains a known hole and is the right target for the next research pass.

**Sources:** https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/instacart-announces-new-enterprise-ai-solutions-to-democratize-ai-for-grocers-of-all-sizes-302603735.html · https://www.instacart.com/company/pressreleases/instacart-announces-new-enterprise-ai-solutions-to-democratize-ai-for-grocers-of-all-sizes · https://www.cnbc.com/2025/11/04/instacart-ai-solutions-sprouts-grocery.html · https://www.grocerydive.com/news/kroger-sprouts-instacart-artificial-intelligence-grocery/804637/ · https://company.instacart.com/tech-innovation/building-the-intent-engine-how-instacart-is-revamping-query-understanding-with-llms · https://tech.instacart.com/supercharging-discovery-in-search-with-llms-556c585d4720 · https://tech.instacart.com/how-instacart-uses-machine-learning-to-suggest-replacements-for-out-of-stock-products-8f80d03bb5af · https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.05555 · https://company.instacart.com/pressreleases/instacart-launches-ai-powered-smart-shop-and-new-features-that-make-healthy-choices-easy · https://docs.instacart.com/developer_platform_api/guide/concepts/recipe · https://company.instacart.com/enterprise-platform/retail-media/carrot-ads · https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/instacart-receives-expanded-mrc-accreditation-for-carrot-ads-bringing-verified-ad-metrics-to-more-than-240-ecommerce-sites-302606493.html · https://www.marketingdive.com/news/instacarts-new-tech-platform-lets-retailers-sell-ads-on-their-sites/620952/ · https://www.googlecloudpresscorner.com/2025-09-10-Google-Cloud-Launches-Conversational-Commerce-Agent,-Delivering-AI-Enabled,-Personalized-Shopping-Experiences-for-Customers

### Fashion retail search & discovery (P0 vertical) — competitive and adjacency landscape for CAS [FACT-CHECKED AND EXPANDED. 13 corrections applied, 7 vendors added. Verification key: [V] = confirmed on a primary vendor/press page I fetched; [S] = search-result summary I saw but did not open; [A] = aggregator (Latka/Tracxn/CB Insights) — NOT deck-safe.]

#### Daydream ("Powered by Daydream") — https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/daydream-launches-ai-powered-search-and-discovery-solution-for-fashion-brands-and-retailers-302837597.html

- **What it does:** MAJOR OMISSION FROM THE ORIGINAL REPORT — and the single most direct competitor to CAS's exact pitch that exists. Founded by Julie Bornstein (former Stitch Fix COO, ex-Nordstrom, ex-Sephora), Daydream is an AI-native fashion search and discovery platform: natural-language and image-based conversational search over a fashion catalog. Critically, on 2026-07-29 it launched "Powered by Daydream" — a B2B program that puts its natural-language search and visual discovery INTO fashion brands' and retailers' OWN websites, against their own inventory. That is precisely CAS's go-to-market motion, from a venture-funded, fashion-exclusive, founder-credentialed competitor. [V]
- **Key features:** Natural-language search — shoppers describe what they want conversationally rather than filtering [V]; Visual discovery / image input [V]; Intent interpretation returning results from the brand's OWN catalog and inventory — the same "sell into the existing site" shape as CAS [V]; Positioned for easy integration with existing website infrastructure and product catalogs [V]; Consumer marketplace side: catalog spans 325 retailers, 10,000+ brands, 3 million products; exited beta with 1.5 million shoppers [V]
- **Named clients:** LIVE on Powered by Daydream at launch: STAUD, Alice + Olivia, Couper, Cult Mia, Hampden Clothing [V — named in the PRNewswire launch release]; SIGNED to join: Anine Bing, Mansur Gavriel, ba&sh, Sandro, Maje, MESHKI, Ramy Brook, plus 25+ additional brands [V]; NOTE the tier: these are contemporary/premium fashion brands, not yet H&M/C&A-scale enterprise retailers. Daydream has NOT yet proven the enterprise tier — which is exactly where LatentView's existing client relationships are the counterweight.
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED — not disclosed in the launch announcement. [V] Funding context: $50M seed round announced 2024-06-20, investors including Forerunner Ventures, Index Ventures, Google Ventures and True Ventures. [S — PRNewswire/TechCrunch coverage]
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: purpose-built and fashion-exclusive — the most fashion-native competitor in this landscape. GROCERY: none, and no stated ambition there. This asymmetry matters: Daydream cannot follow CAS into grocery, so CAS's two-vertical story is a real differentiator against it.
- **Demo/trial:** The consumer product is publicly usable at https://www.daydream.ing — HIGH-VALUE ACTION: this is now the best hands-on benchmark for CAS's natural-language fashion queries, better than YesPlz, because it is the same pitch executed by a well-funded fashion-native team. Run CAS's own eval queries against it before any client meeting. The B2B "Powered by Daydream" tier is sales-led.
- **Vs CAS:** THE MOST URGENT COMPETITIVE FINDING IN THIS PASS, AND IT WAS MISSING ENTIRELY FROM THE ORIGINAL REPORT. Daydream makes CAS's exact argument (fashion needs conversational natural-language understanding, not keyword matching), has a marquee founder, $50M, and as of five weeks ago is selling it as an embeddable layer into retailers' own sites. WHAT THIS CHANGES FOR THE PITCH: (1) CAS can no longer present LLM-based natural-language fashion search as a novel capability — a buyer may already have seen Daydream's demo. (2) The defensible CAS ground shifts to things Daydream does not evidence: typed hard-constraint extraction with score-ceiling enforcement, per-result human-readable reranker reasons, a published eval harness, hybrid BM25 routing into an incumbent keyword stack, and cross-vertical (grocery) coverage. (3) Daydream's weakness is the enterprise tier and integration depth — it is a product, whereas CAS is a consulting-delivered capability that adapts to the client's stack. That is LatentView's actual moat and should be stated plainly. RECOMMENDATION: brief the client lead on Daydream explicitly. Being surprised by it in a room is far worse than raising it first.

#### Syte — https://www.syte.ai/

- **What it does:** Israeli visual-AI product discovery platform for apparel/footwear/jewellery/home. Founded 2015 as image search, now a discovery suite across three solution areas: Visual Discovery, AI Tagging & Merchandising, and Hyper-Personalization. Self-describes as "The only AI-powered product recommendation and discovery platform built from the ground up for ecommerce," "trained on billions of apparel shopper interactions." [V]
- **Key features:** Camera/visual search including multi-object detection and smart object cropping [V]; Shop Similar, Shop the Look, Shop Social visual recommendation modules [V]; Automated deep tagging against a proprietary lexicon of "Over 15,000 fashion, home decor, and jewelry attributes" plus synonyms [V]; MERCHANDISING CONSOLE — the concrete shape of CAS's admitted gap: AI Tag Editor ("Review AI-generated tags and add or remove tags according to your merchandising and brand guidelines"), Lexicon Manager ("Tailor your taxonomy by mapping hundreds of product categories, attributes and values"), Thematic Tags, Tag Analytics, and a unified console to "Set ranking strategies, merchandising and business rules, and run A/B tests" [V]; Out-of-stock recovery — surfaces visually similar in-stock items instead of a dead end [V]; Pre-built cartridges for Shopify/Shopify Plus and Salesforce Commerce Cloud [V]
- **Named clients:** C&A — CONFIRMED named on Syte's fashion page. [V] This is a DIRECT hit on a stated P0 target buyer and the most actionable account-intelligence item in the report.; Also confirmed named on the fashion page: Prada, PrettyLittleThing, Decathlon, Farfetch, Perry Ellis International, Falabella, Baycrews, Venca, Tally Weijl, Nalli Silks [V]; Company-level: "more than 100 customers worldwide in apparel, footwear, jewelry, and home decor" [V — Pereg Ventures press release, 2024-12]; *** CORRECTION TO THE ORIGINAL REPORT *** The original attributed "2.3X higher conversion rate" to PrettyLittleThing. I could not reproduce that attribution. Syte's homepage attributes 2.5X CVR + 40% AOV uplift to DECATHLON; the fashion page surfaced 2.3X against Perry Ellis. Syte's own pages attribute the same-shaped multiples inconsistently across surfaces — which is itself the reason none of these numbers should go in a client deck.
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED. Enterprise quote-only; no self-serve tier. [V] *** CORRECTION *** The original report cited "a comparable visual-search widget (ViSenze) at $200–500/mo up to 50K SKUs" as an indicative band. KILL THIS: I found no primary source for that figure, and ViSenze was acquired by Rezolve Ai on 2025-08-27 [S], so any pre-acquisition price point is stale. There is no defensible public price band for visual search — say "not published" and stop.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: extremely strong — native vertical, plus jewellery and home decor; the Pereg press release explicitly doubles down on apparel. GROCERY: none. Visual similarity is near-worthless for packaged grocery and their lexicon has no dietary/nutritional dimension. Not a competitor in CAS's second vertical.
- **Demo/trial:** No public demo or free trial. Demo-request/sales-contact only via https://www.syte.ai/. Public case studies at https://www.syte.ai/customers-case-studies/
- **Vs CAS:** HIGHEST-PRIORITY INCUMBENT INSIDE A NAMED P0 ACCOUNT (C&A). Overlaps CAS on site search, recommendations, complete-the-look and attribute tagging. Does what CAS does NOT: visual/camera search; a business-user merchandising console with tag editing, taxonomy mapping, ranking strategies and built-in A/B testing (three CAS gaps in one product); out-of-stock similar-item recovery; packaged platform cartridges. CAS does what Syte does not: LLM natural-language intent decomposition with typed hard constraints, per-result reranker reasons, recipe/shopping-list construction, hybrid BM25 routing. *** CORPORATE STATUS — UPGRADED FROM UNVERIFIED TO PRIMARY, AND PARTLY CORRECTED *** The original flagged the acquisition as unverified aggregator data. It is now PRIMARY-SOURCED and deck-safe: Pereg Ventures took a MAJORITY STAKE in Syte on 2024-12-04, co-invested with Magma VC, MizMaa and Stardom Ventures; Pereg co-founder Ziv Ben-Barouch became CEO (PRNewswire release). BUT *** CORRECTION *** the original's "headcount 11–50, down from ~150" is WRONG — the best available figure is ~100 employees as of 2026, down from 158 in 2023 [A — do not quote]. Read it as a control change and a moderate contraction, NOT a collapse. Frame it as: the pure-play visual-search category is consolidating (Syte to Pereg; ViSenze to Rezolve; Wide Eyes to Stylitics), which is both an opening for CAS and a warning about standalone economics in this niche.

#### Lily AI — https://lily.ai/industries/fashion

- **What it does:** Product attribution / catalog enrichment. Takes a retailer's thin, merchant-jargon product data and generates customer-language attributes, phrases, descriptions, metadata and schema markup, then pushes it downstream to site search, Google/Meta shopping feeds, SEO and AI answer engines. It is NOT a search engine — it is a data layer feeding whatever search engine the retailer already runs. This distinction is the strategic core of the whole segment.
- **Key features:** Attribute generation at scale — measured case: attributes "increased by an average of 33% (and from 8 attributes to 24 in 1 particular product example)" [V]; Customer-language mapping — thesis that catalogs are "hindered by retail industry jargon" and use merchant-focused language that doesn't connect with consumers [V]; Lily Max agentic loop: detect gaps, generate enrichments, launch matched A/B tests with holdout groups, measure lift via difference-in-differences, deploy winners, with human approval gates [V]; Content quality scoring across completeness, correctness, compliance, relevance, differentiation [V]; Channel optimization for Google Ads / Meta Ads feeds and Google Merchant Center conversational attributes [V]; Enterprise plumbing: SOC 2 Type II, SAML/OAuth SSO, RBAC, multi-brand/multi-region localization, extensible API [V]
- **Named clients:** CONFIRMED on the fetched Lily AI page: Coach, M&S (Marks & Spencer), J.Crew, Kate Spade, UGG, HOKA, Fabletics, Vuori, Beyond Yoga, Bombas, Tecovas, J.McLaughlin, Simkhai, Arhaus, FOOT LOCKER, HIBBETT SPORTS, NARS, Shiseido, Clé de Peau Beauté, Drunk Elephant [V — Foot Locker, Hibbett, Arhaus, Simkhai and Clé de Peau were MISSING from the original report]; Tapestry (Coach's parent) reported "double-digit improvements" in conversions, average sales per view and SEO metrics [V — NRF blog]; *** CORRECTION — CLIENTS TO KILL *** The original listed "Macy's, The Gap, Bloomingdale's, thredUP" as Lily AI clients sourced only from a search-result summary. I could NOT find these on any Lily AI primary page. REMOVE them. An unverifiable client name in a client-facing deck is worse than an omission.; Attributed testimonials confirmed on-page: Dani Ragan (SVP Data Analytics & AI, Fabletics) and Kate Huyett (GM E-Commerce, Bombas, on Google Merchant Center lift) [V]
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED. Enterprise contract, sales-led; page directs to "Book a Scoping Call." No tiers or ranges disclosed. [V] Company scale [A — NOT deck-safe]: Latka lists ~$17.6M ARR; a $20M Series B-1 closed March 2024 led by Conductive Ventures [S]; total raised is reported inconsistently ($51.9M / $63.9M / $71.9M) — do not quote a total.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: flagship vertical and the strongest published evidence base in the segment. GROCERY/CPG: partial — beauty (NARS, Shiseido, Clé de Peau, Drunk Elephant) and "formulation" attributes are adjacent to grocery's dietary/ingredient problem, but no named grocery retailer found. The model transfers to grocery attribute enrichment conceptually.
- **Demo/trial:** No public demo or trial. Sales-led. Public evidence: https://www.lily.ai/resources/customer-stories/ai-product-discoverability/ and https://nrf.com/blog/how-ai-can-solve-discoverability-problem-retailers
- **Vs CAS:** STILL THE MOST IMPORTANT VENDOR FOR CAS'S STRATEGY, AND A COMPLEMENT NOT A COMPETITOR — the original's core judgement holds and the key evidence VERIFIED CLEANLY. Confirmed exactly: attributes +33% average (8→24 on one product), 8.5% discoverability lift, 3.3% search-driven demand lift, and the enriched data was "deployed into Bloomreach for full implementation" — i.e. THE RANKER WAS NOT CHANGED. *** TWO PRECISION CORRECTIONS THAT PROTECT CREDIBILITY *** (1) The retailer is NOT NAMED — the case study says only "a large, multi-brand speciality retailer." Do not imply a named brand. (2) The $22M is explicitly PROJECTED from the measured 3.3% demand lift, not realised revenue. Present it as "a projected $22M, extrapolated from a measured 3.3% demand lift" — the measured numbers are the credible part. *** NEW CORROBORATING FINDING THE ORIGINAL MISSED *** FOOT LOCKER appears as a named client of BOTH Lily AI [V] and Constructor [V]. That is a better proof of the coexistence architecture than the anonymous Bloomreach anecdote, because both vendors are named and both name the same retailer: the enrichment layer and the ranking layer are bought separately and run together. This is the single most useful slide-ready fact for CAS's "sit alongside the incumbent" pitch. WHERE CAS DIFFERS: CAS infers attributes AT QUERY TIME and discards them; Lily AI PERSISTS them into the catalog, so the same work also improves Google Shopping, Meta, SEO, merchandising and forecasting. Query-time inference helps one surface; persisted enrichment helps every surface. RECOMMENDED MOVE (unchanged, still right): add a batch mode to the translator's constraint extractor that writes inferred attributes back as a catalog artifact.

#### Athos Commerce (Klevu + Searchspring) — https://athoscommerce.com/

- **What it does:** MAJOR OMISSION FROM THE ORIGINAL REPORT. Searchspring (PSG Equity portfolio) acquired Finnish AI search vendor Klevu and merged the two into Athos Commerce, finalised 2025-01-09. Combines Klevu's NLP/semantic/vector search with Searchspring's merchandising, personalization, analytics and product-feed syndication into one platform. Full public launch of the merged platform in Q3 2025. This is the mid-market consolidation event in commerce search and the original report missed it entirely. [S for the merger; V for current product/pricing structure]
- **Key features:** Onsite Discovery — search, merchandising and personalization for the retailer's website [V]; Offsite Discovery — product feed management powering marketplaces, social sites "and AI platforms" (i.e. agentic/GEO channels) [V]; AI Agents sold as add-ons: Conversational Assistant, Channel Assistant, and a GEO Assistant — generative-engine-optimization positioning, the same bet YesPlz, Lily AI and Bold Metrics are all making [V]; AI Add-Ons: AI Search, AI Merchandising, AI Recommendations [V]; Klevu heritage: multimodal search combining keyword, semantic, neural and vector retrieval with NLP intent understanding and typo/synonym tolerance [S]
- **Named clients:** Klevu's pre-merger fashion customer base included Puma [S — third-party buyer's guide, not vendor-primary; treat as indicative]; Searchspring historically served mid-market Shopify/BigCommerce/Magento retailers; NOTE: I did not open a primary Athos client page. Do not quote specific Athos client names without direct verification.
- **Pricing:** *** THIS CORRECTS A KEY CLAIM IN THE ORIGINAL REPORT *** Third-party sources cite Klevu pricing "from $449/month with a free trial" [S], but that is legacy Klevu data. The CURRENT Athos Commerce pricing page publishes NO dollar figures and NO free trial — all three plans (Onsite / Offsite / Complete Discovery) are quote-based, with AI Agents and AI Add-Ons priced individually. [V] The merger announcement stated "no change in pricing for the same features or tiers" [S]. Net: the mid-market tier that once had transparent entry pricing has moved behind a quote wall.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: yes, mid-market and upper-mid-market fashion is a core Klevu/Searchspring segment. GROCERY: some food & beverage coverage historically. Relevant to BOTH CAS verticals, but at a tier below H&M/Kroger enterprise.
- **Demo/trial:** Request Pricing / Book a Demo only at https://athoscommerce.com/request-demo/ — no public self-serve trial on the current site. [V]
- **Vs CAS:** MATTERS FOR TWO REASONS THE ORIGINAL REPORT MISSED. (1) MARKET STRUCTURE: this is the second major consolidation in the segment (alongside Syte→Pereg, ViSenze→Rezolve, Attraqt→Crownpeak, Wide Eyes→Stylitics). Commerce search is consolidating into fewer, broader platforms — which strengthens LatentView's premise that CAS should be a capability sold into an existing stack rather than another point product competing for a shrinking number of standalone slots. (2) NOTE THE PSG CONNECTION: PSG Equity backs BOTH Athos Commerce and Stylitics — the same growth-equity firm is assembling positions across discovery and visual merchandising. Worth flagging to the client lead as evidence of where smart capital thinks this market is heading. Also note their GEO Assistant and "AI platforms" feed product: making the catalog legible to AI shopping agents is now a SHIPPING, SOLD feature at the mid-market, not a frontier idea — which raises the urgency of CAS's agent-readability story.

#### Constructor / Bloomreach / Algolia (incumbent commerce search platforms) — https://constructor.com/blog/forrester-wave-commerce-search-product-discovery-solutions-q3-2025

- **What it does:** The horizontal commerce search and product discovery platforms fashion retailers ALREADY OWN — and therefore the stack CAS is designed to sell INTO rather than replace. The original report covered Constructor and Bloomreach; *** IT MISSED ALGOLIA ***, which is a Leader in the 2026 Gartner Magic Quadrant for Search and Product Discovery for the third consecutive year (announced 2026-06-24) and claims 18,000+ business customers. [S]
- **Key features:** Forrester Wave: Commerce Search And Product Discovery Solutions, Q3 2025 — evaluated the 9 most significant vendors against 31 criteria. Bloomreach: LEADER. Constructor: LEADER. Netcore Unbxd: STRONG PERFORMER and highest score in the Current Offering category. Rezolve Ai: CONTENDER. FactFinder: also evaluated. [S — corroborated across multiple vendor announcements]; Constructor scored 5/5 on Vision, Innovation and Roadmap, with perfect marks across eight Current Offering categories including usability, personalization, conversational discovery and product recommendations; Forrester called it "a best fit for firms that prefer a vendor with a singular focus on commerce search and product discovery and that prioritizes swift innovation" [V]; Bloomreach Discovery — GenAI site search, merchandising, personalization; "self-driving search" building profiles from every search, purchase and click [S]; Algolia — Gartner MQ Leader 3x consecutive (2026); Commerce Pipeline indexing foundation, campaign-driven merchandising, unified search + recommendations; explicitly repositioning toward "AI-powered, agentic discovery" [S]
- **Named clients:** Constructor, CONFIRMED on its own site: Sephora, Under Armour, Gap, REI, Tractor Supply, QVC, Bath & Body Works, FOOT LOCKER, Petco, Williams Sonoma, West Elm, Crate and Barrel, Bonobos, Rothy's [V]; Constructor published case: "How Petco drove a 13% lift in ecommerce conversions with Constructor" [V]; Bloomreach is the platform into which Lily AI's enrichment was deployed in its flagship apparel case study [V]; *** CORRECTION *** The original cited "Constructor with White Stuff +21% conversions; Bonobos +92% recommendations conversions" from search results. I could not verify either on Constructor's own site — the only client-specific figure published there is Petco 13%. Drop the White Stuff and Bonobos numbers or verify them directly first.
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED for any of the three; enterprise, sales-led, typically catalog- and traffic-scaled.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: all three have dedicated fashion/apparel practices. GROCERY: all three serve grocery. This is the incumbent layer in nearly every enterprise retailer CAS will encounter, across BOTH target verticals.
- **Demo/trial:** Demo-request. Forrester Wave Q3 2025 reprints available free via vendor gates (https://visit.bloomreach.com/forrester-wave-search-2025, https://info.constructor.com/forrester-wave-2025-commerce-search); Algolia gates the 2026 Gartner MQ at https://www.algolia.com/lp/gartner-magic-quadrant-2026 — pull BOTH, they are the best free maps of this market's evaluation criteria.
- **Vs CAS:** DEFINES AND VALIDATES CAS'S GO-TO-MARKET SHAPE. These are what an H&M, C&A or Kroger actually runs, and they are analyst-recognised Leaders with A/B frameworks, merchandising consoles, analytics and sub-second latency CAS will not match. The reference architecture is proven: Lily AI did not replace Bloomreach, it enriched the data flowing in — and Foot Locker independently appears as a named client of both Constructor and Lily AI. CAS should copy that shape exactly. CAS's hybrid BM25 router (keyword-first, AI fallback on lexical miss) is the right integration primitive and should be the CENTERPIECE of a fashion pitch. *** IMPORTANT CORRECTION — REMOVE THIS LINE FROM THE DECK *** The original claimed "Constructor markets on never having lost an A/B test." That is FALSE. Constructor's site makes no such claim; it publishes A/B learnings via a "Split/Test Digest" resource and leads with a 98.5% average client retention rate over three years, a 4.8 G2 rating and a 4.9 Gartner Peer Insights rating. [V] Stating an unfounded competitor claim in front of a buyer who uses Constructor would be actively damaging. The underlying POINT still stands and can be made honestly: online experimentation is table stakes in this market, and CAS's lack of an A/B framework is a real gap — CAS's offline eval suite (P@10/MRR/NDCG) is genuinely strong for a PoC and more transparent than anything these vendors publish, but it is NOT the same artifact as online experimentation and must not be presented as if it were.

#### Stylitics — https://stylitics.com/

- **What it does:** Outfitting, bundling and visual merchandising at scale — the category leader in complete-the-look. Generates styled outfits and dynamic bundles across PDP, cart, email and post-purchase. Has expanded into AI Image Studio and Catalog Enrichment, putting it in direct competition with Lily AI.
- **Key features:** Outfitting & bundling formats: Shop the Look, Complete the Look, Styled for You [V]; Hybrid AI + human method — product compatibility models, merchandising logic engines, real-time inventory adaptation, plus human expert input on trends and styling [V]; PRODUCT CATALOG ENRICHMENT — "Fill gaps in product catalogs with structured, accurate attributes at scale," using proprietary vision AI, multi-model consensus validation with human QA, fashion taxonomy normalization and search-optimized metadata; publishes a head-to-head "Stylitics vs. Lily AI" comparison [V]; Trend tagging with a "living taxonomy": in-house team "monitors social media, influencer channels, and niche forums" plus AI trained on millions of fashion SKUs; "Nightly feed updates and AI-powered tagging keep your catalog current with zero manual lift" [V]; Real-time inventory awareness so outfits never surface sold-out components [V]; Operational scale claims: "2B+ monthly API calls", 50+ countries, 30+ languages, 99.99% uptime SLA [V]
- **Named clients:** Published on Stylitics' own site with figures: J.Crew (20% cart-add rate), JD Sports (2.87x higher engagement), Huckberry (9.3x higher revenue per session), Rhone (10x ROI in 100 days), Boston Proper (16.4x ROI), Suzanne's (35% higher AOV) [V]; Madewell [V]; From the $80M Series C press release: "more than 3,600 brands across 100-plus retailers"; 100M+ monthly shoppers see Stylitics styling; ~$4 billion in incremental annual revenue for clients [S — PRWeb/PSG release]; *** CORRECTION *** The original said "more than 4,500 brands and retailers." The press-release figure is 3,600 brands across 100+ retailers. Use the press-release wording or neither.; *** CORRECTION *** Macy's, Kohl's, Revolve, Puma, Room & Board, Express were listed in the original from search-result coverage. I did not verify these on a Stylitics primary page (Retail TouchPoints and PSG both returned HTTP 403). Do not name them in a deck without direct confirmation.
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED, but the MODEL is disclosed and remains the most useful pricing datapoint in the segment: "Our pricing is customized to your catalog size, channels, and business goals" plus "Usage-Based Model: Pricing scales with your catalog and activations." No free trial mentioned on the pricing page; page also claims most customers deploy in under 2 weeks with double-digit ROI improvements. [V] *** CORRECTION *** The original noted a Capterra free-trial claim — the official page shows only "Book a demo"; treat the free-trial claim as false/unverified. *** TIMING CORRECTION *** The original implied the ~$80M Series C was recent. It closed in MARCH 2022, led by PSG, bringing total raised to ~$100M. That is now four years old with no subsequent round found — relevant context, not a sign of current momentum. Latka's $40.6M ARR 2024 figure is [A] and not deck-safe.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: the best-capitalized pure-play here, aimed squarely at CAS's target tier. GROCERY: essentially none — outfitting has no grocery analogue, though the bundling engine conceptually maps to meal-kit/basket building, which is where CAS's recipe feature already plays.
- **Demo/trial:** No public self-serve demo. "Book a demo" only at https://stylitics.com/pricing/. Rich public evidence via https://stylitics.com/products/outfitting/ and the blog.
- **Vs CAS:** DIRECT COMPETITOR TO CAS's WEAKEST FASHION FEATURE — judgement unchanged and well-supported. CAS's cross-sell/upsell occupies the same PDP slot as Complete the Look, but Stylitics does only this, with human merchandiser QA, trend monitoring, inventory-aware outfit assembly and nightly catalog refresh. CAS's version is a demo feature; theirs is a mature product. DO NOT lead a fashion pitch with cross-sell — lead with it in GROCERY, where CAS's ingredient-card shopping list is genuinely differentiated. TWO STRATEGIC WARNINGS STAND: (1) Stylitics now sells Catalog Enrichment and publishes a Lily AI comparison — the enrichment layer is consolidating and the window for CAS to claim that slot is closing. (2) Their trend tagging exposes a CAS gap that was NOT on CAS's own known-gaps list: SEASONALITY AND TREND FRESHNESS. Fashion catalogs turn over weekly; CAS has no trend signal, no recency weighting, and an embedding cache keyed to a static CSV content hash that only re-encodes on full catalog swap — architecturally the opposite of nightly refresh.

#### Crownpeak / Attraqt (incl. Fredhopper) — https://www.crownpeak.com/resources/blogs/crownpeak-enters-agreement-to-acquire-attraqt/

- **What it does:** OMITTED FROM THE ORIGINAL REPORT — and its omission caused a factual error elsewhere in that report. Attraqt is a UK product-discovery SaaS (search, navigation, merchandising, recommendations, personalization, internationalization, incl. the Fredhopper and XO products), acquired by Crownpeak in a ~£63.2M deal finalised December 2022 after regulatory approval. It powers discovery for 300+ brands and retailers with a heavily European and heavily FASHION-weighted customer base. [S]
- **Key features:** API-enabled, algorithm-driven SaaS across search, navigation, merchandising, recommendations, personalization and internationalization [S]; Fredhopper and XO product lines — long-established European merchandising engines with deep business-rule tooling [S]; Now bundled into Crownpeak's composable DXP alongside CMS and accessibility products — i.e. discovery sold as part of a broader experience platform, not standalone [S]
- **Named clients:** ASOS, JD Sports, Screwfix, Calvin Klein, Tommy Hilfiger — reported across multiple independent sources covering the Crownpeak acquisition [S]; 300+ brands, manufacturers and retailers [S]; NOTE: I did not open a Crownpeak primary client page. Verify before naming any of these to a client.
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED; enterprise, sales-led, sold as part of the Crownpeak DXP.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: strong, particularly UK/EU fashion — this is the incumbent CAS is most likely to encounter in a European fashion account (which is exactly where C&A and H&M sit). GROCERY: limited evidence.
- **Demo/trial:** Demo-request only.
- **Vs CAS:** *** THIS VENDOR CORRECTS A LOAD-BEARING ERROR IN THE ORIGINAL REPORT. *** The original stated: "Zalando and ASOS are effectively closed: both build in-house." ASOS is repeatedly named as an Attraqt customer across independent coverage of the Crownpeak acquisition [S]. ASOS therefore BUYS a commercial discovery platform (whatever it also builds in-house), which means the "closed, builds everything itself" framing is wrong and should be removed. More importantly, the strategic read flips: large European fashion retailers DO buy third-party discovery, which is an argument FOR CAS's addressable market, not against it. BROADER RELEVANCE: Crownpeak/Attraqt is the most likely incumbent in a European fashion account and is the specific stack CAS's hybrid BM25 router would have to coexist with at an H&M- or C&A-tier buyer. It should be named in any EU fashion pitch prep. It is also a fourth data point in the consolidation pattern (Attraqt→Crownpeak, Klevu+Searchspring→Athos, Syte→Pereg, ViSenze→Rezolve, Wide Eyes→Stylitics).

#### YesPlz AI — https://yesplz.ai/product-discovery

- **What it does:** Small "fashion-native" AI discovery suite — a close philosophical analogue to CAS. Combines deep product tagging, fashion-tuned embeddings, text search, a distinctive visual "Virtual Mannequin" style filter, an AI stylist and complete-the-look, and is explicitly positioning for AI-agent readability.
- **Key features:** Fashion-Native Search with auto-tagging, fashion embeddings and keyword optimization [V]; Virtual Mannequin Filter — interactive mannequin the shopper manipulates to specify silhouette and attributes; claimed to increase average cart size 1.7x [V]; AI Product Tagging capturing "silhouette, color, pattern, details, and even mood" [V]; AI Stylist — an AI-powered virtual personal shopper [V]; Recommendations: similar-but-varied, frequently-bought-together, bundle suggestions; Complete the Look auto-outfitting [V]; "GEO Ready" — turns "your catalog into fashion data AI agents can read" [V]
- **Named clients:** WConcept — "5x Increased Add-to-Cart Rate" (the only detailed case study found) [V]; Mango and Zalando appear in YesPlz marketing content as market examples of AI-stylist adoption — NOT as YesPlz customers. Do not present them as clients. [V]
- **Pricing:** *** MAJOR CORRECTION — THIS WAS THE ORIGINAL REPORT'S CLEAREST FACTUAL ERROR. *** The original claimed YesPlz "appears to be the ONLY vendor in this set with published pricing" and recommended it as "the best available public anchor for what fashion discovery AI actually costs." I fetched https://yesplz.ai/pricing directly: it publishes NO dollar figures. It shows three tiers — Tagging Starter ("Pay-As-You-Go", routed to a pricing calculator), Discovery Essential (Custom Quote) and Complete Discovery Suite (Custom Quote) — and states pricing "varies based on SKU size and traffic," directing buyers to a calculator or hello@yesplz.ai. [V] NET FINDING FOR THE SEGMENT: **NOT A SINGLE VENDOR IN THIS LANDSCAPE PUBLISHES A PRICE.** That is itself the finding, and it is more useful and more honest than a fabricated anchor.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: purpose-built and deeply fashion-specific (mood, silhouette, drape). GROCERY: none.
- **Demo/trial:** YES — still the vendor with the most accessible hands-on demo besides Daydream. Live demo at https://poc.yesplz.ai/demo and a demo storefront at https://shop.yesplz.ai/. Worth running side-by-side against CAS, though Daydream is now the higher-value benchmark.
- **Vs CAS:** CLOSE DIRECT ANALOGUE AND A GOOD BENCHMARK, but downgraded in priority now that Daydream is on the board. YesPlz makes the same core argument CAS makes — fashion needs a fashion-tuned semantic layer, not keyword matching — and has already built several features CAS lists as gaps (AI stylist/conversational, visual style filter, agent-readable catalog output). Their "GEO Ready" positioning validates CAS's agent-readability angle, and it is notable that Athos Commerce now sells a GEO Assistant too: this has moved from frontier to shipping feature in under a year. Their small size and single case study suggest they have not won the enterprise tier — which is where LatentView's consulting relationships are the real differentiator. CAS does not have to out-product YesPlz; it has to out-integrate them inside an existing client account. What CAS has that they do not: typed hard-constraint extraction with score-ceiling enforcement, per-result reranker reasons, recipe/shopping-list construction, a published eval harness, and a hybrid BM25 router.

#### Marqo — https://www.marqo.ai/blog/improving-search-relevance-in-fashion

- **What it does:** Multimodal embedding search for fashion commerce — processes product images and text together in a unified vector representation, explicitly rejecting both the keyword layer and the attribute-enrichment layer. The closest TECHNICAL analogue to CAS, and it argues the OPPOSITE side of the enrichment-vs-ranker question.
- **Key features:** Unified multimodal embeddings understanding silhouette, fabric texture and colour palette alongside description and attributes [V]; Architectural thesis, verified verbatim: "the intelligence lives at the product level, not in a layer of rules and signals on top of it" [V]; Cold-start advantage, verified verbatim: "New products are discoverable from day one. Because the model understands each product based on its content, not its transaction history, a dress added to your catalog this morning ranks correctly for every relevant style query immediately." [V]; Claimed outcomes: Mejuri "saw a 19.8 percent increase in search-driven conversion after moving to Marqo"; "Marqo has generated over 130 million dollars in attributed revenue uplift for a single retailer, measured through controlled A/B testing" [V — both quotes confirmed verbatim on the fetched page]
- **Named clients:** Mejuri (fine jewellery/accessories) — 19.8% increase in search-driven conversion [V]; *** ADDITIONAL CLIENTS THE ORIGINAL REPORT MISSED, confirmed on the fetched page: Kicks Crew, Redbubble, Kogan, Shutterstock, SwimOutlet, Poshmark [V] *** This is a materially broader and more marketplace-weighted client base than the original's "Mejuri plus one unnamed retailer" implied.; One unnamed retailer with $130M attributed uplift via controlled A/B testing [V]
- **Pricing:** Not established. Marqo has open-source roots and a cloud offering, so a self-serve tier likely exists — NOT VERIFIED in either pass. Do not cite a price.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: purpose-positioned, with a strong cold-start argument for fast-turnover catalogs. GROCERY: less compelling — for packaged goods the differentiating attributes are textual/nutritional rather than visual.
- **Demo/trial:** Not verified. Likely self-serve/cloud trial given open-source lineage — confirm before citing.
- **Vs CAS:** THE INTELLECTUAL COUNTERWEIGHT TO LILY AI AND CAS'S CLOSEST TECHNICAL COUSIN — original judgement verified and strengthened. All four load-bearing Marqo quotes checked out verbatim, which is unusual in this segment. Two things CAS should take: (1) THE COLD-START ARGUMENT IS CAS'S BEST UNCLAIMED FASHION TALKING POINT — behavioural/click-driven rankers need weeks of interaction data before a new SKU ranks, whereas CAS's embedding+LLM path ranks a brand-new item correctly on day one from content alone. Concrete, testable with CAS's existing eval harness, and directly answers the fast-fashion buyer's real pain. (2) They validate that multimodal is the natural next step from CAS's current architecture. Note the honest tension: Marqo's thesis and Lily AI's contradict each other and the evidence does not cleanly settle it. Also note the credibility asymmetry — Marqo's $130M claim is explicitly "measured through controlled A/B testing," which is a stronger methodological statement than most of this segment offers, but the retailer is unnamed and the claim is unaudited.

#### Empathy.co — https://empathy.co/

- **What it does:** OMITTED FROM THE ORIGINAL REPORT — and the single most relevant omission for CAS's CROSS-VERTICAL story, because it is the one vendor credibly serving both a fashion giant and a P0 grocery target. Enterprise commerce search and discovery platform, Spanish/Asturias-founded, positioned on trust and privacy: "Enterprise AI Search made for Commerce" with infrastructure "under EU governance and free from any third party or big tech provider." [V]
- **Key features:** Enterprise commerce search and discovery with an explicit privacy/no-tracking and EU-data-sovereignty stance — a genuinely differentiated positioning no other vendor here occupies [V]; Publishes open models and datasets on Hugging Face, including intent recognition and named-entity-recognition models for specific verticals — Groceries GLiNER, Grocery NER Dataset, Books GLiNER NER, Books NER Dataset [V]; Open-source components and published vertical NER — the most technically transparent vendor in this landscape, and the only one whose approach can be inspected rather than taken on trust [V]; Claims "trusted by over 3000 brands worldwide" [V]
- **Named clients:** CONFIRMED on the Empathy.co homepage: KROGER (a stated P0 grocery target for CAS), Ocado, Casa del Libro, Motive Market; logos also shown for Carrefour, Vodafone and BSH/Bosch Home [V]; *** UNVERIFIED — DO NOT QUOTE *** CB Insights lists INDITEX (Zara's parent) as an Empathy.co customer [A]. This would be an extremely significant fact if true — Inditex is the world's largest fashion group and directly adjacent to the H&M/C&A tier — but Inditex does NOT appear on Empathy.co's own homepage, and the only supporting material I saw was an Empathy.co Medium post analysing Zara.com search data. TREAT AS UNCONFIRMED and verify directly before it goes anywhere near a client conversation.; NOTE: Empathy.co's homepage does NOT mention fashion as a vertical; its published models and demos are grocery- and books-weighted. [V]
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED; no performance metrics on the homepage either. [V]
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** GROCERY: very strong — Kroger, Ocado and Carrefour are exactly the tier CAS targets in its second vertical, and their published grocery NER models attack precisely the problem CAS's dietary-constraint extraction addresses. FASHION: weaker/unevidenced on their own site despite the unconfirmed Inditex association. This is the inverse profile of every other vendor in this report.
- **Demo/trial:** Interactive demos are linked from the homepage (including a "Healthy Eating Ocado" demo) and their models are publicly inspectable on Hugging Face — making this the most technically examinable competitor in the landscape. Worth a hands-on session before any GROCERY pitch. [V]
- **Vs CAS:** THE MOST STRATEGICALLY INSTRUCTIVE OMISSION AFTER DAYDREAM, for three reasons. (1) DIRECT P0 GROCERY INCUMBENT: Kroger is a named CAS target and Empathy.co is already there. Any Kroger conversation is coexistence-or-displacement, not greenfield — the same situation as Syte at C&A, and the client lead should know both before either meeting. (2) THEIR GROCERY NER MODELS ARE A DIRECT ANALOGUE TO CAS'S DIETARY-CONSTRAINT EXTRACTION, and they are OPEN — CAS can actually inspect and benchmark against them rather than guessing, which is rare in this market and a cheap, high-value exercise for the team. (3) THE EU-SOVEREIGNTY / NO-BIG-TECH POSITIONING IS A RISK FLAG FOR CAS'S OWN ARCHITECTURE: CAS currently runs on Groq-hosted gpt-oss-120b. A European fashion or grocery buyer (C&A, H&M, Carrefour) may well have data-residency requirements that a US-hosted LLM inference path fails outright. Empathy.co has built an entire market position on that objection, which is evidence the objection is real and buyer-driven. RECOMMENDATION: prepare a data-residency/on-prem answer for CAS's LLM layer BEFORE a European client meeting — the provider abstraction in app/llm_client.py makes a swap to an EU-hosted or self-hosted model a config change, which is a strong answer if it is rehearsed and a weak one if it is improvised.

#### Google — Vertex AI Search for commerce, Google Lens, and AI Mode — https://cloud.google.com/solutions/vertex-ai-search-commerce

- **What it does:** TWO ROLES, AND THE ORIGINAL REPORT ONLY COVERED ONE. (a) DEMAND-SIDE FORCE: Google Lens has trained billions of shoppers to photograph a garment and expect to find it. (b) *** SUPPLY-SIDE COMPETITOR THE ORIGINAL MISSED ENTIRELY ***: Vertex AI Search for commerce is Google Cloud's managed retail search service — Retail Search, Browse and Recommendations — using Google-grade retrieval, ranking and personalization signals, explicitly tuned for retail domains including fashion, and supporting up to 10,000 custom attributes per catalog for large customers. [S]
- **Key features:** Google Lens: more than 25 billion queries per month, with roughly 1 in 5 (20%) carrying commercial intent — stated by Dan Taylor, VP of Global Advertising at Google, in a press briefing on 2026-03-12 [S — reported by The Asia Business Daily and corroborated across multiple outlets; still a VP quote via secondary outlets, NOT a Google-primary publication]; Lens is integrated into Google's AI Mode, merging multimodal input with generative answers [S]; Vertex AI Search for commerce: managed retail search/browse/recommendations tuned to optimize revenue per visit using proprietary Google signals [S]; *** DIRECTLY COMPETES WITH CAS'S FLAGSHIP GROCERY FEATURE ***: Google's conversational commerce materials describe a grocery shopper saying "I need the ingredients for lasagna" and the AI populating the cart, including brand preference follow-ups — functionally the same as CAS's recipe/shopping-list capability [S]; Albertsons — a stated P0 GROCERY target for CAS — collaborated with Google to bring Conversational Agents for Commerce to market via its "Ask AI" tool [S]
- **Named clients:** Albertsons (Google Cloud conversational commerce / "Ask AI") — DIRECT hit on a P0 grocery target [S]; Platform-level for Lens: used by over a billion people monthly [S]; Retailer-side proof that visual search is old news in fashion: ASOS launched Style Match in 2017 and rolled it out globally in 2018 across 85,000+ products with 5,000 new items added weekly [S]; *** NEW, UNVERIFIED, AND IMPORTANT IF TRUE *** Third-party coverage claims H&M's in-app visual search now accounts for over 12% of mobile product searches, with camera users converting 15% faster and ~8% higher basket values [S — secondary industry blogs only, no H&M primary source found]. If confirmed, this materially changes the H&M account strategy: H&M would already have visual search, making it a weaker differentiator to pitch and a stronger baseline expectation to meet. VERIFY BEFORE USING.
- **Pricing:** Lens: N/A. Vertex AI Search for commerce: usage-based Google Cloud pricing, published in GCP documentation — the only genuinely public pricing in this entire landscape, and worth pulling as a cost-modelling reference for CAS's own commercials.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: maximal on the demand side — apparel is the most visually-driven category. GROCERY: Vertex AI Search for commerce plus the Albertsons partnership make Google a real competitor in CAS's SECOND vertical too, including on recipe-to-cart, which CAS treats as a signature feature.
- **Demo/trial:** Google Lens is freely usable — test it in 30 seconds before pitching. Vertex AI Search for commerce has public GCP documentation, a marketplace listing and self-serve trial access via Google Cloud — meaning a retailer's own cloud team can pilot it without a vendor conversation.
- **Vs CAS:** MAKES CAS'S BIGGEST FASHION GAP UNAVOIDABLE, AND ADDS A GROCERY THREAT THE ORIGINAL REPORT MISSED. On visual: CAS has NO image capability; ASOS shipped it nine years ago, Syte/YesPlz/Daydream are built around it, and Google has trained billions of shoppers to expect it. DO NOT bluff this. DO make the architectural argument: CAS's retrieval is already embedding-based and modality-agnostic, so swapping gte-small for a CLIP-class multimodal encoder is a contained change in app/embeddings.py — search.py, the reranker and the constraint layer all operate on vectors regardless of origin, and the catalog already carries an img_url column, so it is demonstrable rather than hypothetical. Build an image-search spike BEFORE any fashion client demo. *** NEW WARNING FOR THE GROCERY VERTICAL *** Google is shipping recipe-to-cart conversational commerce with Albertsons, a named CAS target. CAS's recipe/ingredient-card feature — currently its most differentiated capability — is NOT unique against Google Cloud, and a grocery buyer already on GCP may see it as something their cloud vendor provides. CAS's answer must be depth (per-ingredient brand alternatives, typed dietary constraint enforcement, published completeness eval against a 70% coverage target) rather than novelty, and the team should be ready for "why not just use Vertex?" as a first-meeting question.

#### ViSenze (a Rezolve Ai company) — https://www.visenze.com/ai-search-discovery/

- **What it does:** OMITTED FROM THE ORIGINAL REPORT AS A VENDOR (it appeared only as a discredited pricing anchor). Visual search, multimodal search, smart recommendations and automated product tagging for retail, with models trained on millions of fashion and apparel products. ACQUIRED BY REZOLVE AI on 2025-08-27 — and Rezolve Ai was named a Contender in the same Forrester Wave Q3 2025 that made Bloomreach and Constructor Leaders. [S]
- **Key features:** Visual/image search — snap-a-photo product discovery for fashion by colour, pattern and style [S]; Multimodal AI search combining image and text signals, which they position as the future of commerce search and product discovery [S]; Automated product tagging trained on millions of fashion/apparel products [S]; Serves fast fashion, luxury, marketplaces, home decor and eyewear [S]
- **Named clients:** Flipkart — image search for fashion discovery [S]; Essilor [S]; NOTE: I did not verify these on a ViSenze primary client page. Verify before naming.
- **Pricing:** NOT PUBLISHED. *** EXPLICIT CORRECTION *** The original report used "ViSenze at $200–500/mo up to 50K SKUs" as an indicative price band for the segment. I found NO primary source for that figure, and ViSenze has been part of Rezolve Ai since August 2025, so any pre-acquisition SaaS price point is stale regardless. DELETE this number from all materials.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** FASHION: yes — trained specifically on fashion/apparel, serving fast fashion and luxury. GROCERY: none.
- **Demo/trial:** Demo-request; public blog and newsletter content at https://www.visenze.com/blog/
- **Vs CAS:** INCLUDED PRIMARILY TO KILL A BAD NUMBER AND TO COMPLETE THE CONSOLIDATION PICTURE. Two takeaways: (1) The ViSenze price band that the original report offered as a band-setting anchor is unsourced and stale — removing it leaves the segment with ZERO public price points outside Google Cloud, which is the honest position. (2) ViSenze→Rezolve is the fifth acquisition in this landscape and it is directionally important: a pure-play visual search vendor was absorbed into a broader commerce-search platform that then placed in the Forrester Wave. Visual search is ceasing to be a standalone category and becoming a FEATURE of platforms. For CAS that cuts both ways — it means visual search alone would never have been a business, but it also means buyers increasingly expect it bundled into whatever discovery layer they buy, which raises the bar on CAS's roadmap answer.

#### Fit Analytics — https://www.fitanalytics.com/

- **What it does:** Size and fit recommendation — the incumbent in apparel sizing. Fit Finder gives shoppers a size recommendation to cut fit-driven returns, and an AI Shopping Assistant now handles size, style, budget and context conversationally, pushing them into discovery adjacency.
- **Key features:** Fit Finder size recommendation widget on the PDP [V]; AI Shopping Assistant — helps shoppers identify what they want by understanding size, style, budget and context; answers questions, filters options and guides toward a purchase. This is the piece that overlaps CAS. [V]; Data moat: a decade's worth of purchase, return, body and garment data [V]; Published average outcomes, confirmed on the homepage: 4-6% conversion rate increase, 2-4% return rate decrease, 30% share of purchases at top 15 shops, 250+ global partners [V]
