Separately, commercetools AI Hub is the agentic-commerce channel layer, and it is a bigger deal than the original report conveyed.
- **Key features:** Three search modes: Lexical / Semantic / Hybrid with RRF fusion; Embeddings generated natively from existing product data — no separate pipeline; Advanced faceting: distinct, range, count and stats facets; Full-text, exact, fuzzy, prefix, wildcard and range expressions; B2B: business-unit-specific search with entitlement filtering; AI Hub (VERIFIED verbatim): 'a universal orchestration layer that translates your brand's commerce logic across AI platforms such as ChatGPT, Gemini and Copilot, making products discoverable and shoppable wherever customers engage'; AI Hub covers the FULL transaction lifecycle — discovery through order completion — and is multi-protocol, explicitly naming Google's UCP and OpenAI's ACP
- **Named clients:** CORRECTION — the original report left this array EMPTY. commercetools names JD Sports, Liverpool (Liverpool FC / Liverpool retail), Frasers Group, John Lewis and Vision Healthcare on its AI Hub page. JD Sports, Frasers Group and John Lewis are all UK fashion/department-store buyers at exactly the C&A/H&M tier LatentView targets. CAVEAT: these are named as commercetools platform customers on the AI Hub page — I did not verify each one is specifically live on Storefront Search or AI Hub.
- **Pricing:** Not published. commercetools uses consumption-based pricing keyed on ORDER VOLUME plus SKU count, API call volume and project complexity; editions are Core, Foundry and Premium. Third-party sources put Core Commerce from ~$50,000/year, most enterprises at $40,000-$300,000+/year in licence fees, and large global deployments at $250K-$2M+, with standard 3-year terms and 1-year options at a 20-30% premium (https://costbench.com/software/enterprise-ecommerce/commercetools/, https://www.swell.is/content/commercetools-pricing). These are aggregator estimates, not vendor-published figures — indicative only, NOT quotable to a client.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Strong European fashion/retail penetration and a common platform choice at the C&A/H&M tier — now evidenced by JD Sports, Frasers Group and John Lewis on their own site. Less common in US grocery. Search is bundled with the platform, so a commercetools client already pays for hybrid semantic search — a real objection to overcome.
- **Demo/trial:** Yes — free developer trial account and public API docs at https://commercetools.com/free-trial
- **Vs CAS:** OVERLAP: lexical + semantic + hybrid RRF is exactly CAS's retrieval architecture, shipped natively in the commerce platform. WHAT COMMERCETOOLS DOES NOT DO: no LLM query decomposition into multiple intents, no typed hard-constraint extraction, no LLM reranker with reasons, no recipe decomposition, no cross-sell grounding, no sponsored-ad relevance gating. Their semantic layer is embedding retrieval, full stop. POSITIONING: for a commercetools client, CAS is an intelligence layer consuming Storefront Search as the candidate generator — do NOT pitch replacement, their 'one platform, no separate search system' TCO argument beats rip-and-replace every time. BIGGEST STRATEGIC FLAG IN THIS SEGMENT: AI Hub is not a side note. It is a shipped, multi-protocol agentic-commerce layer that puts catalogs inside ChatGPT, Gemini and Copilot with full discovery-to-checkout, built against Google's UCP and OpenAI's ACP. CAS has no equivalent and no story for search traffic arriving from an external AI agent instead of a human at a search box.

#### SAP Commerce Cloud — Search Service + Intelligent Selling Services — https://learning.sap.com/courses/transforming-search-dynamics-using-search-service-and-intelligent-search-in-sap-commerce-cloud/understanding-intelligent-selling-services

- **What it does:** SAP's native commerce search: the default is Solr facet search; the modern path is SAP Commerce Cloud Search Service plus Intelligent Selling Services (ISS), layering ML-driven merchandising, personalised search results and AI product recommendations trained on continuously collected interaction data.
- **Key features:** Search Service (modern, replaces default Solr facet search) + Adaptive Search; Intelligent Selling Services: personalised search results, AI recommendations, automated merchandising; AI-generated search term suggestions; Continuous learning from customer interaction data; Merchandising rules and category-level control for business users
- **Named clients:** Galeries Lafayette — VERIFIED as an SAP Commerce Cloud user via the Grid Dynamics case study, which confirms the stack was SAP Commerce Cloud + Mirakl + Google Vertex AI Search for Commerce + Grid Dynamics' own Merchandising Experience Platform
- **Pricing:** Not published. IMPORTANT CORRECTION TO A LOAD-BEARING CLAIM: the original report asserted that customers 'must PURCHASE A SEPARATE SEARCH ADD-ON' and cited the SAP learning page for it. I fetched that page and it does NOT say that — it covers integration requirements and technical prerequisites (SAP Commerce Cloud 2211.38+, Identity Authentication Service migration) but says nothing about licensing or purchasing. DROP the paid-add-on claim; it is unsourced. WHAT IS VERIFIED VERBATIM on that same page, and is still a strong lock-in argument: 'The Personalized Search feature is exclusively available when you integrate SAP Commerce Cloud with Intelligent Selling Services and SAP Commerce Cloud Search Service' and 'This feature isn't available with other search service providers, including the default Solr facet search.' That is architectural lock-in, publicly documented by SAP — use that sentence, not the pricing claim.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** SAP Commerce Cloud is heavily used in European fashion/apparel and in B2B/wholesale. Galeries Lafayette runs SAP Commerce Cloud and still bought Google's AI Commerce Search on top of it — the single most useful proof point in this whole segment: SAP's native search was not sufficient for a 600K-SKU fashion/luxury catalog, and a consultancy-built layer on a third-party AI search engine went in alongside it.
- **Demo/trial:** No public self-serve trial or sandbox found. Demo via SAP sales or partner. Third-party AI search for SAP Commerce is offered by Zoovu (https://zoovu.com/ai-search-sap-commerce) — itself evidence that native search leaves a gap partners fill. NEW CONTEXT: Zoovu was named the ONLY Visionary in the 2026 Gartner MQ for Search and Product Discovery (https://zoovu.com/news/zoovu-named-visionary-2026-gartner-magic-quadrant), so the partner filling SAP's gap is a Gartner-recognised vendor, not a small integrator.
- **Vs CAS:** OVERLAP: personalised search results and AI recommendations inside the platform. WHAT SAP DOES NOT DO: no LLM query understanding, no multi-intent expansion, no generated per-result reasons, no hard-constraint handling, no conversational/recipe surface. KILLED CLAIM: the original report cited a partner blog for '10-20% search-to-cart improvement' and '5-15% AOV increase' (spadoom.com). That is an unaudited single-partner marketing claim with no methodology; I did not re-verify it and I recommend removing it entirely rather than qualifying it — an unaudited partner stat in a client deck is a liability. BEST ANGLE, FULLY SOURCED: the Galeries Lafayette precedent. An SAP-based fashion retailer that needed better long-tail and contextual relevance publicly layered third-party AI search plus a custom merchandising platform on top. That is precisely the LatentView engagement shape.

#### Adobe Commerce — Live Search (GAP FILL: missed entirely by the original report) — https://experienceleague.adobe.com/en/docs/commerce/live-search/overview

- **What it does:** Adobe's SaaS search service for Adobe Commerce (Magento), synchronising catalog data to Adobe's cloud infrastructure. This is a material omission from the original report: Adobe Commerce is a top-tier platform at exactly the fashion/apparel buyer segment LatentView targets, and Live Search is the search layer those retailers get by default — so it is a direct 'why do we need you?' objection, on the same footing as Shopify Search & Discovery and commercetools Storefront Search.
- **Key features:** 'Search as you type' popover with product suggestions and thumbnails; AI-powered DYNAMIC FACETING — facets adjust based on the query and shopper behaviour; AI-driven result re-ranking based on shopper behaviour (Intelligent Ranking); Fuzzy typo handling using Levenshtein-distance logic; Synonyms to expand query reach; Merchandising rules for promotional control; Prioritises exact and near matches, and same-field matches, to surface high-intent results first
- **Pricing:** VERIFIED: the Adobe docs state Live Search is 'included in your license' — it is not a separate paid add-on for Adobe Commerce customers. Adobe Commerce licence pricing itself is not published. Documented operational limits: popover partial matches are limited to 20 characters per word; test environments with no queries for 90+ days hibernate; empty catalogs hibernate after 45 days. No explicit catalog-size ceiling is documented (unlike Shopify's 200K limit).
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Strong fashion/apparel relevance — Adobe Commerce is a common mid-market-to-enterprise apparel platform. Weak US grocery relevance. The competitive shape mirrors Shopify: a capable, free-with-the-platform search layer that sets the buyer's baseline expectation at $0 incremental.
- **Demo/trial:** Documentation and developer guides are public at https://experienceleague.adobe.com/en/docs/commerce/live-search/overview; Live Search installs as a service for Adobe Commerce customers. No standalone public sandbox found.
- **Vs CAS:** OVERLAP: AI-driven re-ranking, dynamic faceting, synonyms, typo tolerance, merchandising rules — all included in the licence. WHAT ADOBE LIVE SEARCH DOES NOT DO (verified against the docs): the documentation describes traditional search enhancement and does NOT detail conversational or natural-language processing — there is no LLM query decomposition, no typed hard constraints, no generated per-result reason, no recipe decomposition, no sponsored-ad gating. WHAT IT HAS THAT CAS DOES NOT: AI dynamic faceting driven by shopper behaviour, and a merchandising-rules UI for business users. POSITIONING: identical to the commercetools/Shopify play — CAS is the intelligence layer consuming Live Search as candidate generator, not a replacement. Add this vendor to the deck: an Adobe Commerce fashion retailer WILL ask 'we already have Live Search, what does CAS add?' and the answer needs to be prepared, not improvised.

#### Salesforce Commerce Cloud — Einstein Search / Agentforce — https://kovil.ai/agentforce/playbook/agentforce-pricing-guide-2026

- **What it does:** Native commerce search plus Einstein AI (search dictionaries, predictive sort, product recommendations, personalisation) and Agentforce agents extending into commerce — conversational shopping, personalised promotions, site content management, grounded in Salesforce Data Cloud.
- **Key features:** Einstein Search with search dictionaries, predictive sort and Einstein Recommendations; Agentforce commerce agents for conversational shopping and personalised promotions; Data Cloud as the identity/behaviour substrate for personalisation; Deep native tie into Service/Marketing Cloud — cross-channel personalisation; Composable Storefront (PWA Kit)
- **Pricing:** HANDLE WITH CARE — SOURCING DOWNGRADED. Salesforce.com returned HTTP 403 to every fetch I attempted, including the pricing pages the original report cited as sources, so NONE of these figures are verified against a Salesforce-published price list. What a third-party 2026 pricing guide states (https://kovil.ai/agentforce/playbook/agentforce-pricing-guide-2026): Agentforce list price remains $2.00 per conversation, where a conversation is one complete session from first message to resolution or escalation regardless of turn count; negotiated enterprise rates fall to $0.80-$1.20 per conversation at 1,000+ conversations/day. CORRECTED TOTAL-COST EXAMPLE: the original report claimed '$200K-$400K first year for 50,000 conversations.' The actual worked example in that source is 5,000 conversations per MONTH (60,000/year) totalling ~$338,000 in year one — $120K conversations + $108K Data Cloud + $30K Einstein licences (~50 users) + $15K-$180K implementation — settling to ~$234K/year ongoing, with typical ROI break-even at 21-24 months. Commerce Cloud itself is typically priced as a percentage of GMV and is not published. IF THE CLIENT LEAD DEMANDS A LINK, this is a third-party aggregator, not Salesforce. Either get it from a Salesforce rep in writing or present it as 'reported list price' with the caveat visible on the slide.
- **Fashion/grocery fit:** Strong fashion/apparel presence (Commerce Cloud is a common H&M/C&A-tier platform); weaker in US grocery. No fashion- or grocery-specific search results published that I could verify.
- **Demo/trial:** No self-serve trial for Commerce Cloud search. Demo via Salesforce sales; Agentforce accessible through Trailhead Playground orgs.
- **Vs CAS:** OVERLAP: conversational discovery and personalised recommendations. WHAT SALESFORCE HAS THAT CAS DOES NOT: identity resolution and cross-channel personalisation via Data Cloud — genuinely hard to replicate and a real CAS gap. WHAT CAS HAS: intent decomposition, typed hard constraints, explained reranking, recipe decomposition, relevance-gated ads, plus dramatically cheaper unit economics. THE COMMERCIAL WEAPON, WITH ITS CAVEAT ATTACHED: $2.00 per conversation is still the sharpest number in the segment — a retailer running 1 million AI-assisted conversations a month is at $2M/month at list, against a Groq gpt-oss-120b cost per query orders of magnitude lower. But because I could not verify it against salesforce.com, present it as reported-list-price with the source shown, and pair it with the verified Azure ($4.32 for 2,000 agentic retrievals) and Google ($2.50/1K searches) numbers, which ARE first-party sourced and therefore safer to lead with. Also be honest about ~7s warm latency — Salesforce and Google both deliver sub-second.

**Segment takeaways:**

- FACT-CHECK SUMMARY, READ THIS FIRST. Of the original report's load-bearing claims I re-verified ~35 independently. VERIFIED EXACTLY (quote freely, links included): all Azure AI Search meters via the Retail Prices API; all Vespa Cloud unit prices and the $20,000/month Enterprise minimum; all Elastic Serverless prices; all Databricks Vector Search DBU rates and the $605/month worked example; every Databricks Vector Search technical constraint (200-result hybrid/full-text cap vs 10,000 vector, BM25 beta on storage-optimized only, ~250ms latency, 100KB rows, 4,096 dims, rrf_param=60); all AWS Personalize and OpenSearch instance prices; Shopify's 2.8/5 from 457 reviews and all three semantic-search limits; the Galeries Lafayette results; the Albertsons 'Ask AI' deployment; Gemo's 2.3x/160x ROI; Vendr's Algolia median $41,650 from 144 purchases; the Forrester TEI headline numbers; the Gartner MQ date, authors, vendor count and Leader list. CORRECTED: 8 claims. KILLED: 4 claims. Details in the takeaways below.
- CORRECTIONS TO CARRY FORWARD — do not ship the original text on these. (1) MARKET SIZE: the Verified Market Reports figure was WRONG. That page now states $3.8B in 2024 growing to $12.7B by 2032 at 12.5% CAGR — NOT the '$12.5B to $30.2B at 10.5%' in the original. Against Cognitive Market Research's $17.41B (2025) to $40.41B (2033) at 11.1%, the two sources now disagree by roughly 4.5x on the base year, not 40%. That is not a range, it is noise. Cite the disagreement itself as the finding, or cite nothing. (2) COVEO CONTRACT VALUES: Vendr's median is $43,621/year, with bands of $30K-$150K SMB/mid-market, $150K-$400K mid-to-enterprise, and $400K-$1M+ large enterprise — not the vague '$30K to $500K+'. (3) DATABRICKS: 'intent-based discovery' is from the TRAVEL section of the retail solutions page, not the retail section; the quotable retail line is 'conversational AI agents that guide product discovery'. (4) AZURE GA: only SOME agentic retrieval features are GA in the 2026-04-01 REST API — portal access remains preview and more features sit in 2026-05-01-preview. Say 'partially GA via REST'. (5) SAP: the 'must purchase a separate search add-on' claim is NOT on the cited SAP learning page — drop it; the verified lock-in quote is that Personalized Search 'isn't available with other search service providers, including the default Solr facet search.' (6) GOOGLE NAMING: 'AI Commerce Search' is the product name (formerly Agent Search for commerce); 'Gemini Enterprise for Customer Experience' is the console context, not a full rebrand. (7) AWS PERSONALIZE free tier is first-two-months only, not standing. (8) ELASTIC H-E-B is directly named on elastic.co, not a snippet — upgrade it to quotable.
- CLAIMS KILLED AS UNSOURCEABLE — remove them, an unverifiable stat in a client deck is worse than an omission. (a) COTOPAXI '+44.78% search conversion after replacing Fast Simon with Google Vertex' — no reachable source. (b) BAYMARD '70% of top-50 sites fail on product-type synonyms' and '34% fail on model number or a mistyped character' — I fetched BOTH cited Baymard pages and neither carries those figures. What Baymard DOES publish on those pages, and what you should use instead: '69% of sites' lack relevant autocomplete for closely misspelled terms, '56% of sites have issues' with search query type handling, '46% get it wrong' on auto-directing to matching category scopes, '54% don't' update thumbnails to match variation searches, and only '19% get everything right' on autocomplete despite 80% offering it — across a benchmark of 344 leading US and European e-commerce sites with 700+ search-specific usability issues observed. Those are equally powerful and actually sourced. The H&M maternity/pregnant example was also not on the pages I fetched. (c) GARTNER '20% of digital commerce transactions executed via AI platforms or agents by 2030' — gartner.com returned 403 and I could not substantiate it; do not use the number (see the next takeaway for a sourced replacement). (d) GOOGLE '>85% of Ask AI conversations start with open-ended questions' — not in any page I fetched; the sourced version is that Albertsons customers using Ask AI 'often add one or more additional items to their cart'.
- THE 'IS AGENTIC A GARTNER CRITERION?' QUESTION — THE ORIGINAL ANSWER WAS TOO CAUTIOUS AND IS NOW CORRECTED. Gartner's OWN market text for the 2026 MQ does use the word: 'Search and product discovery solutions provide class-leading innovations, such as natural language processing, intent detection, algorithmic merchandising, and AGENTIC INTEGRATIONS,' and Gartner-attributed framing that 'enterprise search and product discovery is evolving rapidly from traditional digital experiences toward AI-powered, AGENTIC, and outcome-oriented discovery across product, content, and knowledge journeys.' So agentic is Gartner language, not merely vendor marketing. Separately confirmed: the companion Critical Capabilities report scores 12 vendors across 9 Critical Capabilities, and 'GenAI Product Discovery' is a named Use Case alongside 'Product Search and Discovery'. ONE UNRESOLVED DETAIL: Netcore Unbxd's release says 5 Use Cases, Constructor's blog says 4 — check the document before stating a number. Safe formulation: 'Gartner's own 2026 market description names agentic integrations and intent detection as class-leading innovations, and GenAI Product Discovery is a formal Critical Capabilities use case.'
- THE GARTNER 2026 MQ SLIDE, NOW FULLY VERIFIED. Published 22 June 2026; authors Mike Lowndes, Sandy Shen, Aditya Vasudevan, Noam Dorros; 12 vendors evaluated. LEADERS — all five independently confirmed from separate vendor announcements: Algolia (third consecutive year), Bloomreach (third year running), Constructor (second straight year, and it placed furthest on Completeness of Vision AND highest on Ability to Execute), Coveo, and Netcore Unbxd (third consecutive time). NEW FACT THE ORIGINAL REPORT MISSED: Zoovu is the ONLY vendor in the Visionary quadrant this year — cited by Gartner for a proprietary ontology spanning 17,000+ categories and 50,000 attributes, an AI product finder handling compatible products/bundles/real-time pricing, and expansion into end-to-end search. GOOGLE'S PLACEMENT: Google was a LEADER in the 24 June 2025 edition of this MQ (per Google Cloud's own blog); I could NOT confirm its 2026 position. Do not assert Google is a 2026 Leader without opening the document. Market definition (Gartner, verified): 'applications that augment digital commerce solutions to facilitate navigation, filtering, comparisons and, ultimately, selection of products.'
- WHAT IS ACTUALLY STILL DEFENSIBLE — unchanged after verification, and now sharper because I confirmed the negatives. None of the twelve vendors ships all four of: (1) per-result human-readable GENERATED reason strings from the reranker — Azure returns a 0-4 rerankerScore plus a verbatim EXTRACTIVE caption, Google returns ranked products, Adobe/Shopify/commercetools return nothing of the kind; (2) TYPED HARD CONSTRAINTS with score-ceiling capping ('no chicken', 'under $20', 'for women') that are auditable and overridable per constraint — every vendor's NL understanding is a black box; (3) recipe/meal decomposition into a per-ingredient shopping list with brand alternatives — nobody ships this UX; (4) relevance-gated sponsored ads structurally separated from organic with the bid never exposed. Lead with these four. Do NOT lead with 'multi-intent semantic search' — Microsoft sells that as a metered primitive at $0.000022 per 1K reasoning tokens with a monthly free allowance, Elastic gives it away with a schema change via ELSER, and Shopify, Adobe and commercetools all bundle a semantic layer at $0 incremental.
- BUILD-VS-BUY, THE HONEST VERSION, WITH THE ONE PUBLIC PROOF POINT. Nobody credible recommends a pure build. The defensible consulting position is 'buy the engine, build the differentiated layer', and the best public evidence is Galeries Lafayette — VERIFIED in full: a luxury fashion retailer with 335 stores, 600,000+ SKUs (owned plus Mirakl marketplace) and 60M+ annual visitors, running SAP Commerce Cloud, bought Google's Vertex AI Search for Commerce AND had Grid Dynamics build a custom Merchandising Experience Platform with automated catalog/pricing/stock ingestion pipelines on top, because the merchandising team faced growing manual workload and limited experimentation capability. Result: 7% total revenue increase, 8% higher average basket value, 20% YoY online sales growth in peak periods, and 2X faster merchandising execution. That is the LatentView engagement shape with a citable public reference.
- BOTH TARGET VERTICALS ALREADY HAVE AN INCUMBENT WIRED IN — plan for co-existence, not displacement, and every logo below is now verified on the vendor's own site. GROCERY: Albertsons is Google Cloud's flagship Conversational Commerce customer, live as 'Ask AI' in the search bar of Albertsons, Safeway, Vons, Jewel-Osco, Shaw's, Acme and Tom Thumb; Kroger AND H-E-B are both named on elastic.co/industries/retail-ecommerce; Coop and 7-Eleven are named Databricks customers. FASHION: Burberry is a named Databricks customer; Decathlon, Otto, Vinted and Kleinanzeigen run Vespa; JD Sports, Frasers Group and John Lewis are named commercetools customers. The realistic deal at these accounts is an intelligence layer over the existing stack — which is exactly what CAS's keyword-first router plus BM25 fallback was built for. Make that architecture the LEAD of the pitch, not a footnote.
- THE BEST ROI EVIDENCE IS THE FORRESTER TEI OF ALGOLIA, AND IT VERIFIED CLEANLY (announced 3 March 2026): 213% ROI over three years, payback in under six months, $3.1M NPV, and ~$12M annual revenue increase, on a composite organisation with $600 million annual revenue and 607.5 million searches annually, built from interviews with four decision-makers. It is a COMMISSIONED vendor study — say so on the slide — but it is the only third-party-modelled ROI number in the category. The original report's supplementary figures (11% CTR increase, up to 35% merchandising time savings worth $1.8M over 3 years, 23% marketing and 12% sales time savings, 1.5 developer FTEs redeployed) come from the gated report itself and I could not verify them from the public announcement; either pull them from the actual PDF or leave them out. https://www.algolia.com/about/news/algolia-delivers-forrester-total-economic-impact-study
- NAMED-RETAILER UPLIFT NUMBERS — ALL VENDOR-PUBLISHED, LABEL THEM AS SUCH, AND MIND THE DATES. VERIFIED: Gemo, French affordable-fashion retailer with 400+ stores and 3,600+ employees, Algolia customer since 2022 — 2.3x conversion increase after activating Dynamic Re-Ranking, 160x ROI, and roughly one third of digital revenue flowing through Algolia search. VERIFIED: Galeries Lafayette on Google — +7% revenue, +8% basket, +20% YoY peak online sales. VERIFIED BUT STALE — the Algolia three-retailer blog is dated 10 April 2024, which is over two years old; check before using: Zenni +9% conversion, +44% search traffic, +34% search revenue, +27% revenue per user session; Oh Polly +5.4% conversion, +7.1% revenue, 30-40% increase in search bar usage, search sessions accounting for 20% of revenue; Everlane +5% conversion, -45% no-results rate, +8% CTR. CORRECTED: the original report's 'Oh Polly search is 7% of sessions' is not on that page — only the 20%-of-revenue figure is. KILLED: Cotopaxi (see above).
- TYPICAL CONTRACT VALUES — THE PRICE UMBRELLA CAS SELLS UNDER. VERIFIED FROM VENDR TRANSACTION DATA: Algolia median annual spend $41,650 across 144 purchases (buyers average 23% savings; observed range $12,413 to $260,594), banded at $10-40K for 50K-500K requests/month, $40-150K for 500K-5M requests/month, and $150-500K+ above 5M requests/month. Coveo median $43,621/year, banded $30-150K, $150-400K, and $400K-$1M+. VERIFIED FROM VENDOR PAGE: Vespa Cloud Enterprise carries a hard $20,000/MONTH minimum spend. THIRD-PARTY ONLY, DO NOT QUOTE AS FACT: commercetools Core from ~$50K/year with most enterprises at $40-300K+; Salesforce Agentforce at $2.00 per conversation list, $0.80-$1.20 negotiated at 1,000+/day, ~$338K year-one for a 5,000-conversation/month deployment. The Agentforce figures are the sharpest in the deck and the least verifiable — salesforce.com blocked every fetch — so pair them with the first-party-verified Google and Azure numbers rather than resting the ROI slide on them.
- CLOUD-PLATFORM PRICING IS RADICALLY CHEAPER THAN SaaS SEARCH, AND EVERY NUMBER HERE IS FIRST-PARTY VERIFIED. Google $2.50 per 1,000 searches (~$600K/year at 20M searches/month — arithmetic checked). Azure Semantic Ranker $1.00/1K queries (plus a $16.12/day unit meter and $2.00/1K overage the original report missed); agentic retrieval reasoning tokens $0.000022/1K at low effort, $0.0001/1K at medium; Microsoft's own example puts 2,000 full agentic retrievals at $4.32 all-in. AWS OpenSearch r6g.xlarge $0.335/hr — a 3-node cluster is ~$734/month. Databricks Vector Search 4.00 DBU/hr standard, $605/month for 5M vectors. Elastic Serverless: a PRODUCTION environment with 20GB searchable data is $190-210/month by Elastic's own worked example. Vespa self-hosted is free under Apache 2.0. INFERENCE: infrastructure for a 300K-SKU retailer runs low-to-mid three figures per month; the SaaS alternatives run five to six figures per year. The consulting margin lives in that gap — but only if the delivered layer is worth more than the vendor's merchandising console, A/B framework and analytics dashboard, all of which CAS currently lacks.
- MOST URGENT ENGINEERING CONSTRAINT FOR THE DATABRICKS MIGRATION — RE-VERIFIED LINE BY LINE IN THE DOCS AND ALL OF IT HOLDS. Mosaic AI Vector Search caps BOTH hybrid keyword-similarity search AND full-text search at 200 RESULTS per query, against 10,000 for pure ANN vector search. The BM25 full-text INDEX is BETA and available exclusively on storage-optimized endpoints (query_type='FULL_TEXT' can run on existing indexes on either endpoint type). Storage-optimized endpoints add 'a slightly increased latency of about 250msec'. Delta Sync rows cap at 100KB, the embedding source column at 32,764 bytes, and embedding dimension at 4,096 (gte-small at 384 is fine). Hybrid RRF uses a FIXED rrf_param of 60 — not tunable. ACTION: re-check CAS's scatter-gather across N intents plus RERANK_POOL_K against the 200-result hybrid ceiling BEFORE migration, and note that the ~250ms storage-optimized penalty stacks on top of the already-admitted ~7s warm latency. https://docs.databricks.com/aws/en/generative-ai/vector-search
- LATENCY IS THE MOST LIKELY DEAL-KILLER AND THE COVER STORY IS NOW SOURCED. Google, Azure, Shopify and Adobe all serve sub-second. CAS is ~7s warm on the AI path because gpt-oss-120b is a reasoning model. Microsoft's own documentation concedes, verbatim: 'Agentic retrieval adds latency compared to a single-query pipeline, but it handles query complexity that a single query can't.' That is a hyperscaler publicly accepting a latency penalty for multi-intent decomposition — quote it. It gives cover for a multi-second AI path IF it is positioned as an escalation tier behind a fast lexical path, which is precisely what CAS's keyword-first router already does. Pre-empt the latency question before the client raises it.
- AGENTIC COMMERCE IS THE BIGGEST STRATEGIC BLIND SPOT AND IT IS NOW EVIDENCED WITHOUT NEEDING THE UNVERIFIED GARTNER FORECAST. Drop the '20% of transactions by 2030' number (unsourceable) and use the shipped-product evidence instead: commercetools AI Hub is live as 'a universal orchestration layer that translates your brand's commerce logic across AI platforms such as ChatGPT, Gemini and Copilot', covering the full lifecycle from discovery through order completion, built multi-protocol against Google's UCP and OpenAI's ACP, and fronted by JD Sports, Frasers Group and John Lewis. Google's own AI Commerce Search sits under a Gemini Enterprise console. Gartner's own 2026 MQ market text names 'agentic integrations' as a class-leading innovation. CAS has no story for search traffic arriving from an external AI agent rather than a human at a search box, and no protocol support for UCP/ACP/MCP-mediated shopping. That is a bigger roadmap gap than personalization or visual search, and it is the one a Gartner-reading client asks about first.
- COVERAGE GAPS I FILLED, AND ONE I COULD NOT. FILLED: Adobe Commerce Live Search was missing entirely and is now a full vendor entry — Adobe Commerce is a top-tier fashion/apparel platform and Live Search is 'included in your license', so it is the same $0-incremental objection as Shopify and commercetools; the docs confirm it does AI dynamic faceting and behavioural re-ranking but explicitly do NOT describe conversational or natural-language capability. Also filled: commercetools' named clients (the original had an empty array), Zoovu's status as the sole 2026 MQ Visionary, Google's 2025 MQ Leader position, Walmart as a named Elastic customer, and the extra Azure Semantic Ranker meters. STILL UNCOVERED — flag as known gaps rather than inventing entries: VTEX Intelligent Search (three separate VTEX URLs returned 404; VTEX is a major LATAM/European fashion and grocery platform and belongs in a future pass), Oracle Commerce/Oracle Retail, BigCommerce, Sitecore Search, Lucidworks Fusion (a Leader in an earlier edition of this MQ), IBM watsonx Discovery, and the standalone vector-DB build layer (Pinecone, Weaviate, Qdrant) which is the true 'pure build' option in a build-vs-buy matrix. NOTE ON METHOD: my WebSearch budget was exhausted partway through, so later verification ran on direct page fetches only; salesforce.com, gartner.com and cloud.google.com/*/pricing all returned 403 or truncated content, which is why those three areas carry the weakest sourcing in this report and are labelled accordingly.
